### [1] [LaMET-Agent: An Agent Framework for Large-Momentum Effective Theory Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32225)
*Jinchen He,Xiangyu Jiang,Fei Yao,Dian-Jun Zhao*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: lamet-agent is an open-source LLM-based framework that automates and standardizes the multi-stage LaMET workflow for lattice QCD calculations of parton distributions, validated on pion/kaon PDFs and DAs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reduce expert-dependent, repetitive effort in implementing the mature but complex LaMET workflow for light-cone parton distribution calculations from lattice QCD.

Method: Development of an LLM-powered agent framework (lamet-agent) that orchestrates and executes standardized, reproducible, and inspectable stages: correlator analysis, renormalization, Fourier transformation, perturbative matching, and physical-limit extrapolations (continuum, physical pion mass, infinite-momentum).

Result: Successful end-to-end validation on four benchmark calculations—pion PDFs (gauge-invariant and Coulomb-gauge), and pion/kaon distribution amplitudes—with results consistent with published literature.

Conclusion: lamet-agent establishes a robust, open, and extensible infrastructure for automated LaMET analyses, enabling broader accessibility, reproducibility, and future expansion to TMDs, GTMDs, and gluonic observables.

Abstract: Large-momentum effective theory (LaMET) provides a first-principles framework for computing the $x$ dependence of light-cone parton distributions from lattice QCD. Over the past decade, theoretical and numerical advances have established a mature multi-stage workflow for systematic calculation of parton physics, although its implementation still requires expert judgment and substantial repeated effort. We present lamet-agent, an open-source large language model (LLM) agent framework that organizes this workflow into an executable, reproducible, and inspectable analysis pipeline. The present release supports collinear quark distributions and implements correlator analysis, renormalization, Fourier transformation, perturbative matching, continuum, physical pion mass and infinite-momentum extrapolations, and automated result review. We validate it on four end-to-end analyses: pion parton distribution functions in the gauge-invariant and Coulomb-gauge formulations, and pion and kaon distribution amplitudes, obtaining results consistent with the published calculations. Extensions to transverse-momentum-dependent distributions, generalized transverse-momentum-dependent distributions, and gluonic distribution functions are planned for subsequent releases.

</details>


### [2] [Measuring Mass Splittings in Baryon SU(2) Multiplets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34647)
*Constantia Alexandrou,Simone Bacchio,Antonio Evangelista,Christos Iona,Christian Schneider*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper applies the RM123 method to compute isospin-breaking corrections to baryon mass splittings in lattice QCD, focusing on spin-1/2 and spin-3/2 SU(2) multiplets using near-physical twisted-mass ensembles, at leading order in electromagnetic and quark-mass-difference effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Increasing precision of lattice QCD observables (reaching ~1%) necessitates inclusion of isospin-breaking effects—electromagnetic interactions and the up-down quark mass difference—to achieve physical accuracy.

Method: RM123 method applied to baryon masses; calculations performed on twisted-mass lattice QCD ensembles tuned near physical quark masses; only valence-quark contributions included; isospin-breaking treated at leading order in the small parameters (α_EM and (m_u − m_d)).

Result: Preliminary results for mass splittings within spin-1/2 (e.g., nucleon doublet) and spin-3/2 (e.g., Delta quartet) baryon SU(2) multiplets are reported.

Conclusion: The RM123 framework is viable for quantifying isospin-breaking mass splittings in baryons with controlled systematics at leading order; valence-only results provide a baseline for future inclusion of sea-quark and higher-order effects.

Abstract: The inclusion of isospin-breaking corrections arising from electromagnetic interactions and the up-down quark mass difference is becoming increasingly important in lattice QCD calculations, as the precision of observables is at or approaching the percent level. In this context, the RM123 method has been employed in recent years to take into account such isospin corrections. In this work, we study baryon mass splittings using the RM123 approach. Preliminary results are presented for the mass splittings within spin-1/2 and spin-3/2 baryon SU(2) multiplets, obtained using twisted-mass ensembles tuned close to physical quark masses. We consider only valence contributions, with the splittings computed at leading order in the isospin-breaking parameters.

</details>


### [3] [The Peskin-Takeuchi $S$ parameter and vector meson decay constants in $N_f=8$ QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35371)
*Yasumichi Aoki,Tatsumi Aoyama,Ed Bennett,Toshihide Maskawaa,Kohtaroh Miura,Hiroshi Ohki,Enrico Rinaldi,Akihiro Shibata,Koichi Yamawaki,Takeshi Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the Peskin-Takeuchi S parameter in eight-flavor SU(3) lattice gauge theory—a walking technicolor candidate—using two complementary methods (first time moment of current correlators and vacuum polarization), with careful finite-volume corrections. It finds S ≈ 0.281(5)(17) at the lightest fermion mass, comparable to scaled-up QCD; confirms consistency of chiral and spectral sum rules; measures $F_ρ/F_π$ stable and KSRF-compatible; predicts techni-ρ width $Γ_ρ/M_ρ \lesssim 0.24/N$; and discusses implications for BSM physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess viability of eight-flavor QCD as a walking technicolor model by precisely computing the electroweak S parameter—a key constraint on new strong dynamics—and related low-energy constants ($L_{10}^r$), meson decay constants, and spectral sum rules, while controlling systematic errors (especially finite-volume effects).

Method: Lattice gauge theory simulations in SU(3) with eight light fundamental fermions; novel computation of S via first time moment of vector–axial vector current correlators, cross-validated with conventional vacuum-polarization method; finite-volume corrections estimated from multiple lattice volumes and chiral perturbation theory–motivated formula; extraction of $F_ρ$, $F_{a_1}$, $F_π$, $L_{10}^r(M_ρ)$, and spectral integrals satisfying Weinberg and Das-Mathur-Okubo sum rules.

Result: S = 0.281(5)(17) (one-doublet) at smallest fermion mass—smaller than scaled 2+1-flavor QCD (~0.35) but not monotonically decreasing toward chiral limit once FV corrections applied; $L_{10}^r(M_ρ)$ matches real-world QCD; $F_ρ/F_π$ stable and consistent with KSRF relation; predicted techni-ρ width $Γ_ρ/M_ρ \lesssim 0.24/N$; Weinberg and DMO sum rules satisfied, corroborating S and $L_{10}^r$.

Conclusion: Eight-flavor QCD exhibits QCD-like low-energy dynamics (chiral symmetry breaking pattern, vector meson dominance, sum rule compliance), but its S parameter—while moderately reduced—is not sufficiently suppressed to easily satisfy electroweak precision constraints without additional model-building ingredients; results provide quantitative input for viable walking technicolor or composite Higgs scenarios.

Abstract: We use lattice gauge theory to investigate the Peskin-Takeuchi S parameter in SU(3) gauge theory with eight light fundamental fermions (eight-flavor QCD), a candidate for walking technicolor. We develop a new approach to computing S using the first time moment of current correlators, in addition to the conventional vacuum-polarization method, and obtain consistent results. Finite-volume (FV) corrections are estimated using data at multiple lattice volumes and a formula motivated by chiral perturbation theory. At the smallest fermion mass, the one-doublet contribution is S = 0.281(5)(17), smaller than the value $\sim 0.35$ in scaled-up 2+1-flavor QCD (without subtracting the SM contribution). However, contrary to naive expectations, S toward smaller fermion masses is not necessarily suppressed once FV corrections are included. The resulting Gasser-Leutwyler parameter $L_{10}^r(M_ρ)$ is similar to that in real-world QCD. We also determine the vector and axial-vector meson decay constants, $F_ρ$ and $F_{a_1}$. The ratio $F_ρ/F_π$ is stable against the fermion mass and consistent with the KSRF estimate, implying a $ρππ$ coupling similar to that in real-world QCD. We predict the techni-rho decay width into weak bosons to be $Γ_ρ/M_ρ\lesssim 0.24/N$, where N is the number of weak doublets. We further find that the rho- and $a_1$-dominated Weinberg spectral-function and Das-Mathur-Okubo sum rules are satisfied, yielding S and $L_{10}^r(M_ρ)$ consistent with our main results. We discuss implications for BSM model building.

</details>


### [4] [Multilevel Plaquette-Space Sampling for Lattice Gauge Theories with Local Constraint Solves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35722)
*Ankur Singha,Jacob Finkenrath,Karl Jansen,Vipul Arora,Shinichi Nakajima*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces a multilevel normalizing-flow sampler for lattice gauge theories that operates directly in plaquette space—respecting exact Bianchi constraints—achieving superior sampling efficiency over link-space methods, especially in the weak-coupling regime relevant to continuum limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional Monte Carlo sampling of lattice gauge theories suffers from critical slowing down and topological freezing; generative models in link space face challenges due to increasingly long-ranged correlations at weak coupling, while plaquette space is physically natural (local action) but constrained by Bianchi identities, preventing straightforward generative modeling.

Method: A multilevel normalizing flow that factorizes the sampling process coarse-to-fine, transforming the global Bianchi constraint into a sequence of local constraint solves—each determined plaquette depends on at most four newly generated variables, keeping constraint problem size lattice-size independent.

Result: The Plaquette-Space Sampler (PSS) achieves substantial performance gains over link-space baselines in U(1) 2D/4D and SU(2) 2D benchmarks, with increasing advantage toward weak coupling—a regime where generative sampling is hardest and physically most relevant for asymptotically free theories' continuum extrapolation.

Conclusion: Exact constraint satisfaction in plaquette space via multilevel factorization enables scalable, efficient, and physically faithful generative sampling of lattice gauge theories, overcoming key bottlenecks of both Monte Carlo and prior generative approaches.

Abstract: Lattice gauge theories are an important class of physical models with high-dimensional structured distributions that underpin first-principles calculations in particle, nuclear, and condensed-matter physics. Recent advances in generative modeling have opened new avenues for sampling Boltzmann distributions in lattice gauge theories, offering the potential to alleviate limitations of traditional Monte Carlo methods, including critical slowing down and topological freezing. However, generative samplers for lattice gauge theories are typically constructed in link space, where correlations become increasingly long-ranged toward weak coupling, posing a challenge for learning. Plaquettes provide a more natural representation, as the action is local in these variables. However, exact Bianchi constraints restrict them to a lower-dimensional manifold, complicating direct generative modeling. We introduce a multilevel normalizing-flow construction that samples directly in plaquette space while satisfying these constraints exactly. The key idea is a coarse-to fine factorization that transforms a globally coupled constraint problem into a sequence of local solves: at each refinement, every determined plaquette depends on at most four newly generated variables, while the size of the constraint problem remains independent of the lattice size. We validate the construction for $U(1)$ in two and four dimensions and for $SU(2)$ in two dimensions. Our multilevel Plaquette-Space Sampler (PSS) substantially outperforms link-space baselines, with the advantage increasing toward weak coupling, where the gauge coupling becomes small. This regime is particularly challenging for generative sampling and, in asymptotically free gauge theories, is relevant to continuum studies.

</details>

### [5] [Model-independent searches and anomaly detection at the CMS experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31739)
*Tamas Almos Vami,for the CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents CMS's first model-agnostic anomaly detection search for new physics using five unsupervised ML methods on dijet substructure, validated on data (e.g., top quark recovery), deployed in real time via FPGA-based L1 triggers (AXOL1TL and CICADA), and extended to event-level observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The lack of clear BSM signals at the LHC motivates hypothesis-free search strategies that do not rely on pre-specified signal models.

Method: Five complementary unsupervised machine learning anomaly detection methods applied to jet substructure in dijet events; validation via label-free top quark recovery; real-time deployment of AXOL1TL and CICADA on CMS Level-1 trigger FPGAs.

Result: First CMS model-agnostic dijet resonance search using 138 fb⁻¹ at √s = 13 TeV; successful data-driven validation; >4 billion events selected in 2024 by FPGA-deployed algorithms; extension to event-level anomaly detection outlined.

Conclusion: Unsupervised ML-based anomaly detection is a viable, scalable, and operational strategy for model-agnostic BSM searches at the LHC, now integrated into real-time triggering and poised for broader application.

Abstract: The absence of a clear signal of physics beyond the standard model at the CERN LHC motivates search strategies that do not presuppose a specific signal hypothesis. This proceedings present machine-learning-based anomaly detection to search for new physics in a model-agnostic way. The first such search by CMS looks for dijet resonances whose jets have substructure atypical of jets initiated by light quarks or gluons, using five complementary anomaly detection methods applied to 138 fb$^{-1}$ of proton-proton collision data at $\sqrt{s} = 13$ TeV. The technique has been validated directly on data by recovering the top quark without the use of labels. The program has also moved into real time: two unsupervised algorithms, AXOL1TL and CICADA, now run on field-programmable gate arrays inside the CMS level-1 trigger and selected more than four billion collision events during 2024 data taking. Finally, the extension of resonant anomaly detection to event-level observables is outlined.

</details>


### [6] [Recent CLFV searches at Belle and Belle II](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32531)
*Vindhyawasini Prasad*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent experimental results on charged lepton flavor violation (CLFV) decays—particularly of τ leptons, B mesons, and bottomonium—from the Belle and Belle II experiments, motivated by the fact that CLFV is highly suppressed in the Standard Model but enhanced in many new physics models accessible at current sensitivities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neutrino oscillations imply lepton flavor is not conserved; while minimal SM extensions (e.g., with right-handed neutrinos) generate CLFV, predicted rates (e.g., BR(μ→eγ) ~ 10⁻⁵⁴) are unobservably small. Hence, observation of CLFV would be a clear signature of beyond-SM physics, and current experiments like Belle/Belle II offer competitive sensitivity.

Method: Analysis of large asymmetric e⁺e⁻ collision datasets collected at the Υ(4S) resonance by Belle and Belle II, searching for CLFV decays of τ leptons, B mesons, and bottomonium states using high-precision reconstruction and background suppression techniques.

Result: Presents latest upper limits or null results (no significant signal observed) on various CLFV decay channels—including τ → ℓγ, τ → ℓℓ′ℓ″, B → Kℓℓ′, and ϒ → ℓℓ′—with improved sensitivities over previous measurements.

Conclusion: No evidence for CLFV has been found so far in Belle or Belle II data; however, the achieved sensitivities constrain parameter space of numerous new physics models (e.g., SUSY, leptoquarks, Z′ models), and ongoing data collection will further probe unexplored regions.

Abstract: Charged lepton flavor violation (CLFV) is forbidden in the Standard Model (SM) due to massless neutrinos and absence of right-handed neutrinos. The discovery of neutrino oscillations established that the neutrinos are massive and the lepton flavor is not an exact symmetry of nature. A minimal extension of the SM that incorporates right-handed neutrinos gives rise to CLFV through loop-level diagrams involving neutrinos. However, such processes are extremely rare, for instance, the branching fraction of $μ\to eγ$ is expected to be at the level of $10^{-54}$, far beyond the sensitivity of current collider experiments. In contrast, several new physics models predict CLFV decay rates within the sensitivity of current experiments, such as the Belle and Belle II experiments, which have collected the world's largest asymmetric $e^+e^-$ collision data near the $Υ(4S)$ resonance. The large data sample has utilized to explore the possibility of CLFV decays of $τ$ leptons, $B$ mesons, and bottomonium states. The report reviews some latest results on these CLFV decays at Belle and Belle II.

</details>

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports an improved search for radiative transitions ψ(3770) → γη_c(1S,2S) using 20.3 fb⁻¹ of e⁺e⁻ data at 3.773 GeV collected by BESIII; no significant signal is observed, and tighter 90% CL upper limits are set on product branching fractions and partial decay widths—improving prior limits by factors of ~3 and ~2, respectively.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve sensitivity to the suppressed radiative transitions ψ(3770) → γη_c(1S) and ψ(3770) → γη_c(2S), which probe QCD multipole expansion, charmonium dynamics, and possible non-qq̄ components; previous measurements suffered from limited statistics.

Method: Analyzed 20.3 fb⁻¹ of e⁺e⁻ collision data at √s = 3.773 GeV recorded with the BESIII detector; reconstructed η_c(1S,2S) via hadronic decays η_c → K⁰_S K±π∓; employed background suppression via kinematic fits, sideband subtraction, and efficiency-corrected event counting; set 90% CL upper limits using a Bayesian approach with Gaussian priors and systematic uncertainty incorporation.

Result: No significant signal observed for either transition; 90% CL upper limits: ℬ(ψ(3770) → γη_c(1S)) × ℬ(η_c(1S) → K⁰_S K±π∓) < 5.0 × 10⁻⁶ and ℬ(ψ(3770) → γη_c(2S)) × ℬ(η_c(2S) → K⁰_S K±π∓) < 3.7 × 10⁻⁶; corresponding partial width limits: Γ(ψ(3770) → γη_c(1S)) < 5.5 keV and Γ(ψ(3770) → γη_c(2S)) < 29.4 keV.

Conclusion: The results represent the most stringent constraints to date on ψ(3770) radiative decays to η_c(1S) and η_c(2S), significantly improving prior limits due to larger integrated luminosity and refined analysis techniques; they constrain theoretical models of charmonium radiative transitions and provide input for understanding ψ(3770) internal structure and E1/M1 mixing.

Abstract: Based on an integrated luminosity of $20.3~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ of $e^{+}e^{-}$ annihilation data collected at a center-of-mass energy of $3.773~\rm{GeV}$ with the BESIII detector operating at the BEPCII collider, an improved search for the radiative transitions $ψ(3770) \to γη_{c}(1S, 2S)$ is performed using the hadronic decays $η_{c}(1S, 2S) \to K^{0}_{S} K^{\pm} π^{\mp}$. No significant signal is observed. The corresponding 90$\%$ confidence level upper limits on the product branching fractions are set to be $5.0 \times 10^{-6}$ for the $η_{c}(1S)$ transition and $3.7 \times 10^{-6}$ for the $η_{c}(2S)$ transition. The 90$\%$ confidence level upper limits on the partial decay widths are also reported to be $Γ(ψ(3770) \to γη_{c}(1S)) < 5.5$ keV and $Γ(ψ(3770) \to γη_{c}(2S)) < 29.4~\rm{keV}$. With about seven times larger integrated luminosity than used previously, these results lower the upper limits by approximately a factor of three and two for the $η_{c}(1S)$ and $η_{c}(2S)$ transitions, respectively.

</details>

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: GBAR experiment measured antihydrogen formation cross sections via antiproton–positronium charge exchange at 4.15 and 6.2 keV, yielding first experimental values consistent with three-body theory—critical for antimatter gravity tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide the first experimental charge-exchange cross section data for antihydrogen formation from antiproton–positronium collisions, essential for modeling and optimizing antimatter experiments—particularly GBAR’s upcoming measurement of gravitational acceleration on antihydrogen.

Method: Antiprotons from CERN’s AD-ELENA facility (4.15 and 6.2 keV kinetic energy) were directed onto a positronium cloud; antihydrogen formation rate was measured to extract charge-exchange cross sections, with statistical and systematic uncertainties quantified.

Result: Cross sections: (14.1 ± 1.3_stat +2.2/−1.4_sys) × 10⁻¹⁶ cm² at 6.2 keV and (8.7 ± 2.4_stat +1.5/−0.09_sys) × 10⁻¹⁶ cm² at 4.15 keV; both agree with state-of-the-art three-body theoretical predictions.

Conclusion: This work establishes the first benchmark experimental cross sections for antihydrogen formation via antiproton–Ps charge exchange, validating theoretical models and enabling accurate simulation and design of next-generation antimatter gravity experiments.

Abstract: The GBAR experiment has measured the formation rate of antihydrogen from antiproton impact on a positronium cloud for antiproton kinetic energies of 4 and 6 keV. This is the first charge-exchange cross section measurement performed using antiproton beams, which are provided by the AD-ELENA facility at CERN. The measured cross section values are $(14.1 \pm 1.3 \mathrm{(stat)} ^{+2.2}_{-1.4} \mathrm{(sys)}) \times 10^{-16}$~cm$^2$ at 6.2 keV energy and $(8.7 \pm 2.4 \mathrm{(stat)} ^{+1.5}_{-0.09} \mathrm{(sys)}) \times 10^{-16}$~cm$^2$ at 4.15 keV energy, and agree with recent theoretical three-body calculations for antihydrogen formation. These measurements are an important input for experiments with antimatter, e.g the planned measurements of antihydrogen gravitational acceleration in the GBAR collaboration.

</details>


### [9] [Using Graph Neural Networks for the segmentation of overlapping objects in high granularity calorimeters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34500)
*Matthieu Melennec,Frédéric Magniette*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces an optimized Graph Neural Network (GNN) segmentation block for high-granularity calorimeters at the HL-LHC, enabling efficient disentanglement of overlapping two-photon showers via node-level energy fraction prediction, with accelerated graph operations and reduced algorithmic complexity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-granularity calorimeters at the High-Luminosity LHC face challenges in resolving overlapping particle showers; novel algorithms are needed to maintain reconstruction performance under increased pile-up and data rates.

Method: An optimized Graph Neural Network (GNN) segmentation block is designed to predict node-level energy fractions for separating overlapping two-photon showers; graph construction and convolution operations are accelerated to reduce computational overhead.

Result: The proposed GNN pipeline achieves competitive shower separation efficiency while significantly lowering algorithmic complexity compared to prior approaches.

Conclusion: The optimized GNN-based segmentation block is a computationally efficient and effective solution for photon shower disentanglement in future high-granularity calorimetry systems.

Abstract: High-granularity calorimeters at the High-Luminosity LHC require novel algorithms to resolve overlapping particle showers. We present an optimised Graph Neural Network (GNN) segmentation block that predicts node-level energy fractions to disentangle overlapping two-photon showers. By accelerating graph construction and convolution operations, our pipeline achieves competitive separation efficiency with significantly reduced algorithmic complexity.

</details>


### [10] [A study of CMS analysis pipelines through the Integration Challenge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34698)
*Mohamed Aly,Peter Elmer,Peter Fackeldey,Oksana Shadura*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents CMS analysis pipelines developed for the HL-LHC era, centered on the IRIS-HEP Integration Challenge—an end-to-end test of interoperability, usability, and readiness of modern HEP software stacks—including columnar processing, ML, statistical inference, visualization, skimmed data delivery (e.g., ServiceX), and prototype services like histogram-as-a-service.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The HL-LHC will produce unprecedented data volumes, rendering traditional HEP analysis workflows inadequate; thus, robust, scalable, and interoperable software infrastructure is urgently needed.

Method: Development and evaluation of CMS analysis pipelines leveraging the scientific Python ecosystem and HEP-specific tools, via the IRIS-HEP Integration Challenge—an end-to-end integration test implementing full physics analyses to assess tool interoperability, user experience, data delivery (e.g., ServiceX, diverse formats), and prototype services (e.g., histogram-as-a-service).

Result: A functional, modular analysis pipeline integrating columnar data processing, machine learning, statistical inference, and visualization across multiple CMS analysis scenarios; empirical validation of ServiceX and alternative skimmed-data delivery strategies; identification of interoperability gaps and usability bottlenecks; and prototyping of emerging analysis services.

Conclusion: The Integration Challenge demonstrates tangible progress toward production-ready, composable, and user-centric analysis infrastructure for HL-LHC, highlighting both current capabilities and critical areas requiring further development—particularly in cross-tool standardization, scalability, and service integration.

Abstract: The upcoming High-Luminosity Large Hadron Collider (HL-LHC) at CERN will deliver an unprecedented volume of data for High Energy Physics (HEP). This wealth of information offers significant opportunities for scientific discovery, but its scale challenges traditional analysis workflows. In this paper, we present CMS analysis pipelines being developed to meet HL-LHC demands. These pipelines build on the broader scientific Python ecosystem, complemented by solutions specifically designed for HEP. A central focus of this work is the Integration Challenge, an IRIS-HEP led effort aimed at assessing the readiness of developed software stack to be used in real world physics analysis and improving the readiness of analysis facilities for the HL-LHC era. The Integration Challenge acts as an end-to-end integration test: by implementing a complete physics analysis pipeline, it evaluates tool interoperability and the overall user experience for analysts. The current pipeline includes columnar data processing, machine learning, statistical inference, and visualization tasks covering a variety of CMS analysis scenarios. In addition, the Integration Challenge explores efficient strategies for delivering skimmed data using diverse tools and data formats, as well as evaluating the ServiceX data-delivery system for HEP analyses. Throughout the testing phase, we also investigated several prototype services, such as histogram-as-a-service capabilities, along with other emerging services that may support future HL-LHC analysis workflows.

</details>


### [11] [Muon Detection and Direction Reconstruction with the Upgraded 1-ton Water Cherenkov Prototype Detector at CJPL-I](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35135)
*Yapeng Wang,Yuzi Yang,Shaomin Chen,Wei Dou,Haoyang Fu,Guanghua Gong,Lei Guo,Ziyi Guo,XiangPan Ji,Jianmin Li,Jinjing Li,Bo Liang,Ye Liang,Ling Liu,Qian Liu,Zhiyi Liu,Wentai Luo,Ming Qi,Wenhui Shao,Haozhe Sun,Jian Tang,Yuyi Wang,Zhe Wang,Changxu Wei,Jun Weng,Yiyang Wu,Benda Xu,Chuang Xu,Tong Xu,Tao Xue,Haoyan Yang,Aiqiang Zhang,Bin Zhang,Xinshun Zhang,Yang Zhang,Zhicai Zhang,Lin Zhao,Yangheng Zheng*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: JNE-1ton, a 1-ton prototype neutrino detector at CJPL-I, underwent hardware upgrades—including replacement of 30 Hamamatsu PMTs with 60 North Night Vision 8-inch MCP-PMTs and mechanical redesign—and achieved improved muon detection efficiency (59% higher than pre-upgrade LS mode), reduced directional uncertainty (to 6°, i.e., 30% of prior), and measured consistent muon flux; it also identified its first up-going muon event, enabling future neutrino-induced event studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance muon detection capability and direction reconstruction precision in the Jinping Neutrino Experiment’s prototype detector, leveraging CJPL-I’s ultra-low-background environment (2400-m rock overburden) and enabling rare-event physics—particularly neutrino-induced up-going muons—while demonstrating cost-effective water-based detection.

Method: Hardware upgrade of JNE-1ton: redesigned mechanical support and PMT layout; replaced 30 Hamamatsu PMTs with 60 8-inch MCP-PMTs from North Night Vision; operated consecutively in water mode for two runs (Water-I: 85 days; Water-II: 79 days); analyzed Cherenkov light patterns for muon detection efficiency, flux, and angular reconstruction; applied statistical and systematic uncertainty quantification.

Result: Muon detection efficiency in Water-II increased by ~59% vs. pre-upgrade liquid scintillator mode; measured total muon flux φ_I+II = (3.55 ± 0.43_stat ± 0.28_syst) × 10⁻¹⁰ cm⁻² s⁻¹ (consistent with prior); muon direction reconstruction uncertainty reduced to ~6° (30% of previous value); one unambiguous up-going muon event identified and excluded from cosmic-ray sample.

Conclusion: The upgraded JNE-1ton water detector demonstrates superior performance in muon detection and angular resolution, validates water as a cost-effective medium for underground muon flux measurement at CJPL, and pioneers neutrino-induced event studies via the first observed up-going muon—the first such result from China’s deepest underground lab.

Abstract: The 1-ton prototype detector for Jinping neutrino experiment (JNE-1ton) has completed its hardware upgrade and has been successfully operated in both water and liquid-scintillator modes at CJPL-I, which is situated under a 2400-m rock overburden. During the upgrade, the detector's mechanical support structure and PMT layout were redesigned. The original Hamamatsu PMTs were replaced with 8-inch MCP-PMTs from North Night Vision, increasing the PMT count from 30 to 60. The detector was then operated in water mode for 85 days (Water-I) and 79 days (Water-II). From the accumulated water-mode data, we derive a muon detection efficiency (Water-II) that is approximately 59% higher than that of the pre-upgrade liquid scintillator detector. The measured muon flux is $φ_{\text{I+II}} = (3.55 \pm 0.43_{\mathrm{stat}}\pm 0.28_{\mathrm{syst}}) \times 10^{-10}~\mathrm{cm}^{-2}\mathrm{s}^{-1}$, which is consistent with the previous measurement. Owing to the characteristic angular dependence of Cherenkov radiation and the increased PMT coverage, the uncertainty in muon direction reconstruction is approximately 6$^\circ$, which corresponds to a reduction to 30% of its previous value. Notably, one rare up-going muon event was clearly identified. It is excluded from the cosmic-ray muon flux sample and opens a new window for neutrino-induced event studies in the deepest underground laboratory in China. This work marks the first time that CJPL has employed a cost-effective water detector for muon flux measurement.

</details>

### [12] [Observationanal and Thermodynamic Analysis of Power Law form of $f (Q)$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31776)
*Agnidipto Bhattacharya,Shinjini Ghosh,Atreyee Biswas,Sujay Kr. Biswas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates a power-law f(Q) gravity model (f(Q) = Q + mQ^n) using statistical fitting to cosmological data (Hubble, Pantheon, SH0ES), compares it with ΛCDM, and analyzes its thermodynamic consistency—particularly via Eckart theory and generalized second law—while constraining parameters (m, n) and studying cosmographic evolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the viability of a specific power-law f(Q) gravity model against observational data and thermodynamic principles, addressing both cosmological fit and physical consistency beyond standard ΛCDM.

Method: Statistical analysis using 31-point Hubble, Pantheon SN Ia, and 1721-point SH0ES datasets for parameter estimation; thermodynamic analysis via Eckart’s non-equilibrium formalism; horizon entropy derived from unified first law; GSL and thermodynamic equilibrium constraints applied to bound m and n; cosmographic parameter evolution studied within statistically and thermodynamically allowed parameter ranges.

Result: Best-fit values for m and n obtained; model shows competitive fit with ΛCDM; thermodynamic analysis confirms validity of GSL and equilibrium only for constrained ranges of m and n; cosmographic behavior (e.g., deceleration–acceleration transition) is consistent within those ranges.

Conclusion: The power-law f(Q) = Q + mQ^n model is observationally viable and thermodynamically consistent only for specific parameter ranges, offering a physically motivated alternative to ΛCDM that satisfies both statistical and irreversible thermodynamic criteria.

Abstract: In this study, the power law form of f(Q) gravity $(f(Q)=Q+mQ^n)$ has been studied in view of statistical analysis with current observed data and irreversible thermodynamics. The best fit values of model specific parameters are found with the use of of 31point Hubble data, Patntheon type Supernva and 1721 point SH0ES data and a comparative study has been performed between $f(Q)$ and standard $Λ$CDM model. The thermodynamic consequence using standard Eckart theory of non-equilibrium thermodynamics has been examined independently for general form of $f(Q)$ gravity followed by power law form of $f(Q)$ where the horizon entropy is calculated using unified law of thermodynamics. Suitable range of power law model specific parameters has been explored through investigating validity of generalized second law of thermodynamics and thermodynamic equilibrium. Finally evolution nature of cosmographic parameters are studied within the specific range of $m,n$ which is compatible with the results obtained from both statistical and thermodynamical analysis.

</details>


### [13] [Chaos bound for spinning particles in a hairy black hole with two photon spheres](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31791)
*Chuanhong Gao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies orbital chaos bounds for spinning test particles in a hairy black hole spacetime with two photon spheres, finding that the inner unstable circular orbit branch always satisfies the chaos bound while the outer branch can violate it—unlike prior near-horizon-focused studies—and that particle angular momentum and background deformation strongly affect bound validity, whereas spin has only a secondary shifting effect.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how the classical orbital chaos bound depends on orbital structure (inner vs. outer unstable circular orbits) in a hairy black hole spacetime with double photon sphere geometry, and to assess the roles of particle spin, total angular momentum, and background deformation.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of geodesic deviation and Lyapunov exponents for spinning test particles moving in a hairy black hole spacetime featuring two distinct branches of unstable circular orbits; evaluation of the chaos bound via comparison of orbital instability (Lyapunov exponent) with black hole surface gravity.

Result: The inner branch of unstable circular orbits always satisfies the chaos bound; violations occur only on the outer branch. Total angular momentum enlarges the bound-violating region on the outer branch. The background deformation parameter modulates both the Lyapunov exponent and surface gravity, thereby regulating bound validity. Particle spin induces only minor shifts in orbit locations and critical boundaries via spin-curvature coupling.

Conclusion: The orbital chaos bound exhibits nontrivial dependence on the specific branch of unstable circular orbits—not just proximity to the horizon—highlighting the importance of global spacetime structure (e.g., double photon sphere) and dynamical parameters (angular momentum, deformation) over spin alone in determining chaos-bound compliance.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the unstable circular orbits of spinning test particles and the associated classical orbital chaos bound in a hairy black hole spacetime admitting a double photon sphere structure. This background supports two distinct branches of unstable circular orbits, referred to as the inner and outer branches. We find that, within the parameter ranges considered, the inner branch always satisfies the chaos bound, whereas violations can occur on the outer branch. This behavior contrasts with many previous studies focusing primarily on near-horizon violations of the chaos bound and reveals a clear dependence of the bound on the orbital branch. Our analysis further shows that the total angular momentum of the particle has a pronounced effect on the local orbital instability and can enlarge the bound-violating region on the outer branch. Meanwhile, the deformation parameter of the background geometry regulates the validity of the bound by modifying both the orbital Lyapunov exponent and the black hole surface gravity. By contrast, the effect of the particle spin is comparatively weak and mainly shifts the existing unstable circular orbits and the corresponding critical boundaries through the spin-curvature coupling.

</details>


### [14] [Cosmological Dynamics and Observational Constraints of a Logarithmic Herglotz $f(R,T)$ Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31800)
*Vishal M C,Sankarsan Tarai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a logarithmic Herglotz-type f(R,T) gravity model, f(R,T)=R+βln(−T/T₀), and tests its cosmological viability against observational data (CC, DESI DR2 BAO, Union3 SNe Ia); it yields a consistent late-time expansion history with w_eff,0 ≈ −0.702 and a smooth deceleration–acceleration transition, distinguishing it from ΛCDM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore alternatives to ΛCDM by constructing a viable modified gravity model that incorporates non-minimal matter–geometry coupling via a logarithmic Herglotz-type f(R,T) functional form, aiming to explain cosmic acceleration without dark energy.

Method: Formulated modified field equations in a flat FLRW background with pressureless matter; recast them as a coupled dynamical system involving the Herglotz field and normalized matter density; adopted a phenomenological closure for the effective Herglotz sector; solved numerically; constrained parameters using Cosmic Chronometers, DESI DR2 BAO, and Union3 SNe Ia within a Bayesian MCMC framework.

Result: The combined dataset analysis yields statistically consistent constraints; reconstructed expansion history gives present-day effective equation of state w_eff,0 ≈ −0.702; shows expected transition from decelerated (high-z) to accelerated (low-z) expansion; Om(z) and statefinder diagnostics confirm deviation from ΛCDM while remaining observationally viable.

Conclusion: The logarithmic Herglotz-type f(R,T) model provides a phenomenologically consistent description of the late-time homogeneous and isotropic cosmic expansion, offering a viable, non-ΛCDM alternative grounded in modified gravity with matter–geometry coupling.

Abstract: We investigate the cosmological dynamics and observational viability of a logarithmic Herglotz-type $f(R,T)$ gravity model described by $f(R,T)=R+β\ln(-T/T_0)$. Assuming a spatially flat Friedmann--Lemaître--Robertson--Walker background and pressureless matter, the modified field equations are formulated as a coupled dynamical system involving the Herglotz field and the normalized matter density. A phenomenological closure relation is adopted for the effective Herglotz sector, allowing the background evolution to be determined numerically. The model is constrained using Cosmic Chronometer measurements, the DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillation compilation, and the Union3 supernova sample, considering both individual and combined datasets within a Bayesian Markov-chain Monte Carlo framework. The combined analysis provides a statistically consistent description of the background expansion. The reconstructed expansion history yields a present-day effective equation of state $w_{\rm eff,0}\simeq-0.702$ and exhibits the expected transition from accelerated expansion at late times toward decelerated expansion at higher redshifts. We further investigate the $Om(z)$ diagnostic and statefinder parameter. These results indicate that the logarithmic Herglotz model provides a consistent phenomenological description of the homogeneous and isotropic late-time expansion history, while allowing departures from the standard $Λ$CDM background.

</details>


### [15] [Identifiability Limits of Gravitational Wave Phase Deviations: Multiclass Classification with a Multihead Neural Network](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31993)
*Lavinia Heisenberg,Shayan Hemmatyar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper evaluates neural networks for detecting and characterizing gravitational-wave phase deviations from general relativity using classification (identifying post-Einsteinian exponent b) and regression (estimating coupling strength log10|β|); performance is benchmarked against an approximate residual-based classifier, revealing that confusion stems from intrinsic waveform similarity between neighboring phase families—not network architecture limitations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop robust, data-driven methods for testing general relativity with gravitational-wave observations, specifically identifying subtle phase deviations beyond GR using interpretable, high-fidelity neural network classifiers and regressors.

Method: A dual-head neural network (classification + regression) is trained on response functions—quantifying mismatch sensitivity to phase deformations—using simulated Gaussian noise (Advanced LIGO design spectrum, 262 sources; O3 Hanford spectrum, 132 sources) and real O3 Hanford strain data. Classification targets six parametrized post-Einsteinian phase families (b ∈ {−7,−5,−3,−1,+1,+2}); regression predicts log10|β|. Performance is compared against an approximate classifier built from analytically predicted waveform residuals.

Result: Classification accuracies: 0.427 (design spectrum) and 0.329 (O3 Hanford spectrum); recurrent networks perform best among 13 variants but still underperform the approximate residual-based classifier; confusion patterns match between both classifiers, indicating fundamental degeneracy—not model limitation—as the bottleneck; networks trained on real or simulated noise generalize well to recorded strain.

Conclusion: Neural networks can reliably detect and estimate gravitational-wave phase deviations, but classification accuracy is fundamentally limited by waveform similarity among neighboring post-Einsteinian families—not by network capacity or training data fidelity—suggesting that future tests require either higher SNR, broader parameter coverage, or joint inference over multiple deviation types.

Abstract: We study how well gravitational-wave phase deviations can be identified using a neural network with classification and regression heads. The classification head distinguishes general relativity (GR) from six parametrized post-Einsteinian phase families with exponents $b\in\{-7,-5,-3,-1,+1,+2\}$; the regression head predicts the logarithm of the coupling magnitude, $\log_{10}|β|$. The network input is a response function quantifying the sensitivity of waveform mismatch to waveform deformations. We use stationary Gaussian noise with the Advanced LIGO design spectrum for 262 sources and a measured Hanford spectrum from the third observing run (O3) for 132 sources, and also test injections into recorded Hanford strain. On the two Gaussian datasets, the network identifies the exponent of deviations clearly above the noise with accuracies of $0.427$ and $0.329$. To interpret these results, we compare the network with an approximate classifier built from predicted waveform residuals for the six phase families and same sources. The two classifiers often confuse the same families. Examining residuals left by different phase corrections, we link these errors to similarities between the underlying deviations. On selected recorded Hanford strain, networks trained on simulated or recorded noise achieve overall classification accuracies close to those on simulated Hanford noise. Of thirteen neural-network variants tested on the Advanced LIGO design dataset, the recurrent network has the highest mean classification accuracy for loud deviations, although all variants remain below the approximate classifier. The network nearly matches that classifier's accuracy, indicating that identification is limited by similarities between neighboring phase families rather than by the network, for known intrinsic source parameters.

</details>


### [16] [Relational Frame Equivalence and Exact Cosmology in Brans--Dicke Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32047)
*Yaser Tavakoli*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs an exactly solvable relational cosmological model in Brans–Dicke theory using a canonical clock-sector framework, yielding closed-form expressions for key observables (e.g., scale factor, Hubble rate, e-folds) and demonstrating frame equivalence between Jordan and Einstein frames via clock-adapted dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain an explicit cosmological realization of frame-adapted relational dynamics in scalar–tensor gravity and derive closed-form cosmological observables within Brans–Dicke FLRW cosmology.

Method: Applies the canonical clock-sector formalism to spatially flat FLRW Brans–Dicke cosmology; derives the clock momentum $P_Φ = p_φ - a p_a / (2φ)$ from the full ADM reduction; identifies a quadratic Jordan-frame potential whose Einstein-frame counterpart is constant; solves the reduced relational system exactly using a single parameter $u$; compares analytical solution with numerical integrations in Jordan/Einstein frames and naive reduction.

Result: Exact integrability of the relational FLRW system; closed-form expressions for $a(u)$, $H(t)$, $\epsilon_H(u)$, $N(u)$, $w_φ(u)$; identification of an inflationary regime starting at $u = \operatorname{arctanh}(1/\sqrt{3})$ transitioning to asymptotic de Sitter; numerical confirmation of trajectory equivalence under clock-dependent canonical transformations.

Conclusion: The model serves as an exactly solvable laboratory for relational frame equivalence in scalar–tensor cosmology; provides explicit, physically interpretable cosmological observables; validates the clock-sector framework as a robust tool for constructing frame-invariant relational dynamics and enables extensions to more general potentials and theories.

Abstract: We apply the canonical clock-sector framework developed in Ref.~\cite{Tavakoli:2026coc} to spatially flat FLRW cosmology in Brans--Dicke theory. The aim is to obtain an explicit cosmological realization of the frame-adapted relational dynamics and to determine the corresponding cosmological observables in closed form. Starting from the previously developed full ADM theory and restricting it to the homogeneous sector, we obtain the canonical clock momentum as $P_Φ=p_φ-ap_a/2φ$. This identification allows the Jordan and Einstein-frame reduced systems to be represented on a common canonical phase space. We then consider a quadratic Jordan-frame potential for which the Einstein-frame potential is constant. The resulting relational FLRW system is exactly integrable. The complete trajectory can be expressed in terms of a single parameter $u$, yielding closed-form expressions for the scale factor, the cosmic-time Hubble rate, the Hubble slow-roll parameter, the number of e-folds, and the scalar equation-of-state parameter. In particular, the exact solution provides an explicit inflationary regime that begins at $u=\operatorname{arctanh}(1/\sqrt{3})$ and transitions toward an asymptotic de Sitter regime. We finally compare the exact solution with direct numerical integration in the adapted Jordan and Einstein descriptions, and with the naively reduced Jordan system based on the unshifted scalar momentum. The numerical results reproduce the exact trajectory and illustrate how the different reduced canonical representations are related by the clock-dependent transformation implied by the canonical clock-sector construction. The analysis provides an exactly solvable laboratory for relational frame equivalence in scalar--tensor cosmology and gives explicit cosmological observables that can be used in extensions to more general potentials and scalar--tensor models.

</details>


### [17] [Inhomogeneous dust collapse in five-dimensional AdS-Chern-Simons gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32200)
*Luis Avilés,José Díaz,Cristian Quinzacara,Patricio Salgado*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies gravitational collapse of a 5D spherically symmetric dust cloud in AdS Chern–Simons gravity, deriving an exact interior solution in torsion-free LTB coordinates; it identifies shell-focusing curvature singularities, establishes regularity and energy condition preservation, and determines apparent horizon formation via a quasilocal mass threshold when matched to a static black-hole exterior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how higher-dimensional Chern–Simons gravity modifies classical gravitational collapse—particularly singularity formation, horizon dynamics, and matching to vacuum solutions—in contrast to GR or Lovelock theories.

Method: Uses comoving Lemaître–Tolman–Bondi (LTB) coordinates in the torsion-free sector of five-dimensional AdS Chern–Simons gravity; solves reduced field equations exactly for inhomogeneous dust; analyzes regularity (central conditions, shell crossing), curvature (Kretschmann scalar), energy conditions, and junction conditions to the static black-hole exterior; computes null expansions to locate apparent horizons.

Result: Obtains an exact collapsing interior solution where areal radius evolves periodically but physical collapse terminates at shell focusing or loss of regularity; proves shell focusing is a genuine curvature singularity satisfying weak/strong energy conditions; derives critical quasilocal mass threshold for finite-radius apparent horizon formation; shows smooth matching to black-hole exterior and consistent homogeneous (FLRW) limit.

Conclusion: Five-dimensional AdS Chern–Simons gravity admits well-behaved dust collapse with controlled singularity formation and horizon genesis governed by a geometrically defined quasilocal mass; the theory preserves key physical features of GR collapse while introducing distinct threshold behavior absent in standard Einstein gravity.

Abstract: We study the gravitational collapse of a spherically symmetric, inhomogeneous dust cloud in five-dimensional AdS Chern--Simons gravity. Working in the torsion-free sector and using comoving Lemaître--Tolman--Bondi coordinates, we derive the reduced field equations and obtain an exact collapsing interior solution. The areal radius evolves periodically in time, but the physical collapsing branch extends only up to shell focusing or an earlier loss of regularity, while the initial density determines a radial function whose square is proportional to the conserved quasilocal mass. This separates the roles of the initial velocity and density profiles in controlling shell focusing and trapping.
  We derive conditions for central regularity and for avoiding shell crossing, analyze the Kretschmann scalar, and show that shell focusing corresponds to a genuine curvature singularity, the weak and strong energy conditions are preserved throughout the regular collapsing phase. The homogeneous limit reduces consistently to a five-dimensional FLRW interior.
  The collapsing cloud is matched smoothly to the black-hole branch of the static AdS Chern--Simons exterior. The null expansions show that a finite-radius apparent horizon forms only when the quasilocal mass exceeds a critical threshold. Initially untrapped shells above this threshold become trapped before shell focusing, whereas below the total-mass threshold no finite-radius future-trapped symmetry sphere forms in the regular interior.

</details>


### [18] [Stability Analysis of Canonical Acoustic Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32277)
*HengZhong Fang,LiQiang Miao,ChangSen Liu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the stability of a canonical acoustic black hole under scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac field perturbations using third-order WKB to compute quasi-normal modes (QNMs); all perturbations yield positive effective potentials and negative QNM imaginary parts, confirming stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the stability of canonical acoustic black holes against non-minimally coupled scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac field perturbations—key for assessing their physical viability as analog gravity models.

Method: Third-order WKB approximation to compute quasi-normal modes (QNMs); analysis of effective potential behavior for each field type under varying quantum numbers and coupling parameters.

Result: All three perturbations yield positive effective potentials and negative imaginary parts of QNMs—indicating stable decay. Scalar perturbations depend on l, n, and coupling ζ (larger ζ → faster decay); electromagnetic perturbations depend on l and n; Dirac perturbations depend on n and |k|.

Conclusion: The canonical acoustic black hole is stable under non-minimally coupled scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac field perturbations, as confirmed by positive effective potentials and damped QNMs.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate the stability of canonical acoustic black hole under the perturbation of non-minimally coupling scalar field, electromagnetic field and Dirac field. The quasi-normal modes(QNMs) are computed using the three-order WKB approximation method. The results show that, for all these three kinds of perturbation, the values of the effective potential are positive, while the imaginary parts of QNMs are negative, the canonical acoustic black hole is a table. In details respectively, for scalar field perturbation, the effective potential and the QNMs depend on the angular quantum number $l$, the overtone number $n$, and the coupling parameter ζ, the perturbation decays more rapidly for bigger ζ. For electromagnetic field perturbation, the effective potential and QNMs depend on $l$ and $n$. For Dirac field perturbation, the effective potential and QNMs depend on $n$ and the total angular quantum number | k |.

</details>


### [19] [Memory effect from neutron star merger counterparts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32412)
*T. Brabant,M. Pillas,H. Inchauspé,Z. Lin,F. Foucart,M. Bulla*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper quantifies nonlinear and linear gravitational-wave memory contributions from binary neutron star (BNS) mergers—including GWs, GRBs, afterglows, neutrinos, ejecta, and kilonovae—and assesses their detectability with Einstein Telescope (ET) and LISA; it finds that ET could have detected the combined memory from GW170817 at SNR ≈ 10.7 (dominated by nonlinear memory), while linear memory from BNS counterparts remains undetectable except in extreme, highly favorable cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable the first observational detection of the gravitational-wave memory effect—particularly distinguishing between nonlinear and linear contributions—by leveraging next-generation detectors (ET and LISA) and multi-messenger data from BNS mergers.

Method: The authors compute both nonlinear memory (from GW self-interaction) and linear memory (from anisotropic emission of matter/radiation) across multiple BNS merger components: GWs, GRBs, afterglows, neutrinos, dynamical/disk-wind ejecta, and kilonovae. They model memory waveforms and estimate signal-to-noise ratios (SNRs) for detection with ET and LISA, applying the framework to GW170817 and a synthetic GRB population.

Result: For GW170817, the total memory SNR in ET is 10.7, dominated by nonlinear memory; linear memory contributions are subdominant. In a synthetic GRB population, only extreme-energy, optimally oriented GRBs yield detectable linear memory in ET. LISA is not sensitive to these BNS memory signals due to frequency band mismatch.

Conclusion: Next-generation ground-based detectors like ET will be capable of detecting the nonlinear GW memory from BNS mergers, but detecting linear memory from associated astrophysical emissions (e.g., GRBs, kilonovae) remains highly challenging and feasible only under exceptional conditions.

Abstract: The gravitational-wave (GW) memory effect is a prediction of general relativity, characterized by a permanent change in spacetime geometry. Although it has not yet been observed, next-generation detectors such as the Einstein Telescope (ET) and the Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA) are expected to provide the sensitivity required for its first detection. GW memory comprises two distinct contributions: the nonlinear memory, generated by the self-interaction of GWs, and the linear memory, originating from the anisotropic emission of matter and radiation associated with astrophysical transients. In this work, we quantify both nonlinear and linear memory contributions produced during binary neutron star mergers (BNSs), including those associated with GWs, gamma-ray bursts (GRBs), afterglows, neutrinos, dynamical and disk-wind ejecta, and kilonova emission. We evaluate the detectability of the resulting memory signals with ET and LISA through two applications. First, we apply our analysis to the multi-messenger event GW170817 - GRB 170817A - AT2017gfo. We find that the combined memory signal from this event would have been detectable by ET with a signal-to-noise ratio of 10.7 dominated by the nonlinear memory. We also assess the detectability of the GRB memory across a synthetic population of GRBs, and find that only extreme-energy and jointly favorable configurations could produce a detectable signal in ET. Together, these results demonstrate that while next-generation detectors will probe nonlinear memory, observing linear memory from BNS counterparts will remain elusive except in the most favorable scenarios.

</details>


### [20] [Energy extraction from Kerr--Bertotti--Robinson black holes: polar region collapse and the capture of negative energy protons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32494)
*Ke Wang,Xiao-Xiong Zeng*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes particle acceleration and energy extraction near a Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black hole — an exact Einstein-Maxwell solution with rotation and asymptotically uniform magnetic field — revealing distinct electromagnetic structure and novel energy extraction mechanisms compared to the Wald-Kerr case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how charged particle acceleration and energy extraction operate in a rotating black hole immersed in a uniform magnetic field, specifically contrasting the Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (KBR) solution with the standard Wald-Kerr setup, where magnetic geometry and energy flux behavior differ fundamentally.

Method: Pointwise calculation of charged particle acceleration using electromagnetic invariants; identification of particle capture domains via the gauge-invariant flux criterion; integration over the (r,θ) plane to compute net energy and angular momentum fluxes; comparison of electromagnetic field topology (e.g., E·B=0 surface, A_φ structure) between KBR and Wald-Kerr solutions.

Result: Protons and electrons undergo opposite radial acceleration in polar vs. equatorial regions; the polar acceleration region shrinks with increasing B; captured protons carry negative energy/angular momentum, electrons positive; net energy extraction occurs in strong magnetic fields, unlike Wald-Kerr which requires anisotropic plasma for extraction — here it arises naturally under spherical symmetry.

Conclusion: The Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black hole enables Penrose-like energy extraction without requiring anisotropic plasma distributions, due to its intrinsic non-cylindrical magnetic geometry and contracting E·B=0 surface — offering a physically distinct and potentially more robust mechanism for astrophysical energy extraction.

Abstract: The Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black hole is an exact Einstein-Maxwell solution describing a rotating black hole in an asymptotically uniform magnetic field. It differs from the Kerr black hole in the Wald solution: the magnetic field lines are no longer cylindrical, with the constant-\(A_φ\) lines saturating at large distances, and the interface \(\vec{E}\cdot\vec{B}=0\) is no longer a cone but contracts with radius and eventually disappears. Determining the acceleration of charged particles pointwise, we find that protons are driven outward and electrons inward in the polar region, and vice versa in the equatorial region; the polar region shrinks as the magnetic field grows. Captured protons carry negative energy and angular momentum, whereas captured electrons carry positive energy and angular momentum. Fixing the capture domain by the gauge-invariant flux criterion and integrating over the \((r,θ)\) plane, we find that in weak magnetic fields the black hole gains mass, while in strong magnetic fields energy extraction occurs. Energy extraction can be achieved under the usual spherically symmetric density, without introducing the anisotropic density distribution required in the Wald solution for a Kerr black hole.

</details>


### [21] [Exact Solutions for Chiral Gravitational Waves from Spin-2 Mixing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32571)
*Mohammad Ali Gorji,Yuhang Zhu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper solves exactly the coupled system of inflationary tensor perturbations (gravitons) and a spectator spin-2 field with parity violation, yielding closed-form power spectra via hypergeometric functions; it predicts exponential chiral enhancement of one graviton helicity and bounds circular polarization by tanh(πθ).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond perturbative treatments of graviton–spectator spin-2 mixing during inflation, especially to capture nonperturbative effects and parity-violating dynamics that may generate highly chiral primordial gravitational waves.

Method: Exact analytical solution of the coupled tensor mode equations in de Sitter space without expanding in the linear mixing parameter; uses operator-valued Gauss hypergeometric functions acting on Whittaker modes; derives late-time power spectra in terms of generalized hypergeometric functions $_3F_2$.

Result: Closed-form expressions for the late-time power spectra of both graviton helicities and the spectator field, plus their cross-spectrum; exponential enhancement of one helicity; circular polarization bounded by $\tanh(\pi\theta)$, independent of mass or mixing strength; recovery of perturbative Schwinger-Keldysh result in weak-mixing limit.

Conclusion: Strong nonperturbative mixing between gravitons and a massive spectator spin-2 field can produce large, highly chiral primordial gravitational wave signals; the exact solution establishes a robust nonperturbative framework for analyzing such sourced tensor spectra.

Abstract: A spectator spin-2 field can mix linearly with the metric tensor perturbations. We study the coupled tensor system in an inflationary background, including a parity violating effect parametrised by $θ$. Without expanding in the linear mixing between the two fields, we solve the system exactly for arbitrary mass while treating the mixing strength nonperturbatively. The mode functions of both helicities are constructed by acting with an operator-valued Gauss hypergeometric function ${}_2F_1$ on Whittaker modes, and the late-time power spectrum of each graviton helicity is expressed in closed form in terms of the generalised hypergeometric function ${}_3F_2$. We find that one helicity is exponentially enhanced, showing that strong mixing with a spectator spin-2 field can generate a large and highly chiral primordial gravitational wave spectrum. The degree of circular polarisation is bounded by $\tanh(πθ)$, independently of the spin-2 mass and the mixing strength. The same solution also gives the late-time power spectrum of the spectator field and its cross spectrum with the graviton. In the weak mixing limit, our exact result reproduces the perturbative Schwinger-Keldysh result. These solutions provide a nonperturbative framework for studying primordial gravitational waves sourced by additional tensor modes.

</details>


### [22] [Thermal equilibrium in general relativity and the special nature of the cosmological constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32608)
*M. L. Rodrigues,J. B. Formiga*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates thermal equilibrium in general relativity, highlighting the cosmological-constant fluid as uniquely always in thermal equilibrium and possessing a universal energy $E_Λ = ρ_Λ V$; it employs teleparallel gravity to compute quasi-local energies of black holes (Reissner-Nordström-de Sitter and regular), addressing zero-point energy issues and suggesting implications for quantum gravity with minimal length scales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conceptual issues surrounding thermal equilibrium and gravitational energy in general relativity—particularly the special thermodynamic status of the cosmological constant—and to reconcile gravitational energy definitions with quantum gravity constraints such as minimal length.

Method: Uses the teleparallel equivalent of general relativity (TEGR) to define and compute quasi-local gravitational energy; applies it to Reissner-Nordström-de Sitter and regular black hole spacetimes; analyzes thermodynamic properties of the cosmological-constant fluid and its energy density-volume relation.

Result: Demonstrates that the cosmological-constant fluid is the only perfect fluid always in thermal equilibrium; derives universal intrinsic energy $E_Λ = ρ_Λ V$ for any spatial volume $V$; computes consistent quasi-local energies for two black hole solutions in TEGR; identifies implications for zero-point energy and minimal-length quantum gravity models.

Conclusion: The cosmological constant’s fluid has a privileged thermodynamic role and yields a universal, volume-dependent energy; TEGR provides a robust framework for quasi-local energy in curved spacetimes with Λ, offering insights into gravitational energy localization and potential bridges to quantum gravity.

Abstract: The problem of thermal equilibrium in general relativity is discussed and the unique role played by the fluid associated with the cosmological constant is emphasized. In addition to being the only fluid that is always in thermal equilibrium, it is shown here that its energy can also be seen as a universal quantity given by $E_Λ=ρ_ΛV$, where $V$ is a spatial volume. Furthermore, if the cosmological constant turns out to be a universal constant, then the conclusion that any spatial volume $V$ has an intrinsic energy follows naturally from the approach considered here. A possible application to quantum theories of gravity that postulate the existence of a minimal length scale is discussed. In studying the energies, the so-called teleparallel equivalent of general relativity is used, and the difficulty with the zero-point energy is discussed in light of the gravitational energy problem. The quasi-local energies of the Reissner-Nordström-de Sitter black hole and of a regular black hole are calculated. The results obtained here with the teleparallel theory are consistent with our expectations.

</details>


### [23] [One nat per cell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32879)
*Ira Wolfson*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy formula $S_{BH} = A/(4\ell_P^2)$ from first principles—using only the Planck-scale irreducibility of entropy and horizon scrambling—without relying on quantum field theory, general relativity field equations, or a microscopic Hilbert space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a model-independent, operational derivation of black hole entropy that does not depend on specific quantum gravity proposals, field equations, or assumptions about microstates.

Method: Applies information-theoretic reasoning: (1) treats entropy as objective, irreducible uncertainty at the Planck scale (not subjective ignorance); (2) assumes the horizon acts as a complete scrambler, driving the Wigner function below the Planck resolution (Ehrenfest time folding); (3) computes the number of indistinguishable configurations per Planck cell via filament crossing orderings, yielding $\Omega_1 = e$ (i.e., 1 nat per cell); (4) combines $N = A/\ell_P^2$ independent cells to obtain total phase-space volume $\Omega = e^N$, hence $S = N$ nats; (5) introduces kinematic factor $1/4$ from null phase-space causality to recover $S_{BH}$ in standard units.

Result: Derives $S_{BH} = A/(4\ell_P^2)$ solely from Planck-scale information bounds and horizon scrambling, without invoking quantum fields, Einstein’s equations, or a pre-specified Hilbert space.

Conclusion: Black hole entropy is a direct consequence of fundamental limits on resolvable information at the Planck scale and dynamical scrambling at the horizon—establishing $S_{BH}$ as an inevitable, theory-agnostic measure of irreducible uncertainty.

Abstract: A black hole horizon carries exactly one nat of information per Planck cell, without naming what the black hole is made of. Two inputs suffice: entropy is irreducible uncertainty (the configurations no observation can resolve at the Planck floor, an objective bound, not ignorance), and the horizon is a complete scrambler, so by the Ehrenfest time its Wigner function has folded below the floor and no cell can be certified empty. A single cell then holds unresolved filament crossings; a cell crossed $n$ times admits $n!$ orderings that scrambling erases, so cardinality $n$ carries weight $1/n!$ and its count is $Ω_1=\sum_n 1/n!=e$, one nat. The $N=A/\ell_P^2$ independent cells give $Ω=e^N$, hence $S=N$. Multiplied by the kinematic factor $\tfrac{1}{4}$ (the causally accessible fraction of the null phase space, established separately), this returns $S_{BH}=A/4\ell_P^2$. The derivation invokes no field equations, no microscopic theory, and no posited Hilbert space.

</details>


### [24] [Gravitational-Wave Modulation of Atomic Spontaneous Emission: Electrodynamical Theory, Quadrupolar Redistribution, and Rydberg Cavity Metrology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32905)
*Sohrab Rahvar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a QED framework for spontaneous emission under weak gravitational waves (GWs), revealing Floquet sideband formation, quadrupolar angular redistribution of emission, and transient spectral asymmetries; it proposes a cavity-QED-based differential detection scheme using Rydberg atoms or highly charged ions for high-frequency GW sensing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum matter–spacetime interplay via gravitational waves by extending QED to dynamical spacetime backgrounds.

Method: Quantizes the electromagnetic field in a linearized gravitational background; applies Floquet theory to analyze GW-induced phase modulation of vacuum modes; derives spontaneous emission modifications; formulates a cavity-QED master equation and proposes a differential cross-cavity detection protocol with Rydberg ensembles or highly charged ions.

Result: GWs induce Floquet ladders in photon states; preserve total decay rate to first order in strain but impose spin-2 quadrupolar angular redistribution; generate phase-dependent transient spectral asymmetries; cavity-QED detection is shown to be theoretically viable for high-frequency GWs.

Conclusion: Cavity QED provides a feasible platform for testing QED in dynamical spacetime and enables novel high-frequency gravitational-wave detection strategies.

Abstract: Studying Gravitational Waves (GWs) provide a unique probe of the interplay between quantum matter and the dynamics of spacetime. We develop a QED framework for spontaneous emission in the presence of a weak classical GW by quantizing the electromagnetic field in a linearized gravitational background. We show that a passing GW dynamically phase-modulates vacuum electromagnetic modes, dressing photon states into a Floquet ladder of sidebands separated by the GW frequency. While the total spontaneous decay rate remains unchanged to first order in the GW strain, the emission pattern develops a characteristic spin-2 quadrupolar angular redistribution modulated with the GW phase. For finite interaction times, interference between the carrier and Floquet sidebands generates phase-dependent spectral asymmetries in the transient emission spectrum. To explore experimental observability, we formulate a cavity-QED master equation description and propose a differential cross-cavity detection protocol employing Rydberg ensembles or highly charged ions. These results establish cavity QED as a promising platform for exploring quantum electrodynamics in dynamical spacetime and for high-frequency gravitational-wave detection.

</details>


### [25] [Hybrid-star asteroseismology across sharp phase interfaces: Slow and rapid conversion in the $f$- and $p_1$-mode spectra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33002)
*Zoey Zhiyuan Dong,Hao-Jui Kuan,Nicolas Yunes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how first-order phase transitions (e.g., hadron-to-quark) in neutron-star interiors affect polar quasi-normal modes (QNMs), specifically the f- and p₁-modes, using full general relativity. It compares two physical limits of phase conversion—slow (interface comoves with fluid) vs. rapid (interface follows phase equilibrium)—and finds that the p₁-mode frequency is highly sensitive to the interface dynamics, exhibiting up to ~3.5 kHz separation on twin-star branches, while the f-mode remains largely insensitive to interface conditions but reflects global EOS deviations from pure hadronic models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the nature of strong-interaction phase transitions in neutron-star cores via gravitational-wave asteroseismology, particularly distinguishing between slow and rapid hadron–quark phase conversion mechanisms using quasi-normal-mode signatures.

Method: Full general-relativistic linear perturbation theory applied to cold, nonrotating hybrid stars with a sharp hadron–quark interface; equation of state constructed by matching SLy4 hadronic EoS to a constant-sound-speed quark EoS; eigenmode spectra (f and p₁) computed for both slow and rapid phase-conversion limits and their interpolation; parameter-space survey over transition pressure and density discontinuity.

Result: The real part of the p₁-mode frequency shows sizable separation (~3.5 kHz max) between slow and rapid conversion limits—strongest when eigenfunction tangential displacement changes rapidly across the interface and at low transition pressure + large density jump; f-mode is weakly affected by interface condition but deviates from SLy4 reference mainly at low P_trans and/or large Δρ, with distinct boundaries for real vs. imaginary fitting residuals.

Conclusion: The p₁-mode serves as a local probe of the hadron–quark interface dynamics, while the f-mode encodes global structural deviation of hybrid stars from purely hadronic sequences—making combined f/p₁ analysis a powerful diagnostic for phase-transition physics in neutron stars.

Abstract: First-order phase transitions in neutron-star interiors can leave signatures in the polar quasi-normal-mode spectrum. We compute the complex quadrupolar $f$- and $p_1$-mode spectra in full general relativity for cold, nonrotating hybrid stars with a sharp hadron--quark interface. We model the equation of state by matching a hadronic SLy4 phase to a constant-sound-speed quark-phase. We compare slow phase conversion, in which fluid elements retain their phase and the interface moves with the fluid, with rapid phase conversion, in which matter changes phase as it crosses the boundary and the interface follows phase equilibrium rather than the fluid, together with an algebraic interpolation between these limits. On identical equilibrium backgrounds, the two physical limits produce a sizable separation in the real part of the $p_1$-mode frequency. This $p_1$ frequency separation is largest when the local tangential-displacement fraction of the eigenfunction changes rapidly across the interface. Across the surveyed EOS parameter space, rapid conversion generally lowers the $p_1$-mode frequency relative to slow conversion, with compactness-averaged separations reaching $\sim3.5\,{\rm kHz}$ on disconnected twin-star branches. The largest separations occur at relatively low transition pressures and large density discontinuities. By contrast, the $f$-mode is only weakly affected by the interface condition for individual stellar models. Across the sampled constant-sound-speed parameter space, departures exceeding the hadronic SLy4 reference residual occur mainly at low transition pressure, large density discontinuity, or both, with different boundaries for the real- and imaginary-part fitting variables. Thus, the $p_1$-mode probes the local interface response, whereas the tracked $f$-mode reflects the global departure of the hybrid sequence from the hadronic calibration.

</details>


### [26] [A construction of charged naked singularities for the Einstein--Maxwell-charged scalar field equations in 3+1 dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33168)
*Weihao Zheng*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs global naked singularity solutions for the spherically symmetric Einstein–Maxwell–charged scalar field system in (3+1)D, using asymptotically flat, singular characteristic initial data; it proves the singularity is globally visible (not hidden by a horizon), quantitatively controls the nonzero Maxwell field up to the singularity, and introduces a general framework for building naked singularity exteriors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
