Motivation: To rigorously demonstrate the existence of physically relevant, globally naked singularities—violating cosmic censorship—in a coupled gravitational-electromagnetic-scalar system, with controlled matter fields and geometric structure.

Method: Constructs solutions via characteristic initial value problem with C^{1,α} asymptotically flat one-ended data; employs a tailored geometric PDE analysis to control curvature blow-up, Maxwell field behavior, and causal structure; develops a novel framework for constructing the exterior region (causal future of the past light cone from the singularity), accommodating singular data and nontrivial electromagnetic contributions.

Result: Existence of a class of global, spherically symmetric, asymptotically flat spacetimes solving Einstein–Maxwell–charged scalar field equations, featuring a curvature singularity that is globally naked (i.e., lies in the causal past of future null infinity), with a quantitatively bounded-away-from-zero Maxwell field extending continuously up to the singularity and admitting an explicit asymptotic profile.

Conclusion: Cosmic censorship can fail even for genuinely charged, dynamically coupled gravitational-matter systems with mild regularity assumptions; the constructed naked singularities are robust in the sense that their exteriors admit a generalizable geometric-analytic construction framework.

Abstract: We construct a class of global naked singularity solutions for the $(3+1)$-dimensional spherically symmetric Einstein--Maxwell-charged scalar field equations. The solutions arise from $C^{1,α}$ asymptotically flat one-ended characteristic initial data with $0<α\ll1$ and develop a curvature singularity that is not enclosed by an event horizon. The resulting spacetimes are genuinely charged, with a nonzero Maxwell field that satisfies a quantitative positive lower bound up to the singularity and admits an explicit asymptotic profile. As part of the construction, we develop a general framework for constructing naked singularity exteriors, namely, the causal future region of the past light cone emanating from the singularity. The framework accommodates a broad range of singular characteristic data and, in particular, allows for singular behavior of the Maxwell field near the singularity.

</details>


### [27] [Two-horizon sector thermodynamics and NUT bi-hair in Kerr-Newman-NUT spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33032)
*Di Wu,Robert B. Mann,Shuang-Qing Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes thermodynamic consistency in Kerr-Newman-NUT black holes using a two-horizon (Σ/Δ) entropy–temperature decomposition, showing that NUT charge N behaves as a charge-like variable in the sum sector, while its secondary hair J_N = MN acts rotation-like in the difference sector—distinct from ordinary angular momentum J and not reducible to Q or N alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: First law consistency is insufficient to uniquely fix the thermodynamic state space for NUT-charged black holes; multiple inequivalent macroscopic formulations exist. The paper seeks to identify physically grounded, minimal homogeneous state spaces by leveraging the full charged-rotating-NUT geometry as a stricter test than uncharged Taub-NUT.

Method: A two-horizon diagnostic is developed: inner- and outer-horizon entropies and inverse temperatures are decomposed into sum (Σ) and difference (Δ) sectors. Thermodynamic variables (M, Q, J, N, J_N) are assigned scaling weights; Bekenstein-Smarr relations and first-law coefficient matching are used to test closure and consistency of candidate state spaces.

Result: The Σ sector closes only with Q and N (charge-like), while Δ requires J and J_N (rotation-like). Removing either J_N or N breaks first-law coefficient agreement and prevents Bekenstein-Smarr closure. J_N = MN is identified as an off-shell, thermodynamically emergent secondary hair—not an independent metric parameter or conserved charge.

Conclusion: NUT charge N and its secondary hair J_N occupy distinct thermodynamic roles: N is charge-like (Σ sector), J_N is rotation-like (Δ sector). This supports a 'bi-hair' structure for NUT black holes within minimal homogeneous state spaces, with implications for black hole microstate counting and extended thermodynamics.

Abstract: First law consistency alone does not determine the black hole state space of NUT-charged spacetimes, because several inequivalent macroscopic formulations can yield consistent thermodynamics. We develop a two-horizon sector diagnostic for the electrically charged Kerr-Newman-NUT family by organizing the inner- and outer-horizon entropies and inverse temperatures into sum ($Σ$) and difference ($Δ$) sectors. The charged rotating geometry gives a more stringent test of the uncharged Taub-NUT sector result, since the mass $M$, ordinary electric charge $Q$, ordinary angular momentum $J$, NUT charge $N$, and the NUT secondary hair $J_N=MN$ all enter the same two-horizon thermodynamics. For the physically anchored minimal homogeneous state spaces defined and tested here, we find a clear sector separation: the $Σ$ sector closes with the charge-like variables $Q$ and $N$, whereas the $Δ$ sector additionally requires the rotation-like variables $J$ and $J_N$. Natural reductions that eliminate either $J_N$ or $N$ fail to reproduce the first law coefficient fixed by the sector temperature, and their associated Bekenstein-Smarr relations do not close with the assigned scaling weights. Within this class, electric charge and ordinary rotation therefore do not absorb the NUT secondary response, and the charged rotating family supports the NUT bi-hair organization: $N$ has a charge-like sector role, while $J_N=MN$ is a rotation-like thermodynamic secondary hair. The latter is defined off shell in the homogeneous equation of state, not as a new metric parameter or asymptotic conserved charge.

</details>


### [28] [Quasinormal modes of Hayward black holes from Schwarzschild to extremality: a comparative spectral analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33267)
*Davide Batic,Denys Dutykh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes quasinormal modes (QNMs) of the Hayward black hole using multiprecision Chebyshev collocation, analyzes scalar/electromagnetic and axial perturbations across the Schwarzschild-to-extremal range, identifies discrepancies in spin-2 effective potentials, and cautions against misinterpreting purely imaginary eigenvalues as physical QNMs without analytic continuation validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve inconsistencies in existing QNM calculations for Hayward black holes—particularly regarding spin-2 perturbations and interpretation of purely imaginary eigenvalues—and to provide high-precision spectral data across the full parameter range up to extremality.

Method: Multiprecision Chebyshev collocation applied to scalar, electromagnetic, and axial Regge–Wheeler-type equations; comparison of two spin-2 effective potentials; numerical analysis of imaginary-axis eigenvalue sequences including gap dispersion and sensitivity to discretization parameters.

Result: High-accuracy reproduction of known low-lying QNMs; quantified frequency differences between alternative spin-2 potentials; identification of reproducible purely imaginary eigenvalue ladders with spacing ~0.25Mω near extremality and throat-radius-dependent product ~1/3; no evidence that these imaginary sequences correspond to true QNMs (i.e., poles of the continued Green function).

Conclusion: Purely imaginary eigenvalues from discretisation are spectral candidates—not confirmed QNMs—requiring analytic continuation for physical classification; reported spacings and products are numerical artifacts of the finite-table discretisation, not universal physical laws.

Abstract: We study scalar and electromagnetic quasinormal modes of the Hayward black hole and the axial spectrum of a specified Regge--Wheeler-type effective model, from the Schwarzschild limit to exact extremality. Multiprecision Chebyshev collocation reproduces most available low-lying oscillatory benchmarks and supplies further overtone candidates. The spin-2 equation is model dependent. It differs from the effective-source potential used in recent Hayward studies, and we quantify representative frequency differences. The discretisation also produces purely imaginary eigenvalue sequences, whose physical classification requires special care because highly reproducible imaginary-axis ladders need not be poles of the continued Green function. Accordingly, the imaginary-axis entries reported here are retained as unclassified spectral candidates, not established QNMs. We quantify their finite-table gap dispersion and sensitivity to the number of included gaps. Their spacings near one quarter in $Mω$, and the extremal product near $1/3$ with the throat radius, remain properties of the tabulated candidates, and they do not establish universal QNM spacing or throat-controlled damping.

</details>


### [29] [Dimensional (In)dependence of Lorentzian Quantum Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33452)
*Masazumi Honda,Hiroki Matsui,Kota Numajiri,Kazumasa Okabayashi,Takahiro Terada*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes quantum transition amplitudes for the universe’s creation from nothing in D-dimensional de Sitter minisuperspace using Lorentzian path integrals and Picard–Lefschetz theory; it identifies dominant saddle points, resolves Stokes ambiguities via Borel–Padé resurgence, and finds the tunneling proposal universally realized across 3D–5D and large-D limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a well-defined, dimensionally robust quantum cosmological amplitude for universe creation from nothing, resolving ambiguities inherent in Euclidean path integrals and addressing dimensional dependence of proposals like Hartle–Hawking vs. tunneling.

Method: Lorentzian path integral formulation within de Sitter minisuperspace approximation; Picard–Lefschetz theory to identify and contour-deform onto relevant complex saddle points; numerical Borel–Padé resummation for resurgence analysis of asymptotic series and resolution of Stokes phenomena.

Result: Well-defined Dirichlet-boundary-condition transition amplitudes are obtained; dominant saddle points correspond to tunneling geometries; resurgence successfully eliminates residual Stokes ambiguities; tunneling proposal is consistently realized in D = 3, 4, 5 and in the large-D limit.

Conclusion: The tunneling proposal for universe creation from nothing is a universal feature of Einstein-gravity-based quantum cosmology across spacetime dimensions, robust under rigorous Lorentzian treatment and resurgence completion.

Abstract: We study transition amplitudes of the quantum universe in $D$-dimensional spacetime within the de Sitter minisuperspace approximation in Einstein gravity. Using the Lorentzian path integral combined with Picard-Lefschetz theory, we identify the saddle points that contribute to the integral and derive a well-defined transition amplitude under Dirichlet boundary conditions. We further show numerically that a resurgence analysis based on Borel-Padé resummation is effective in resolving the residual Stokes ambiguities that arise in certain cases. Analyzing three-, four-, and five-dimensional cases, as well as the large-$D$ limit, we find that the tunneling proposal is commonly realized for the creation of the universe from nothing. This result implies a universal feature of quantum cosmology based on Einstein gravity across dimensions.

</details>


### [30] [Polar-metric/axial-gauge perturbations of the Maldacena-Milekhin-Popov wormhole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33511)
*Abhishake Sadhukhan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes linear perturbations of the Maldacena-Milekhin-Popov (MMP) traversable wormhole on a magnetically charged background, revealing coupling between polar metric and axial gauge field perturbations; it derives two coupled Zerilli-Moncrief–type master equations, shows consistency requires fermionic backreaction including the full 2D conformal anomaly and an induced Hall current, identifies AdS₂×S² throat geometry at α=0 with Poschl-Teller spectra, computes O(α) corrections that mix modes and lower frequencies, and proves absence of growing modes for l ≥ 2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess linear stability of the MMP traversable wormhole—specifically whether its semiclassical construction (relying on Casimir energy from lowest Landau level fermions in a magnetic throat) remains consistent under perturbations, and to resolve whether the original MMP treatment (which omitted the trace part of the 2D conformal anomaly) is physically complete.

Method: Decomposes linear perturbations into polar/axial sectors on a magnetically charged background; identifies coupling between polar metric and axial gauge perturbations; reduces dynamics to two coupled master equations of Zerilli-Moncrief type; incorporates fermionic backreaction α via exact 2D conformal anomaly (including trace term); derives induced Hall current from effective action, requiring an Euler-term coupling (c/48π) analogous to Wen-Zee; solves throat equations analytically at α = 0 (AdS₂×S² → Poschl-Teller), then computes O(α) corrections in closed form; matches solutions across throat-mouth overlap; analyzes potential positivity and mode stability.

Result: The throat sector is self-consistent only when both the full 2D conformal anomaly (trace included) and the induced Hall current are incorporated; the O(α) corrections mix the two decoupled Poschl-Teller modes, lower their normal frequencies, split degenerate levels with real first-order shifts, and yield a symmetric potential that is strictly positive—ensuring no exponentially growing modes for l ≥ 2 at linear order in α.

Conclusion: The MMP wormhole’s throat sector is linearly stable against polar-axial coupled perturbations for l ≥ 2 when fermionic backreaction is treated fully—i.e., including the trace anomaly and Hall response—thereby validating and extending the semiclassical construction beyond the original MMP approximation.

Abstract: We study linear perturbations of the Maldacena-Milekhin-Popov (MMP) traversable wormhole. On a magnetically charged background, polar metric perturbations couple to axial perturbations of the gauge field. We treat this sector and reduce it to two coupled master equations of Zerilli-Moncrief type. The wormhole has two regions. In the mouths the geometry is a nearly extremal magnetic Reissner-Nordstrom black hole with no quantum source. In the throat the Casimir energy of the lowest Landau level of charged fermions holds the wormhole open. We show that the throat equations are consistent only if the fermions respond to the perturbation, computed exactly from the two-dimensional conformal anomaly with the full stress tensor including the trace part that MMP discard. This response must be accompanied by an induced Hall current of the fermions. With both effects included, the linearised Bianchi identities hold at first order in the fermionic backreaction $α$. Deriving the Hall current from the fermion effective action instead requires an Euler term with coefficient $c/48π$, which becomes a coupling between magnetic flux and curvature similar to a Wen-Zee term. At $α=0$ the throat is exact AdS$_2\times S^2$ and the modes decouple into two Poschl-Teller problems with masses $l(l-1)$ and $(l+1)(l+2)$, with levels spaced by $1/\ell$ in frequency. We give the $O(α)$ corrections in closed form. They mix the two modes and depend on frequency, and match the mouths in the overlap region. The symmetric part of the potential is positive throughout the wormhole, excluding purely exponential growth. The corrections lower the normal frequencies and split the levels shared by the two modes, with real shifts at first order in $α$. This sector has no growing mode for $l\ge2$ at this order.

</details>


### [31] [Stochastic gravitational-wave background from self-interacting superradiant clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33596)
*Yin-Da Guo,Richard Brito,Chen Yuan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB) from black hole–boson cloud systems with ultralight self-interacting scalar fields, showing that self-interactions suppress the SGWB—relaxing current LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA constraints—and quantifying detectability prospects for Einstein Telescope and Cosmic Explorer.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gravitational-wave observations provide a probe for new fundamental fields; BH–boson clouds formed via superradiance are a well-motivated source of nearly monochromatic GWs, but prior SGWB studies neglected scalar self-interactions—whose inclusion is critical for realistic constraints and detection forecasts.

Method: The authors compute the SGWB spectrum from populations of BH–boson clouds, incorporating scalar self-interactions (via quartic coupling parameterized by decay constant $f_s$) that limit cloud growth and GW emission duration; they model the cosmological population, integrate over BH mass–spin–redshift distributions, and assess signal-to-noise ratios for LIGO, Einstein Telescope, and Cosmic Explorer using standard SGWB cross-correlation sensitivity curves.

Result: Self-interactions suppress the SGWB amplitude, rendering LIGO at design sensitivity insensitive to scalar fields with $f_s \lesssim 3\times10^{17}$ GeV regardless of mass; ET can detect SGWB from $f_s = 10^{17}$ GeV scalars in mass range $[10^{-13.0}, 10^{-11.8}]$ eV, CE in $[10^{-13.2}, 10^{-11.7}]$ eV; minimum detectable $f_s$ are $\sim6\times10^{16}$ GeV (ET) and $\sim3\times10^{16}$ GeV (CE).

Conclusion: Scalar self-interactions significantly weaken the SGWB from BH–boson clouds, invalidating previous null-constraint interpretations from LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA; next-generation detectors remain sensitive to moderately self-interacting ultralight scalars, making SGWB a viable probe for such physics in the 2030s.

Abstract: Gravitational-wave (GW) observations offer a powerful probe of new fundamental fields. One well-motivated source is black hole (BH)--boson cloud systems, in which an ultralight scalar field forms a cloud around a rotating BH via superradiance and emits long-lived nearly monochromatic GWs. In this work, we compute the stochastic GW background (SGWB) from such systems, extending previous work by including scalar self-interactions, and discuss its detectability with next-generation ground-based GW detectors. We find that self-interactions can suppress the SGWB and thereby relax existing LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA constraints inferred from null searches. Namely, the LIGO detectors at design sensitivity are insensitive to a SGWB produced by scalar fields with a decay constant $f_\mathrm{s} \lesssim 3\times 10^{17}$ GeV, independently of the scalar field mass. Looking ahead, we show that a moderately self-interacting cloud can still produce a detectable SGWB with next-generation detectors. Under conservative assumptions, for a decay constant $f_\mathrm{s} = 10^{17}$ GeV, the Einstein Telescope (ET) will be sensitive to scalar masses in the range $\sim[10^{-13.0},10^{-11.8}]$ eV, while Cosmic Explorer (CE) will be sensitive to scalar masses in the range $\sim[10^{-13.2},10^{-11.7}]$ eV. We also find that the minimum decay constants that still yield a detectable SGWB for ET and CE are $f_\mathrm{s}\sim 6\times10^{16}\,$GeV and $\sim 3\times10^{16}\,$GeV, respectively.

</details>


### [32] [Exact solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations in null homogeneous Petrov spaces with non-solvable motions groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33613)
*V. V. Obukhov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper classifies exact vacuum solutions of Maxwell's equations in null homogeneous Petrov spacetimes admitting simply transitive unsolvable $G_3$ isometry groups acting on null hypersurfaces, using canonical frame representations of the metric and electromagnetic field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify all exact electromagnetic vacuum solutions invariant under unsolvable $G_3$ symmetry groups in null homogeneous Petrov spacetimes—geometrically distinguished but underexplored backgrounds in exact GR-EM solution theory.

Method: Exploits the canonical frame associated with the $G_3$ group action to express both the contravariant metric tensor and invariant electromagnetic field components as functions of the frame vector fields; applies Lie algebraic classification of unsolvable $G_3$ algebras and symmetry reduction of Maxwell’s vacuum equations.

Result: A complete, explicit classification of all such invariant vacuum electromagnetic fields, tabulated in a ready-to-use form with metric and field components specified up to equivalence under group action.

Conclusion: All Maxwell vacuum solutions respecting the given geometric and symmetry constraints (null homogeneous Petrov type, unsolvable simply transitive $G_3$ on null hypersurfaces) are fully enumerated and structurally characterized via canonical frame formalism.

Abstract: A complete classification of exact solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations for invariant electromagnetic fields in null homogeneous Petrov spaces with unsolvable groups of motions $G_3$, acting simply transitively on null hypersurfaces of null homogeneous Petrov spaces, is conducted. The classification of exact solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations is based on using the representation of components of the contravariant metric tensor and components of the invariant electromagnetic field in terms of vector fields of a canonical frame. The classification results are presented in a form convenient for further use.

</details>


### [33] [Perturbations of Kerr-Newman black holes: separation and mode stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33661)
*Peter Hintz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves mode stability for linearized perturbations of subextremal Kerr-Newman black holes by decoupling spin-1 and spin-2 field equations via a matrix integrating factor and establishing absence of real-frequency quasinormal modes using a Whiting-type transform.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing problem of coupled electromagnetic (spin-1) and gravitational (spin-2) perturbations in the Kerr-Newman spacetime, which Chandrasekhar described as 'apparently indissoluble'.

Method: Applies a matrix integrating factor to decouple suitably transformed first-order systems governing radiative degrees of freedom; performs angular separation and analyzes resulting radial ODEs using a Whiting-type transformation.

Result: Mode stability is rigorously proven on the real axis—i.e., no exponentially growing or stationary (real-frequency) quasinormal modes exist for linearized perturbations of subextremal Kerr-Newman black holes.

Conclusion: The apparent coupling between spin-1 and spin-2 perturbations can be overcome analytically, enabling a proof of real-axis mode stability for the full Einstein-Maxwell system around Kerr-Newman black holes.

Abstract: We study linearized perturbations of the subextremal Kerr-Newman black hole solution of the Einstein-Maxwell equations. We show how to overcome what Chandrasekhar called the "apparent indissolubility of the coupling between the spin-1 and spin-2 fields" by using a matrix integrating factor for suitably transformed first-order systems that describe the radiative degrees of freedom. For the radial ODEs obtained after angular separation, we prove mode stability on the real axis, i.e., the absence of quasinormal modes with real frequencies, using a Whiting-type transform.

</details>


### [34] [Relic Neutrinos Probing Small Scale Primordial Non-Gaussianity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34324)
*Suraj Gavhale,Shin'ichi Nojiri,Sergei D. Odintsov,Oem Trivedi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a cubic photon number source from acoustic damping and introduces a unified response kernel incorporating neutrino decoupling, thermalization, and acoustic coherence to link primordial non-Gaussianity on small scales to observable relic neutrino capture rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe primordial modes erased from the primary CMB by leveraging electromagnetic entropy imprints preserved in relic neutrinos, especially through post-decoupling physics.

Method: Derivation of the cubic photon number source from acoustic damping; construction of a single response kernel unifying neutrino decoupling, thermalization, and acoustic coherence; analysis of folded bispectrum sensitivity and transmission conditions for excited initial states.

Result: A sharpness-dependent transmission condition emerges from the kernel’s filtering of the physical folded profile, enabling direct mapping between small-scale primordial non-Gaussianity and relic neutrino capture rates.

Conclusion: Relic neutrinos serve as a novel probe of pre-CMB entropy production and primordial non-Gaussianity, with observational signatures encoded in their capture rates via a well-defined, physically grounded response kernel.

Abstract: Relic neutrinos retain a record of electromagnetic entropy production after neutrino decoupling, probing primordial modes erased from the primary CMB. We derive the cubic photon number source from acoustic damping and its leading folded sensitive bispectrum window, with neutrino decoupling, thermalization, and acoustic coherence combined in a single response kernel. For excited initial states, this kernel filters the physical folded profile and yields a sharpness dependent transmission condition, directly connecting small scale primordial non-Gaussianity to relic neutrino capture rates.

</details>


### [35] [Avoided crossings and resonances in black hole ringdown with coupled fields: A case study of the Einstein-Maxwell-axion system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34401)
*Takuya Takahashi,Hayato Motohashi,Kazufumi Takahashi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates avoided crossings in quasinormal mode (QNM) spectra of charged black holes in the Einstein-Maxwell-axion system, revealing resonant effects—including enhanced excitation, effective slowdown of decay, and cancellation of QNM contributions—in ringdown waveforms due to coupling between gravitational and electromagnetic modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how avoided crossings—resonant features in QNM spectra—affect black hole ringdown waveforms in multi-field perturbation systems, especially where gravitational, electromagnetic, and axionic fields are coupled.

Method: Analyzes QNM spectra of charged black holes in the Einstein-Maxwell-axion theory; classifies QNM branches by their decoupling-limit character (gravity-, EM-, or axion-led); identifies and characterizes an avoided crossing between gravity- and EM-led branches; validates QNM reconstruction against time-domain evolution; examines decay rates, excitation factors, and mode interference in physical waveforms.

Result: Near the avoided crossing: (i) QNM reconstruction accurately matches ringdown waveforms; (ii) certain initial perturbations yield effectively slower decay; (iii) resonantly enhanced contributions from the two nearby QNMs largely cancel in the physical waveform; (iv) the two branches exchange their physical character (e.g., dominant field content) across the resonance.

Conclusion: Avoided crossings imprint distinct, observable signatures on black hole ringdowns in multi-field theories—such as decay-rate modulation, destructive interference, and mode character swapping—enabling potential detection of field coupling and new physics via gravitational-wave spectroscopy.

Abstract: Avoided crossings in black hole quasinormal-mode (QNM) spectra exhibit characteristic resonant properties, including enhanced excitation factors. We extend the study of this phenomenon to perturbation systems with multiple coupled fields and investigate its consequences for ringdown waveforms. As a concrete example, we consider a charged black hole in the Einstein-Maxwell-axion system, whose QNM branches can be classified according to the gravitational, electromagnetic, and axion modes to which they continuously connect in the decoupling limit. We focus on an avoided crossing between the gravity- and electromagnetic-led branches thus defined. We verify that the QNM reconstruction accurately reproduces the ringdown portion of the directly evolved time-domain waveform. Near the avoided crossing, an effectively slower decay emerges for particular initial perturbations, while the resonantly enhanced contributions from the two QNMs largely cancel in the physical waveform. The QNM decomposition further reveals an exchange of the physical characters of the two branches across the resonance. These results characterize the imprint of resonances on black hole ringdown in systems with multiple coupled fields.

</details>


### [36] [Charged Simpson-Visser-AdS spacetime: QPOs and observational constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34443)
*Saeed Ullah Khan,Javlon Rayimbaev,Muhammad Zahid,Tolaniddin Nurmukhamedov,Abat Muratov,Weiwei Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies particle motion and quasi-periodic oscillations (QPOs) in a charged Simpson-Visser-AdS black hole/wormhole spacetime, showing how the regularization parameter b, charge Q, and AdS radius l affect horizons, ISCO, orbital frequencies, and twin-peak QPOs—constraining Q and l jointly but leaving b unconstrained by current X-ray timing data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how quantum-gravity-inspired regularization (via the Simpson-Visser metric) and AdS curvature modify geodesic structure and QPO phenomenology in black hole spacetimes, beyond standard GR predictions.

Method: Analytical analysis of metric roots and horizon structure; derivation of effective potential for timelike geodesics; computation of ISCO, energy, angular momentum, and epicyclic frequencies (Keplerian, radial, vertical); modeling and comparison of twin-peak high-frequency QPOs with observational data from black hole binaries.

Result: Three horizon topologies emerge (non-extremal BH, extremal BH, horizonless wormhole); b elevates fundamental QPO frequencies relative to Schwarzschild/Reissner–Nordström–AdS; QPO data rule out Schwarzschild at ~4σ but only constrain a degenerate Q–l combination; b remains observationally unconstrained.

Conclusion: The Simpson-Visser regularization introduces qualitatively new QPO signatures independent of charge and cosmological curvature; current QPO observations are insensitive to b, highlighting its potential as a 'hidden' quantum-gravity parameter in strong-field gravity tests.

Abstract: In this article, we examine particle dynamics and Quasi-periodic oscillations within a charged Simpson-Visser-Anti-de Sitter (AdS) spacetime. We first analyze the behavior of the metric and black hole horizons under the competing impact of electric charge $Q$, regularization parameter $b$, and AdS radius $l$. The roots of the metric function reveal transitions between three distinct topological classes: non-extremal configurations featuring distinct inner and outer horizons, extremal limits with a single degenerate horizon, and strictly positive, horizonless wormhole geometries. By evaluating the effective potential, we determine how this modified geometry shifts the Innermost Stable Circular Orbit (ISCO) and alters the angular momentum and energy of test particles. Moreover, we derive the Keplerian, radial, and vertical frequencies to model quasi-periodic oscillations (QPOs). We show that the interplay between the regularization parameter $b$ and the AdS radius $l$ significantly modifies twin-peak QPOs. In particular, $b$ drives fundamental QPO frequencies higher than those predicted by standard general relativistic backgrounds. Twin-peak high-frequency QPO data from two black-hole binaries disfavor the Schwarzschild limit at $\sim\!4σ$, but constrain only a degenerate combination of charge and AdS curvature, leaving the regularization parameter entirely unconstrained by the data.

</details>


### [37] [Dynamical dark energy from quantum gravity condensates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34917)
*Daniele Granata,Daniele Oriti*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends group field theory (GFT) condensate cosmology to model dynamical dark energy from quantum gravity, incorporating multiple condensate modes, phase dynamics, and an extra scalar matter field; it shows asymptotic de Sitter expansion with transient phantom-like or w-crossing dark energy behavior, linking matter content to quantum-gravity-induced cosmic acceleration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize and strengthen the GFT condensate mechanism for quantum-gravity-originated dark energy by moving beyond simplified models—specifically, by including a second scalar field, multi-mode condensates, and full phase dynamics—to assess robustness and observational viability.

Method: Uses mean-field approximation with coherent peaked GFT states and Hermitian interactions; derives effective Friedmann–Lemaître–Robertson–Walker cosmological dynamics from the GFT quantum hydrodynamics framework; analyzes late-time asymptotics and equation-of-state evolution, particularly w(z), including phantom divide crossing conditions.

Result: The system asymptotically approaches de Sitter space dominated by a single condensate mode; however, transient phases exhibit dynamical dark energy with w ≠ −1, including phantom regimes (w < −1) and phantom-divide crossings enabled by interplay between condensate phase, subdominant modes, and the additional scalar matter field.

Conclusion: Dynamical dark energy—including observationally relevant features like w-crossing—is a generic, robust outcome of GFT condensate cosmology beyond minimal setups; the inclusion of matter fields and multi-mode phase dynamics strengthens the phenomenological relevance and provides concrete pathways for observational tests of quantum gravity.

Abstract: We investigate dynamical dark energy emerging from quantum gravity within the framework of group field theory (GFT) condensate cosmology. We generalise previous models of quantum gravity induced cosmic acceleration by considering a broader parameter space, including an additional scalar matter field besides the relational clock, and accounting simultaneously for the phase dynamics and the contribution of multiple condensate modes. Working in a mean-field approximation with coherent peaked states and restricting to Hermitian GFT interactions, we derive the corresponding effective cosmological dynamics and analyse its late-time behaviour. We show that the system approaches an asymptotic de Sitter regime, dynamically dominated by a single condensate mode, while the approach to this regime can exhibit a non-trivial dynamical dark-energy evolution. In particular, both the condensate phase and subdominant modes can generate deviations from the asymptotic equation of state $w=-1$, including phantom behaviour and, under suitable conditions, a crossing of the phantom divide. The additional scalar matter field enters directly into these conditions, providing a link between the matter content and the quantum gravity induced dark-energy dynamics. These results extend the robustness and generality of the GFT mechanism for dynamical dark energy and provide a basis for confronting the underlying quantum gravity dynamics with cosmological observations.

</details>


### [38] [Horizon, Null-Orbit, and Photon-Sphere Phase Structures in Logarithmic Nonlinear Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34957)
*Peshwaz Abdulkareem Abdoul*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes static, charged, asymptotically flat black holes in logarithmic nonlinear electrodynamics (LNED), identifying three interrelated phase structures—horizon geometry, background circular null orbits, and effective optical photon orbits—in the (Q, ε) parameter space. It reveals a universal zero-radius boundary Q_nc(ε; M), distinct critical curves (extremal, merger, photon-orbit merger), and a bifurcation point (Q_b, ε_b). For ε ≥ ε_b, approaching Q_nc yields 'black points'—zero-horizon remnants with finite photon spheres, vanishing or divergent temperature, quantum-corrected entropy, and modified effective potentials that repel or partially reflect geodesics. Critical impact parameters encode dual gravitational–optical phase behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how nonlinear electrodynamics modifies black hole structure, phase transitions, and light propagation near extremality—particularly whether zero-horizon remnants ('black points') retain observable features like photon spheres and how geodesic dynamics distinguish gravitational vs. optical geometries.

Method: Analytical study of static, spherically symmetric black hole solutions in logarithmic nonlinear electrodynamics (LNED); systematic analysis of horizon existence, circular null geodesics (background and effective optical metric), effective potentials V_eff^bg(r) and V_eff^ph(r), thermodynamic quantities (temperature, entropy), and geodesic behavior (radial/non-radial, impact parameter dependence) across the (Q, ε) parameter space.

Result: Three phase structures share Q_nc(ε; M) = γ M^{2/3} ε^{1/6} as a zero-radius boundary but exhibit distinct critical curves converging at a bifurcation point (Q_b, ε_b). For ε ≥ ε_b, black points emerge with finite photon sphere, quantum-corrected entropy S_η → η, vanishing T at bifurcation but divergent T otherwise (except when ηδ = 1); V_eff^bg develops infinite repulsive barrier at r = 0, while V_eff^ph remains regular with finite maximum—enabling metastable photon orbits. Horizonless configurations exist only above Q_ext for ε < ε_b, but persist up to Q_m^ph for ε ≥ ε_b. Non-radial geodesics are singularity-avoiding and support bound states; radial ones either terminate at r = 0 or escape. Critical impact parameter reflects both spacetime and optical geometry phase structure.

Conclusion: LNED supports novel black hole remnants—'black points'—with no event horizon yet retaining a finite photon sphere and nontrivial optical phenomenology. The decoupling of gravitational and optical critical structures (e.g., Q_m vs. Q_m^ph not meeting at bifurcation) signals intrinsic geometric duality in nonlinear electrodynamics. These results challenge conventional black hole paradigms by showing that zero-horizon limits can preserve observational signatures and support stable photon orbits, mediated by distinct effective potentials governing matter vs. light propagation.

Abstract: We investigate static, charged, asymptotically flat black holes in LNED in the $(Q,ε)$ parameter space. We distinguish three phase structures: the horizon geometry, the background circular null orbits, and the circular photon orbits of the effective optical geometry. All share a zero-radius boundary $Q_{\rm nc}(ε;M)=γM^{2/3}ε^{1/6}$, but have distinct critical structures: the extremal curve $Q_{\rm ext}(ε;M)$ and the merger curve $Q_{\rm m}(ε;M)$ both terminate at the bifurcation point $(Q_{\rm b},ε_{\rm b})$, with $Q_{\rm b}=9M/[2Γ^2(1/4)]$ and $ε_{\rm b}=128π^2Q_{\rm b}^2$, whereas the effective photon-orbit merger curve $Q_{\rm m}^{\rm ph}(ε;M)$ does not pass through this point.
  For $ε\geε_{\rm b}$, approaching $Q_{\rm nc}$ shrinks the horizon to zero and produces black points with quantum-corrected entropy $S_η(r_{\rm h}\to0)=η$. Their temperature vanishes at the bifurcation point and diverges for $ε>ε_{\rm b}$, except at $ηδ=1$. The background potential $V_{\rm eff}^{\rm bg}(r)$ develops an infinite barrier at $r=0$ without local extrema, acting as a perfect repeller. The photon potential $V_{\rm eff}^{\rm ph}(r)$ is regularized with a maximum at finite radius, acting as a partial reflector. Thus the black points retain a photon sphere at finite radius.
  Away from $Q_{\rm nc}$, for $ε<ε_{\rm b}$ both geometries support horizonless structures above $Q_{\rm ext}$; for $ε\geε_{\rm b}$ only the optical geometry does so in $Q_{\rm nc}<Q<Q_{\rm m}^{\rm ph}$. Non-radial geodesics are blocked from the singularity and can form metastable bound states, while radial geodesics either reach $r=0$ or escape to infinity. The critical impact parameter encodes both gravitational and optical-geometry effects, probing the distinct phase structures.

</details>

TL;DR: This paper constructs a local, generally covariant effective field theory—based on the cuscuton model with an angular variable θ identified as the LQC polymerization angle 2λb—that exactly reproduces the standard flat-FLRW Loop Quantum Cosmology background bounce dynamics (H² ∝ ρ(1−ρ/ρ_c)) without extra dynamical degrees of freedom.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a closed-form, local, and generally covariant effective description of the standard LQC cosmological bounce, bridging quantum-corrected background dynamics with classical scalar-tensor or modified gravity frameworks, while avoiding unphysical degrees of freedom or non-locality.

While it matches the homogeneous background exactly and respects the null energy condition, it does not imply equivalence at the level of inhomogeneous perturbations or full quantum gravity derivation.

Abstract: Loop Quantum Cosmology (LQC) replaces the big bang singularity of the homogeneous universe by a bounce, usually described by the modified Friedmann equation $H^2=ρ(1-ρ/ρ_c)/(3M_p^2)$. We show that this background dynamics follows from a local cuscuton effective theory, whose scalar equation is a constraint rather than a wave equation. One way to establish this is to write the cuscuton in terms of an angular coordinate $θ$, identified with the LQC polymerization angle $2λb$. Its constraint gives $H\propto\sinθ$, while the Einstein constraint gives $ρ=ρ_c\sin^2(θ/2)$, exactly reproducing the LQC bounce. To our knowledge, this is the first closed-form, local, generally covariant realization of the exact standard flat-FLRW LQC background dynamics for minimally coupled matter satisfying null energy condition, without introducing additional local dynamical degrees of freedom. A branchwise Legendre transformation establishes a canonical equivalence between the clock-gauge-fixed homogeneous mimetic system in the case of vanishing mimetic dust energy density and the cuscuton systems. It selects the constant-tension cuscuton coordinate $Θ=\sinθ-θ$, while $θ$ retains its interpretation as the LQC polymerization angle. This equivalence does not establish agreement of the inhomogeneous theories or their perturbations. The angular action also admits an explicit branched $f(K)$ representation, where $K$ is the mean extrinsic curvature of spatial hypersurfaces. The construction is therefore an effective covariant representation of the LQC holonomy correction, not a derivation from full loop quantum gravity.

</details>


### [40] [Kasner critical scaling in Einstein--Maxwell--scalar black holes with nonlinear collapse and scalarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35500)
*Chihang He,Chao Liu*

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the conjectured universality of the 1/2 critical exponent for the Kasner parameter in scalarized black holes by investigating whether alternative scaling behaviors can arise from controlled modifications of the scalar coupling.

Result: Demonstrated existence of critical exponents 1/(2m) for all integers m ≥ 1; derived exact scaling law β_K ∝ |q/q⋆ − 1|^(−1/(2m)) with explicit analytical prefactor; recovered previously observed 1/2 exponent as special case (m = 1), and established hierarchy including 1/4 (m = 2) and 1/6 (m = 3).

Conclusion: The 1/2 critical exponent is not universal; the scaling behavior of the Kasner parameter is tunable via higher-order structure of the scalar coupling, revealing a discrete family of critical exponents governed by integer m, thereby generalizing and refining the critical phenomenology of scalarized black holes.

Abstract: A critical scaling exponent of $1/2$ was numerically observed for the Kasner parameter of asymptotically flat scalarized black holes and conjectured to be universal [Phys.\ Rev.\ Lett.\ \textbf{136}, 251402 (2026)]. In the same static, spherically symmetric Einstein--Maxwell--scalar setting, we show that this exponent is not universal. We develop a rigorous mathematical framework and construct couplings realizing exponents $1/(2m)$ for any integer $m\ge1$ by tuning higher-order terms at fixed quadratic coupling parameter $μ$. Nonlinear interior collapse produces a spacelike Kasner singularity with $β_{\rm K}\propto\varepsilon^{-1}$, where $\varepsilon$ is the value of scalar field at the event horizon. An exterior branch satisfying $q/q_\star-1\propto\varepsilon^{2m}$, therefore, yields $β_{\rm K}\propto|q/q_\star-1|^{-1/(2m)}$, where $q$ is the charge-to-mass ratio and $q_\star$ its critical value. We also determine the prefactor explicitly. This recovers the $1/2$ law and establishes a hierarchy including $1/4$ and $1/6$.

</details>


### [41] [From Density to Mass: A New Parametrized Framework for Dark Matter Environment Around Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35771)
*Rajes Ghosh,Nicholas Speeney,Avijit Chowdhury,Chiranjeeb Singha,Miguel A. S. Pinto,Emanuele Berti*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a mass-based framework to model dark matter-dressed black holes, enabling systematic study of environmental effects on quasinormal modes, tidal Love numbers, and EMRI gravitational-wave fluxes—revealing complementary spatial sensitivities across observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Astrophysical black holes reside in complex environments (e.g., DM halos, baryonic matter), which perturb spacetime geometry and leave imprints on gravitational-wave observables; existing density-based halo modeling lacks direct connection to measurable gravitational fields and physical consistency constraints.

Method: Develops a general spacetime framework parameterized by the enclosed mass function M(r), enforcing regularity, causality, and correct asymptotics; derives modifications to QNMs, static TLNs, and EMRI GW fluxes within this geometry; compares sensitivity regions across observables.

Result: QNMs are most sensitive to inner halo structure (r ≲ 5M), static TLNs to outer structure (r ≳ 10M), and EMRI fluxes encode integrated effects from orbital dynamics and wave propagation through the full matter distribution.

Conclusion: The enclosed-mass formulation provides a flexible, physically grounded, and observationally relevant framework for linking astrophysical environment models to precision gravitational-wave tests of BH environments.

Abstract: Astrophysical black holes (BHs) are not isolated vacuum objects but are expected to reside within complex environments, such as dark matter (DM) halos and baryonic distributions. Such environments can modify the spacetime geometry surrounding the BH and leave observable imprints on gravitational-wave (GW) signals, including shifts in the quasinormal mode (QNM) spectrum, tidal Love numbers (TLNs), and the inspiral dynamics of extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs). We develop a general framework for describing DM-dressed BH spacetimes by parameterizing the geometry directly in terms of the enclosed mass function, rather than the traditional density-based profile. This mass-based formulation is naturally connected to the total gravitational field, accommodates several common halo profiles as limiting cases, and incorporates basic physical consistency requirements such as regularity, causality, and appropriate asymptotic behavior. Within this framework, we investigate the environmental imprints on QNMs, TLNs, and GW fluxes from EMRIs. We find a complementary sensitivity to the halo structure: QNMs predominantly probe the inner regions of the environment, whereas static TLNs are more sensitive to its outer structure. EMRI fluxes, in contrast, probe the matter distribution through its influence on the orbital motion and GW propagation. Our results establish the enclosed-mass formulation as a flexible and physically controlled framework for connecting astrophysical environment models with precision GW observables.

</details>


### [42] [Analytic detector responses and entanglement in constant-curvature spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29992)
*Dyuman Bhattacharya,Jiayue Yang,Ming Zhang,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives analytic response functions for Unruh–DeWitt detectors coupled to massless, conformally coupled scalar fields in various spacetimes (Minkowski, AdS, dS, BTZ), isolating effects of field spectrum, geometry, and switching protocol; it characterizes excitation probabilities, coherences, entanglement conditions (e.g., KMS-constrained optimal gap in dS), and near-singularity behavior in BTZ via Legendre series and image sums.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle contributions from field spectrum, global spacetime geometry, and interaction protocol (e.g., switching function) in detector responses—crucial for interpreting quantum field effects in curved spacetimes and relativistic quantum information.

Method: Analytic derivation of detector response functions using Gaussian and sharp switching protocols; application of time-ordered perturbation theory; use of conformal invariance and image-sum techniques (especially in BTZ); Legendre series expansion for infalling detectors; numerical validation of analytic results.

Result: Closed-form leading-order excitation probabilities and two-detector coherences in D ≥ 3 Minkowski, AdS, and dS; identification of time-ordered vs. coincident coherence regimes; KMS-determined optimal energy gap for maximal nonlocal coherence/entanglement ratio in dS; concurrence peak at smaller positive gap; logarithmic divergence coefficient near BTZ singularity determined analytically via covering-AdS image sum; smooth switching removes onset artifacts but preserves leading-log coefficient.

Conclusion: Detector response encodes separable geometric, spectral, and protocol-dependent features; analytic control enables precise tests of entanglement generation, thermal behavior (KMS), and singularity physics; results validate robustness of leading-order perturbative QFT in curved spacetime for relativistic quantum information tasks.

Abstract: We derive analytic response functions for Unruh--DeWitt detectors coupled to real, massless, conformally coupled scalar fields, to distinguish the effects of the field spectrum and global geometry from those of the interaction protocol. For Gaussian-switched static pairs in Minkowski, anti-de Sitter, and de Sitter spacetimes, we obtain the leading-order excitation probabilities and two-detector coherences in spacetime dimension $\mathcal D=d+1\ge3$. Time-ordered coherence requires distinct spatial worldlines; the local response and single-excitation coherence also admit coincidence. For equal-redshift pairs, Gaussian switching separates the gap dependence of the time-ordered coherence. In de Sitter space, KMS detailed balance fixes the gap that maximizes the ratio of nonlocal coherence to local excitation, giving a single-gap test for the existence of leading-order entanglement and restricting any entangling gaps to one bounded interval. When entanglement is present, the concurrence itself peaks at a smaller positive gap. In the three-dimensional BTZ black hole, an integrated Legendre series gives the sharply switched response of a radially infalling detector. A uniform image-sum estimate establishes logarithmic growth near the singularity and determines its coefficient from the covering-AdS response. A sufficiently smooth monotone onset removes the finite-time onset glitches but leaves a positive logarithmic coefficient; the accumulated leading-order response remains finite. Independent numerical calculations test the analytic expressions, their convergence properties, and these physical distinctions.

</details>

### [43] [Cardy random matrix theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31825)
*Marco Ambrosini,Alexandre Belin,Jan de Boer,Julian Sonner*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces a Cardy random matrix model constrained solely by S-modular invariance, regularized via coarse-graining over fixed-energy windows; it reproduces the Cardy density of states macroscopically and GUE-level statistics microscopically, with emergent scale hierarchy and an effective Thouless time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a minimal random matrix model encoding only S-modular invariance—key to 2D CFTs and holographic duals of 3D quantum gravity—without ad hoc assumptions, bridging statistical mechanics of spectra and conformal bootstrap constraints.

Method: Imposes coarse-grained S-modular invariance as a constraint on the eigenvalue density; derives an effective single-matrix potential whose ground state yields the Cardy asymptotics; performs Monte Carlo simulations; identifies scaling limits separating macroscopic (window-scale), mesoscopic (within-window), and microscopic (level-spacing) regimes.

Result: The coarse-grained spectral density matches the Cardy formula above a black-hole threshold gap; eigenvalue statistics within windows obey Gaussian Unitary Ensemble (GUE) universality; a clear scale hierarchy emerges, with an effective Thouless time inversely proportional to the coarse-graining window width.

Conclusion: The Cardy random matrix model realizes modular invariance as a statistical constraint that simultaneously enforces universal thermodynamic behavior (Cardy) and quantum chaotic level statistics (GUE), offering a computationally tractable framework for exploring the matrix/tensor model dual of 3D quantum gravity.

Abstract: We construct and study a ``Cardy random matrix model'': a random matrix theory whose only input, in a maximal-ignorance prescription, is the constraint of $\mathbb{S}$-modular invariance. We regularize this constraint by coarse-graining over energy windows of fixed width, and obtain a potential for a single random matrix whose eigenvalues play the role of the scaling dimensions of primaries in a 2d CFT. The potential drives the coarse-grained density of states to the Cardy profile above a gap at the black hole threshold. We perform Monte Carlo simulations to confirm this behavior and find good agreement for the coarse-grained density of states, while at the same time revealing GUE statistics for nearby eigenvalues. We identify the scaling limit in which these phenomena emerge. In this limit, the model realizes a hierarchy of scales: at the macroscopic scale given by the window size, the occupations are frozen and the bootstrap constraint rigidly fixes the spectrum to the Cardy profile, while at the mesoscopic scale (inside a window) the Vandermonde repulsion rearranges the eigenvalues as in a random matrix theory. It is only at the microscopic scale of the mean level spacing that the individual levels are resolved. Accordingly, our random matrix model displays an effective Thouless time, set by the inverse energy width of the coarse-graining window. Our model provides a first step towards solving the matrix/tensor model dual to 3D quantum gravity on a computer.

</details>


### [44] [Localizing the M-Theory Path Integral](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31829)
*Pietro Benetti Genolini,Florian Gaar,Jerome P. Gauntlett,Jaeha Park,James Sparks*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives a supersymmetric localization formula for the M-theory path integral's perturbative part, yielding closed-form analytic expressions for ABJM and $V^{5,2}$ partition functions—including previously unknown Airy constants—and connects them to black hole entropy, topological strings, and integrated correlators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain exact, closed-form analytic results for the perturbative M-theory path integral in various 3d SCFTs (e.g., ABJM, $V^{5,2}$), especially where field-theoretic methods fail or yield only numerical data, and to unify descriptions of black hole microstates, topological strings, and supersymmetric observables.

Method: Supersymmetric localization in eleven-dimensional supergravity with equivariant flux background; construction of relative equivariant Chern–Simons couplings for the localized action; use of tangent-cotangent character for M2-brane one-loop determinant; application to squashed $S^3$, topologically twisted $S^2 \times S^1$, and $V^{5,2}$ geometries.

Result: First closed-form expression for full perturbative ABJM partition function at general squashing and chemical potentials; new analytic formula for ABJM topologically twisted index (black hole entropy); closed analytic Airy constant for non-toric $V^{5,2}$ theory; all-genus Type IIA expansion matching equivariant topological string constant map; extraction of integrated correlators.

Conclusion: The proposed localization framework unifies and extends known results across M-theory, AdS/CFT, topological strings, and black hole physics, providing exact analytic predictions beyond current field-theoretic reach and establishing a robust computational tool for quantum gravity observables in three dimensions.

Abstract: We propose a supersymmetric localization formula for the local perturbative contribution to the M-theory path integral, reducing it to an integral over equivariant flux data. Relative equivariant extensions of the eleven-dimensional Chern--Simons couplings determine the localized action, while for M2-brane backgrounds a tangent-cotangent character computes the one-loop determinant. This gives the first closed form expression for the complete perturbative ABJM partition function at general squashing and chemical potentials, and new analytic results for the ABJM topologically twisted index describing black holes. Applied to the non-toric $V^{5,2}$ theory, the same prescription yields a closed analytic Airy constant previously known only numerically. Our formulas reproduce all available independent field theory results, and provide analytic predictions at general parameters where no field theory answer is currently known. We derive an all-genus result for the Type IIA expansion which is an equivariant form of the usual topological string constant map result, and also extract integrated correlators.

</details>


### [45] [Nonlinear electroweak sphaleron clouds around Kerr black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31845)
*Carlos Herdeiro,Jutta Kunz,Hendrik Mennenga,Eugen Radu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs nonlinear electroweak sphaleron clouds around Schwarzschild and Kerr black holes in the probe limit, revealing a rich branch structure and finite mass gap—distinct from superradiant linear clouds—and provides a closed-form leading-order Higgs solution on an exact Yang-Mills configuration in Schwarzschild spacetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the interplay between two fundamental nonlinear solutions—Kerr black holes (GR) and electroweak sphalerons (Standard Model)—by studying how sphaleronic configurations behave in strong gravitational fields.

Method: Uses the probe limit to construct nonlinear sphaleron cloud solutions around Schwarzschild and Kerr black holes; revisits flat-spacetime static/spinning multi-sphalerons with physical electroweak parameters; analyzes domain of existence, branch structure, and physical properties; derives a closed-form leading-order Higgs solution on an exact Yang-Mills background in Schwarzschild geometry.

Result: Sphaleron clouds exhibit a rich branch structure and retain a finite mass gap; they do not degenerate to linear clouds, unlike superradiant cases; a closed-form analytic Higgs solution is obtained on an exact Yang-Mills configuration in Schwarzschild spacetime.

Conclusion: Nonlinear sphaleron–black hole configurations form stable, nontrivial families distinct from superradiant clouds, offering a new class of hybrid GR–Standard Model solutions with potential implications for early-universe physics and black hole quantum structure.

Abstract: Kerr black holes and electroweak sphalerons are remarkable solutions of two pillars of modern physics: General Relativity and the Standard Model of particle physics, respectively. In this work, we investigate their interplay by constructing nonlinear sphaleron clouds around Schwarzschild and Kerr black holes in the probe limit. We first revisit static and spinning (multi-)sphalerons in flat spacetime, using the measured electroweak parameters. We then explore the domain of existence and physical properties of the corresponding black-hole configurations, revealing a rich branch structure. Remarkably, the sphaleron clouds retain a finite mass gap and do not approach linear clouds within the families studied, distinguishing them from the familiar clouds associated with the onset of superradiant instabilities. We also obtain a closed-form leading-order Higgs solution on an exact Yang-Mills configuration in a Schwarzschild background.

</details>


### [46] [Quantum vacuum interaction between semitransparent plates in a Pöschl-Teller background: an exact relative TGTG formula](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31866)
*Jose M. Muñoz-Castañeda,Inés Cavero-Peláez,Gonzalo Sancho-Garrido*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives an exact background-dressed TGTG formula for the one-loop quantum vacuum interaction between two semitransparent Dirac-delta plates embedded in a one-soliton Pöschl-Teller background, defining the interaction via a relative determinant that subtracts isolated plate contributions; it shows position- and separation-dependent energy, absence of IR divergence from the zero mode for repulsive plates, and extends results to planar geometries and position-dependent forces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously compute quantum vacuum interactions between localized objects (Dirac-delta plates) in a nontrivial solitonic background—beyond flat-space approximations—while properly handling infrared issues and background dependence.

Method: Exact spectral analysis using relative zeta-function regularization; construction of a background-dressed TGTG (T-matrix, Green’s function, trace, and determinant) formalism; subtraction of isolated one-plate contributions within the same solitonic background; extension to planar geometry and force computation via functional differentiation.

Result: An explicit, exact expression for the position- and separation-dependent one-loop interaction energy; demonstration that the normalizable zero mode does not cause infrared divergence in the relative energy for repulsive plates; derivation of position-dependent Casimir forces for planar configurations.

Conclusion: The background-dressed TGTG framework enables consistent, divergence-free computation of quantum vacuum interactions in solitonic backgrounds; geometric and background effects are inseparable, and position dependence is intrinsic—not merely due to boundary conditions but also to scattering data modulated by the soliton.

Abstract: We derive an exact background-dressed TGTG formula for the one-loop quantum vacuum inter- action between two semitransparent Dirac-delta plates in the one-soliton Pöchl-Teller background. The interaction is defined through a relative determinant in which the two isolated one-plate contributions are subtracted in the same background. As a result, the energy depends on the individual plate positions as well as on their separation. For repulsive plates, the normalizable zero mode does not induce an infrared divergence in the relative interaction energy. We also extend the construction to planar plates and analyze the corresponding position-dependent interaction forces.

</details>


### [47] [Spread Complexity for Local Operator Quenches with Conserved Momentum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31867)
*Ali Fatemiabhari*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies locally excited states in 2D CFTs on various geometries, constructs them with chiral/anti-chiral regulators to carry net momentum, computes Lanczos coefficients and spread complexity analytically (early time) and numerically (late time), and finds a mismatch between the growth rate of spread complexity and its proposed holographic dual (proper momentum of infalling bulk particle).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand quantum complexity growth in excited states of 2D CFTs and test holographic proposals linking spread complexity to bulk kinematic quantities (e.g., infalling particle momentum).

Abstract: We study a family of locally excited states in two-dimensional conformal field theories in different setups, defined on the plane, on the cylinder, or at finite temperature. These states are constructed by assigning independent chiral and anti-chiral regulators to a local primary operator, leading to excitations carrying net momentum. We analytically calculate the first few Lanczos coefficients and the early time behaviour of the spread complexity associated with these states. Then, using numerical methods, we obtain the coefficients reaching larger Krylov indices and find the associated spread complexity for a longer range of time evolution. We also compare the rate of change of the resulting spread complexity with the proposed holographic dual quantity, related to the proper momentum of an infalling particle in the dual bulk, but we observe a mismatch.

</details>


### [48] [Transient Coherence and Multiscale Spectral Structure after a Mass Quench in a 1+1-Dimensional Scalar Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31884)
*Gabriel Abellán,Nelson Bolívar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a bilocal observable based on the Keldysh correlator smeared with a Morlet wavelet to quantify anomalous pair correlations after a mass quench in a 1+1D free scalar field; it shows thermal independence of the relative coherence weight, analyzes finite-time window effects, compares with Wigner spectrum, derives positivity bounds, and extends results to Hartree dynamics in de Sitter spacetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize non-thermal, anomalous pair correlations generated by a mass quench—distinct from final particle occupations—and to develop a robust, renormalizable, and observation-window-aware measure of quantum coherence that is insensitive to initial thermal amplitude.

Method: Defines a bilocal observable via Morlet-wavelet smearing of the statistical Keldysh correlator over finite relative time; computes excess over dephased (incoherent) limit to obtain dimensionless relative coherence weight; analytically derives its thermal independence for Gaussian initial states; analyzes finite-window response, wavelet annihilation properties, and connection to signed Wigner spectrum; establishes Gaussian-extension positivity bound; benchmarks against exact smooth-quench solutions; formulates renormalized Hartree extension for interacting scalar in 2D de Sitter.

Result: The relative coherence weight is exactly independent of initial temperature for Gaussian thermal states; the anomalous signal is intrinsically window-dependent; zero-mean wavelets annihilate the constant-in-relative-time anomalous term on infinite time domain; signed Wigner spectrum differs qualitatively; a strict Gaussian-extension positivity bound is derived; exact smooth-quench solutions confirm the observable's dynamical fidelity; Hartree extension reveals oscillatory vs. nonoscillatory regimes for scalar modes in de Sitter.

Conclusion: Anomalous pair correlations are quantifiably distinct from occupation numbers and require finite-window, bilocal observables for unambiguous detection; thermal robustness arises from amplitude cancellation—not purity—and generalizes beyond wavelets; the framework enables renormalized, background-coupled diagnostics of quantum coherence in cosmological and condensed-matter quenches.

Abstract: A mass quench generates anomalous pair correlations that are absent from the final particle occupations. For a free real scalar field in $1+1$ dimensions, we define a bilocal observable by smearing the statistical Keldysh correlator with a Morlet kernel on the finite post-quench relative-time interval. Its excess over the dephased correlator gives a dimensionless relative coherence weight. At fixed momentum, this weight is exactly independent of the initial temperature for Gaussian thermal states evolved with the same free Hamiltonian: the common thermal amplitude cancels between numerator and denominator. The cancellation is not a purity criterion and is not exclusive to wavelets. We derive the finite-window response and show that the anomalous signal depends essentially on the observation window; an admissible zero-mean wavelet annihilates the relative-time-constant anomalous term on the full line. We compare this response with the signed finite-time Wigner spectrum and derive a distinct Gaussian-extension positivity bound. Exact smooth-quench occupations provide an independent dynamical reference. Finally, we formulate a renormalized Hartree extension and delimit the oscillatory and nonoscillatory regimes of a scalar mode on a fixed two-dimensional de Sitter background.

</details>


### [49] [Cylinder Defect Casimir Energy and Weyl Anomalies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34529)
*Yang Zhou*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper identifies two distinct Casimir observables for conformal surface defects—circular and great cylinders—and relates their Casimir energies to displacement and local conformal anomalies, respectively, with verification from free-field and holographic methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the role of conformal anomalies in Casimir energy for surface defects by distinguishing between geometric embeddings (circular vs. great cylinder) and linking each to a specific anomaly type (displacement vs. local).

Method: Analytic computation of Casimir energy for two conformal defect embeddings—circular cylinder in flat space and great cylinder via radial quantization—combined with consistency checks using free-field theory and holography.

Abstract: We distinguish two Casimir observables associated with a conformal surface defect. A circular cylinder embedded in flat space has a logarithmic contribution to its Casimir energy, $-d_1\log(R/ε)/(24R)$, fixed by the displacement anomaly. A great cylinder obtained by radial quantization of the ambient theory instead has Casimir energy $-b/(12R)$ in the conformal defect vacuum with a fixed local anomaly convention. Free-field and holographic calculations provide checks of the proposed relations in both embeddings.

</details>


### [50] [General Relativity with Finite Boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31928)
*Chawakorn Maneerat*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This thesis studies general relativity on manifolds with a finite timelike boundary under conformal boundary conditions, analyzing linearized Einstein equations in Lorentzian signature (about Minkowski, dS, AdS) and horizon thermodynamics in Euclidean signature via the gravitational path integral in a conformal canonical ensemble.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand gravitational dynamics and thermodynamics on spacetimes with finite timelike boundaries—relevant for holography, quantum gravity, and black hole/cosmological horizon physics—under consistent, gauge-invariant conformal boundary conditions.

Method: Uses conformal boundary conditions (fixed conformal class of induced metric and trace K); applies Kodama-Ishibashi formalism adapted to bounded manifolds for linearized analysis in Lorentzian signature; computes semiclassical gravitational path integral with fixed conformal temperature and K in Euclidean signature to derive thermodynamic quantities.

Result: Provides well-posed linearized dynamics for all Λ (including Minkowski, dS, AdS) with timelike boundaries; derives horizon thermodynamics—including entropy and free energy—for black hole and cosmological horizons enclosed by the boundary within a conformal canonical ensemble.

Conclusion: Conformal boundary conditions yield a consistent, diffeomorphism-invariant framework for studying gravity on manifolds with finite timelike boundaries, unifying treatment across signatures and cosmological constants, with implications for holography and horizon statistical mechanics.

Abstract: In this thesis, we consider general relativity on a manifold with a timelike boundary of finite size, across all signs of the cosmological constant $Λ$. We impose conformal boundary conditions, whereby the conformal class of the induced metric and the trace of the extrinsic curvature $K$ are fixed at the boundary. In Lorentzian signature, we analyse the linearised Einstein equations about Minkowski, de Sitter (dS), and anti-de Sitter (AdS) space for a variety of boundaries, employing the Kodama-Ishibashi formalism adapted to manifolds with a boundary. In Euclidean signature, we study the thermodynamics of black hole and cosmological horizons enclosed by the boundary, computing the leading semiclassical approximation of the gravitational path integral, for which the boundary data define a conformal canonical ensemble of fixed conformal temperature and $K$.

</details>


### [51] [Wormhole fragmentation and the axion Weak Gravity Conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32063)
*Puxin Lin,Gary Shiu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives the axion Weak Gravity Conjecture (aWGC) from nonperturbative Euclidean wormhole fragmentation into charged defects, fixes the action-to-charge bound and its order-one coefficient, relates it to the Imaginary Distance Bound (IDB), generalizes it to multiple axio-dilaton pairs with geometric interpretations, and argues that aWGC-satisfying axions destabilize wormholes—mitigating factorization issues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve factorization problems in quantum gravity by establishing a robust, nonperturbatively motivated form of the axion Weak Gravity Conjecture (aWGC) rooted in Euclidean wormhole physics.

Method: Analyzes nonperturbative fragmentation of Euclidean wormholes into charged topological defects; imposes action minimization to constrain the axion action-to-charge ratio; extends analysis to multi-axion and axio-dilaton systems using geometric formulations.

Result: Derives the aWGC with a precise order-one coefficient determined by fragmentation dynamics; shows coefficient matches IDB without extra scalars but strengthens with them; provides geometric representation for multi-axion cases; demonstrates wormhole instability under aWGC.

Conclusion: The aWGC emerges as a necessary condition for Euclidean wormhole consistency in quantum gravity; its validity suppresses wormhole proliferation and resolves factorization paradoxes.

Abstract: We derive the axion Weak Gravity Conjecture (aWGC) from the nonperturbative fragmentation of Euclidean wormholes into charged defects. Requiring fragmentation to lower the Euclidean action fixes the axion action-to-charge bound and its order-one coefficient. Without additional scalars, this coefficient coincides with that of the Imaginary Distance Bound (IDB), while additional scalars strengthen the bound. The aWGC in the presence of multiple axio-dilaton pairs is discussed, leading to geometric representations. Axions satisfying the aWGC render the corresponding wormholes unstable, potentially suppressing their proliferation and resolving associated factorization problems.

</details>


### [52] [A backreacted bouncing geodesic in 2D](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32071)
*Gauri Batra,Adam Levine,Stephen H. Shenker*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies quantum corrections to finite-temperature correlation functions in 2D dilaton gravity holography, showing that gravitational backreaction smoothes out geodesic singularities associated with black hole singularities; instead, singularity physics is encoded via a Stokes phenomenon near t=0, with structural parallels to forbidden singularities in large-c 2D CFT blocks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum corrections—beyond classical geodesic approximations—affect the holographic encoding of black hole singularity physics in analytically continued thermal correlators.

Method: Analyzes gravitational backreaction of null worldlines onto the black hole geometry in 2D dilaton gravity; employs steepest descent analysis of the backreacted geodesic saddle; identifies and interprets Stokes phenomena near the coincident-point singularity at t=0 and its thermal image.

Result: Backreaction smooths out the bouncing-geodesic singularities in G(t); singularity information is instead encoded non-analytically via a Stokes phenomenon localized near t=0 (and t=iβ); the pattern of Stokes lines mirrors that seen in forbidden singularities of large-c Virasoro conformal blocks.

Conclusion: Quantum gravitational effects—specifically worldline backreaction—alter the analytic structure of thermal correlators qualitatively: singularity signatures shift from explicit poles/branch points at nonzero t to non-perturbative Stokes phenomena at t=0, suggesting deeper connections between bulk singularity resolution and CFT analyticity constraints.

Abstract: In holography, analytically continued finite temperature correlation functions $G(t)$ exhibit signatures of the black hole singularity. These take the form of singularities at certain values of $t$ whose bulk origin is a null geodesic worldline "bouncing off" the black hole singularity. We study certain quantum corrections to this effect in 2D dilaton gravity that are captured by gravitational backreaction of the worldline onto the black hole geometry. These corrections smooth out the bouncing geodesic singularities. By following the backreacted geodesic saddle in a steepest descent analysis we find instead that the black hole singularity physics gets encoded in a subtle way involving a Stokes phenomenon near the coincident point singularity of $G(t)$ at $t=0$ (and its thermal image). We point out a parallel between the structure of Stokes lines uncovered in this analysis and the structure present in the analysis of forbidden singularities in large $c$ 2D conformal blocks. Finally we make some preliminary remarks about the significance of quantum corrections in these models beyond backreaction.

</details>


### [53] [Learning Scattering Amplitudes with Transformer Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32096)
*Philip Velie,Tianji Cai,Piyush Jha,Vijay Ganesh,Aishik Ghosh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces a transformer-based RL algorithm integrating known symmetries and linear relations to efficiently learn high-loop scattering amplitudes in planar N=4 SYM, drastically reducing required prior knowledge and ensuring physical consistency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome factorial growth in state space at higher loop orders and enable generalization of ML methods to high-loop amplitudes while respecting exact physical constraints (symmetries, linear relations).
