Method: Combines transformer architecture with Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS); uses symbology representation to map discrete sequences to integer coefficients; enforces known linear relations via coefficient propagation, falling back to MCTS when propagation is insufficient.

Result: Significantly reduced fraction of a priori known solution needed; all outputs strictly satisfy known physical relationships; improved performance over pure transformer baselines on high-loop amplitude reconstruction.

Conclusion: Incorporating domain-specific structure—especially exact linear constraints and symmetries—into transformer-RL frameworks is essential for scalable, physically consistent ML for high-loop quantum field theory calculations.

Abstract: We introduce a transformer reinforcement learning algorithm that learns to solve high loop-level scattering amplitudes in planar N = 4 Super Yang-Mills theory. Our algorithm improves on previous transformer-only results by incorporating previously derived symmetries and relationships into the learning algorithm. This results in a greatly decreased fraction of the solution that needs to be known a priori to solve the entire problem. An additional benefit is that our algorithm also ensures that every output obeys the set of known relationships. Rather than predicting all coefficients independently, the model proposes assignments that are propagated through exact linear relations, while MCTS searches over assignments when propagation alone is insufficient. This is crucial to the generalization of machine learning approaches to higher loops, as without this, there is no way to overcome the factorially scaling of state sizes and compare to results derived via other methods. Using the symbology representation of the form factor, we frame the problem as learning a mapping between discrete sequences and integer coefficients.

</details>


### [54] [Hairy Taub-NUT solution in gauged supergravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32139)
*Masato Nozawa*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs exact Taub-NUT-AdS black hole/horizon solutions with scalar hair in 4D N=2 gauged supergravity, where the geometry is supported only by a scalar field and its potential—no gauge fields. It analyzes horizon existence (at most one, always nondegenerate), proves absence of Killing spinors (i.e., no supersymmetry), addresses CTC pathologies, computes mass covariantly, formulates a NUT-fixed first law, and studies Euclidean regularity (nuts/bolts).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and systematically analyze fully backreacted, hairy generalizations of Taub-NUT-AdS spacetimes in a controlled supergravity setting—specifically probing how scalar hair modifies horizon structure, supersymmetry, thermodynamics, and Euclidean regularity compared to vacuum NUT solutions.

Method: Analytic construction of exact solutions to the equations of motion of 4D N=2 gauged supergravity with vanishing gauge fields; systematic analysis of Killing horizons via metric and scalar field equations; computation of Killing spinors to assess supersymmetry; evaluation of conserved charges via the covariant phase space formalism; derivation of thermodynamic identities; and detailed study of Euclidean signature metrics including regularity conditions for nuts and bolts.

Result: Exact scalar-hairy Taub-NUT-AdS solutions are obtained; horizon existence is characterized (unique, nondegenerate); supersymmetry is ruled out (no Killing spinors); CTCs persist; mass is computed covariantly; a restricted first law holds with fixed NUT charge; Euclidean regularity yields novel bolt/nut families, including an exceptional Euclidean-only case.

Conclusion: Scalar hair in Taub-NUT-AdS supergravity enriches the solution space without restoring supersymmetry or eliminating CTCs, introduces new horizon branches inaccessible in the static limit, and modifies thermodynamic structure—highlighting robustness of NUT-related pathologies and the necessity of careful regularization in the Euclidean sector.

Abstract: We present and study a family of exact Taub-NUT-AdS solutions with scalar hair in four-dimensional ${\cal N}=2$ gauged supergravity. The solutions are supported solely by a nontrivial scalar field and its self-interaction potential, with all gauge fields switched off. We determine the conditions under which Killing horizons exist and demonstrate that the spacetime admits at most one Killing horizon, and whenever it exists it is necessarily nondegenerate. In particular, the NUT deformation can give rise to a horizon in a branch whose static limit is horizonless. We also establish that the solutions with nontrivial scalar hair admit no Killing spinors. As in ordinary Taub-NUT-AdS spacetime, the global structure is afflicted by closed timelike curves. We compute the conserved mass using the covariant phase space method and formulate a restricted first law with the NUT parameter held fixed. Finally, we study the Euclidean sector, including an exceptional family specific to Euclidean signature, and determine the regularity conditions for bolts and nuts.

</details>


### [55] [Higher-Form Description of Chiral Soliton Lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32212)
*Yutaka Ookouchi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the chiral soliton lattice (CSL) using a higher-form gauge theory with Stückelberg-coupled two-form and three-form fields, enabling direct treatment of strings and domain walls as extended sources; it reproduces exact Sine–Gordon results—including lattice spacing, wall interactions, energy minimization, and gapless phonons—without fitting parameters, reinterpreting scalar winding as branch flow in a nonlinear multi-branch framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometrically transparent, parameter-free description of the chiral soliton lattice (CSL) that treats topological defects (strings and domain walls) as fundamental extended objects, going beyond conventional compact scalar field models and reconciling effective field theory with exact integrable results.

Method: Constructs a higher-form gauge theory with a Stückelberg coupling between a two-form gauge field C₂ and a three-form gauge field A₃; derives quadratic dual effective theory to compute wall–wall Yukawa interaction; extends to nonlinear multi-branch higher-form theory to capture exact dilute limit, periodic CSL configuration, energy minimization, and phonon dynamics.

Result: Exact agreement with Sine–Gordon prediction for CSL lattice spacing; correct repulsive Yukawa interaction coefficient between parallel domain walls fixed by duality; reproduction of dilute-wall interaction, periodic CSL solution, energy-minimization condition, and gapless phonon mode; identification of phonon as collective displacement of higher-form background and scalar winding as branch flow.

Conclusion: The higher-form framework provides a unified, geometric, and predictive description of the CSL, elevating extended objects (strings/walls) to fundamental dynamical entities and revealing that scalar-field winding emerges from nonlinear branch structure of the higher-form theory.

Abstract: The chiral soliton lattice (CSL) is conventionally described as a periodic winding configuration of a compact scalar field. In this paper, we formulate the CSL in a higher-form framework based on a Stückelberg coupling between a two-form gauge field $C_2$ and a three-form gauge field $A_3$, in which strings and walls can be treated directly as extended sources. Higher-form gauge invariance requires the string worldsheet to be the boundary of the wall worldvolume. In the quadratic dual effective theory, exchange of the massive higher-form mode generates a repulsive Yukawa interaction between parallel walls. With its coefficient fixed by duality, the resulting CSL lattice spacing agrees well with the exact Sine--Gordon result without parameter fitting. We then extend the description to a nonlinear, multi-branch higher-form theory. It reproduces the exact dilute wall--wall interaction coefficient, the periodic CSL and its energy-minimization condition, as well as the gapless CSL phonon. The phonon is identified as a collective displacement of the periodic higher-form background, while scalar winding is reinterpreted as branch flow of the nonlinear higher-form theory.

</details>


### [56] [Rational Probes of Spectral Geometry in Hermitian Matrix Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32497)
*Ali Nassar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a finite-plane bootstrap method using Cauchy kernel probes and Pick matrix positivity to constrain low moments in planar Hermitian matrix models, revealing how dressed saddles (nonbranching double zeros of the spectral discriminant) geometrically structure the moment space via conormal constraints tied to filling fractions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguity in low moments left undetermined by planar loop equations in Hermitian one-matrix models, and to geometrically characterize how a fixed multicut topology embeds in moment space—beyond Hankel positivity—by leveraging local analytic and positivity constraints.

Method: Introduces spectral probes $f_n(x) = (z_n - x)^{-1}$, constructs their Gram matrix—the Pick matrix $P_{mn}$—and enforces its positive-semidefiniteness as a finite-node bootstrap; uses dressed saddles (real nonbranching double zeros of the spectral discriminant) to derive transverse constraints on the moment-space locus; interprets $P(z,z)$ as a spectral response and $\nabla_{\mathbf{m}} P(z,z)$ as moment-space susceptibility, analyzing its $\eta^{-2}$ enhancement near real dressed saddles.

Result: Derives geometric characterization of the moment-space locus for regular multicut families: dressed saddles define conormal directions orthogonal to the filling-fraction manifold; susceptibility enhancements reconstruct conormal and tangent spaces; recovers known planar results (quartic/sextic models) analytically via Pick positivity + reality + analyticity; exposes explicit 1D filling-fraction geometry (asymmetric quartic) and independent conormal directions (sextic).

Conclusion: Local consistency conditions—encoded in Pick positivity, resolvent analyticity, and reality—geometrically constrain low moments prior to global period matching or variational equilibrium selection; the finite-plane bootstrap provides a refined, probe-based framework linking spectral singularities (dressed saddles) to the differential geometry of the filling-fraction moduli space.

Abstract: The planar loop equations of a Hermitian one-matrix model leave finitely many low moments undetermined. Hankel positivity constrains these moments but does not reveal how a fixed multicut family is embedded in moment space. We introduce a finite-plane diagnostic using the Cauchy kernels $f_n(x)=(z_n-x)^{-1}$ which we use as spectral probes. Their Gram matrix is the positive-semidefinite Pick matrix $P_{mn}$, whose positivity defines a finite-node bootstrap analogous to the Hankel bootstrap.
  For a fixed regular multicut topology, the nonbranching double zeros of the spectral discriminant, which we call \emph{dressed saddles}, impose transverse constraints on the corresponding moment-space locus, while its remaining directions are filling fractions. Writing $z=E+iη$, $E$ selects a spectral region and $η>0$ sets a continuous resolution scale. We use $P(z,z)$ as a finite-plane spectral response and interpret $\nabla_{\mathbf m}P(z,z)$ as a moment-space susceptibility. Near a regular real dressed saddle, this susceptibility is enhanced as $η^{-2}$ and aligns with a conormal to the filling-fraction manifold, whereas the leading enhancement cancels along tangent deformations. Responses near several independent saddles can therefore reconstruct the conormal space and, through their common kernel, its tangent space.
  Combining Pick positivity with reality and analyticity of the resolvent, we obtain alternative analytic derivations of known planar results in quartic and sextic models. The asymmetric quartic makes the one-dimensional filling-fraction geometry explicit, while sextic models exhibit independent conormal directions. These results clarify how local consistency conditions constrain the low moments before global period matching and the equilibrium variational inequality select the equilibrium measure.

</details>


### [57] [Greybody Factors and Hawking Spectra of Quantum-Corrected Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32702)
*Moslem Shafiee,Ahmad Sheykhi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies Hawking radiation from a quantum-corrected Schwarzschild black hole incorporating vacuum polarization and conformal anomaly effects, revealing a two-horizon geometry, non-monotonic temperature vanishing at critical mass, logarithmic entropy correction, modified greybody factors (suppressed at low energy, enhanced at high energy), dominance of scalar s=l=0 mode in late evaporation, and asymptotic approach to a stable extremal remnant with greatly extended lifetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum gravitational corrections—specifically vacuum polarization and conformal anomaly—affect black hole thermodynamics, Hawking radiation, and evaporation dynamics, particularly near the Planck scale where semi-classical approximations break down.

Method: Analytical computation of greybody factors, emission spectra, and particle emission rates for massless bosonic fields (spins 0, 1, 2) using the quantum-corrected Schwarzschild metric with two horizons; analysis of temperature evolution, entropy correction, and mode dominance across evaporation stages.

Result: Quantum corrections induce a non-monotonic Hawking temperature that vanishes at a critical mass, yield logarithmic entropy corrections, suppress low-energy greybody factors while enhancing high-energy ones, cause strong suppression of emission in late-stage evaporation, render higher-spin modes negligible, and lead to scalar s=l=0 mode dominance—culminating in asymptotic approach to a stable extremal remnant with dramatically increased lifetime.

Conclusion: Quantum corrections fundamentally alter black hole evaporation: they prevent complete evaporation, stabilize the black hole as an extremal remnant, and shift spectral and modal characteristics of Hawking radiation—supporting the possibility of Planck-scale remnants as endpoints of quantum black hole decay.

Abstract: We investigate Hawking radiation of a quantum-corrected Schwarzschild black hole with vacuum polarization and conformal anomaly effects. The corrected geometry has two horizons, a non-monotonic Hawking temperature that vanishes at a critical mass, and a logarithmic entropy correction. We compute greybody factors, spectra, and emission rates for massless bosonic fields of various spins and angular momenta. Quantum corrections suppress low-energy greybody factors and enhance high-energy ones. During late evaporation, where quantum influences become significant, the decreasing temperature strongly suppresses emission. In this case, the higher-spin modes become negligible, and the scalar $s=l=0$ mode dominates. The black hole lifetime is greatly extended as it asymptotically approaches a stable extremal remnant.

</details>


### [58] [Nonlinear pole topology of slow currents in holographic probe-brane metals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32748)
*Yan Han,Xiang-Qian Li*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the nonlinear (third-harmonic) electromagnetic response of holographic systems—specifically Dirac–Born–Infeld probe branes in Lifshitz black-brane backgrounds—with focus on pole structure classification, infrared scaling, and dynamical selection of response topology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve how low-frequency Drude response relates to conserved sectors in non-equilibrium transport, and to determine whether nonlinear response topologies (e.g., constitutive vs. relaxation vs. pair-frequency poles) are dictated by symmetry or dynamically selected in strongly coupled quantum matter.

Method: Classification of cubic retarded response functions by pole topology (incoming-, emitted-, and pair-frequency poles); holographic computation of third-harmonic conductivity using finite-density DBI probe branes in Lifshitz black-brane geometries with dynamical exponent z = 1, 3/2, 2; analytic derivation of nonlinear DC solution and scaling behavior.

Result: In z = 2 marginal Lifshitz theory, IR third-harmonic response is dominated by relaxation-type poles (p_IR ≈ 0.994), with a universal zero at ω_×/Γ_J ≈ 0.2918; z = 1 and z = 3/2 show mixed constitutive–relaxation weights, proving topology is dynamically selected; exact nonlinear DC solution yields T^{3/2}-scaling crossover field E_nl and parameter-free dense-limit scaling function.

Conclusion: Nonlinear response topology is not fixed by the DBI action’s square-root structure but emerges dynamically from IR physics; this bridges higher-form slow currents, nonlinear hydrodynamics, and holographic transport, and extends naturally to multi-mode slow sectors (e.g., energy or strain).

Abstract: Low-frequency Drude response does not by itself determine which nearly conserved sector carries an electric current. We classify the leading cubic retarded response of an isolated, inversion-symmetric slow vector by its pole topology. Constitutive nonlinearity produces only incoming-frequency poles, whereas nonlinear relaxation adds a propagator at the emitted frequency; coupling to a second scalar slow mode generates pair-frequency poles. We then compute the full complex third-harmonic response of finite-density Dirac--Born--Infeld probe branes in Lifshitz black-brane backgrounds. In the marginal $z=2$ theory, the dense infrared response approaches the relaxation topology, with a signed infrared weight $p_{\rm IR}=0.994\pm0.002$ and a normalization-independent zero at $ω_\times/Γ_J=0.2918$. Nonmarginal $z=1$ and $z=3/2$ backgrounds instead realize mixed constitutive--relaxation weights, demonstrating that the topology is selected dynamically rather than imposed by the DBI square root. The exact nonlinear dc solution further yields a parameter-free dense-limit scaling function and a crossover field $E_{\rm nl}\propto T^{3/2}$ at $z=2$. These results connect emergent higher-form slow currents, nonlinear response theory, and holographic transport, while the two-mode extension identifies the additional pole structures expected when energy or deformation modes remain slow.

</details>


### [59] [RG Limit Cycles in BKT Flows $\equiv$ Periodic Real-Time Dynamics in the Current-Current Perturbed $SU(2)_1$ WZW Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34248)
*Parameshwar R. Pasnoori*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes an exact correspondence between RG evolution and real-time quantum dynamics in the anisotropic current-current perturbed SU(2)₁ WZW model, showing that RG limit cycles map to periodic time evolution of couplings via quantum Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov equations and integrability constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize RG limit cycles as physical dynamical phenomena and bridge renormalization-group flow with real-time quantum dynamics in a controlled, integrable quantum field theory.

Method: Uses integrable fermionic representation of the SU(2)₁ WZW model with current-current perturbation; constructs exact time-dependent many-body wavefunction via generalized Bethe ansatz; derives quantum Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov equations under periodic boundary conditions; matches integrability constraints to BKT RG flow equations by identifying physical time with log(RG scale).

Result: Demonstrates exact equivalence between the Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless RG flow equations and the consistency conditions of quantum KZ equations; shows RG limit cycles correspond to periodic real-time modulation of interaction strengths; establishes a direct link among cyclic RG flows, quantum integrability, and periodically driven QFTs.

Conclusion: RG limit cycles are not merely formal features of coarse-graining but correspond to observable, periodic dynamical behavior in an interacting quantum field theory—providing the first exact dynamical realization of such cycles and unifying concepts from RG theory, integrability, and Floquet physics.

Abstract: We establish an exact correspondence between renormalization-group (RG) evolution and real-time quantum dynamics in the anisotropic current-current perturbed $SU(2)_1$ Wess-Zumino-Witten (WZW) model. Using its integrable fermionic representation, we construct the exact time-dependent many-body wavefunction by means of the generalized Bethe ansatz and show that periodic boundary conditions lead to quantum Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov equations associated with the XXZ trigonometric $R$-matrix, which corresponds to the quantum affine algebra $\mathcal{U}_q(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}_2})$ evaluated in its spin-$1/2$ evaluation representation. Consistency of these equations constrains the temporal evolution of the longitudinal and transverse interaction strengths. In the universal regime, these integrability conditions are exactly equivalent to the Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless RG flow equations upon identifying physical time with the logarithmic RG scale. Consequently, the RG limit cycles of the anisotropic current-current perturbed $SU(2)_1$ WZW model are realized as periodic real-time evolution of its interaction strengths. Our results provide an exact dynamical realization of RG limit cycles and establish a direct connection between cyclic RG flows, quantum integrability, and periodically driven interacting quantum field theories.

</details>


### [60] [3d Lovelock gravity and the holographic c-theorem: Proof at all orders and resummation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32866)
*Gokhan Alkac,Luis Guajardo,Hikmet Ozsahin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves that 3D Lovelock–Horndeski gravity admits a holographic c-theorem at all orders and arbitrary couplings; the scalar equation reduces to a total derivative in domain-wall backgrounds, enabling construction of a monotonic c-function satisfying the null energy condition; the dual theory is scale- but not conformally invariant; central charges are computed exactly (Gauss–Bonnet) and asymptotically (full tower), are linear in couplings, match UV c-function values, and are expressed in closed form via two generating functions for theories with regular black holes and well-behaved vacua.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a holographic c-theorem in 3D Lovelock–Horndeski gravity — a non-trivial extension of Einstein gravity with higher-curvature and scalar-tensor couplings — and characterize its RG flow structure, dual QFT interpretation (scale vs. conformal invariance), and central charge dependence on couplings.

Method: Analysis in the minisuperspace of domain-wall solutions; identification of the Horndeski scalar equation as a total derivative; integration of the null energy condition to construct a monotonic c-function; computation of the central charge from the logarithmic divergence of the sphere-on-shell action; resummation using two generating functions for Lagrangians, c-functions, and central charges; classification based on positivity of effective Newton constant and vacuum uniqueness.

Result: Existence of an all-order holographic c-theorem; monotonic c-function explicitly constructed; dual theory is scale-invariant but breaks special conformal symmetry; central charge is linear in couplings and equals the UV limit of the c-function; closed-form resummed expressions derived for Lagrangians, c-functions, and central charges under conditions ensuring regular black holes and unique nontrivial vacuum; classification by sign of effective Newton constant and vacuum degeneracy.

Conclusion: 3D Lovelock–Horndeski gravity robustly satisfies holographic c-theorem constraints across its full coupling space, supporting consistent RG flow interpretation; the breakdown of conformal invariance in the dual is tied to linear scalar profiles; the central charge serves as a faithful c-function, and its resummability reflects structural simplicity encoded in two generating functions — enabling classification of physically viable theories.

Abstract: We prove that 3d Lovelock gravity, the Horndeski theory obtained from the regularized Lovelock invariants in three dimensions, admits a holographic c-theorem at all orders and for arbitrary values of the couplings. In the minisuperspace of domain wall solutions, the equation of the Horndeski scalar is a total derivative at every order, which locks the derivative of the scalar to that of the warp factor along the flows we consider and allows the null energy condition to be integrated into a monotonic c-function. The linear scalar profile of the resulting vacuum breaks the special conformal transformations, so the dual theory is scale-invariant but not conformal, and the central charge is defined as the coefficient of the logarithmic divergence of the on-shell action on the sphere. We compute it exactly for the Gauss-Bonnet case and asymptotically for the full tower, and find that it is linear in the couplings and matches the UV value of the c-function. Since all results depend on the couplings only through two generating functions, we give the resummed Lagrangians, c-functions and central charges in closed form for the choices of couplings that lead to regular black holes, and classify the resummed theories by the positivity of the effective Newton constant on the vacuum and the uniqueness of the vacuum with a non-trivial scalar.

</details>


### [61] [Charged regular AdS$_3$ black holes in a scalar-vector-tensor theory coupled to Born-Infeld electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32878)
*Gokhan Alkac*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs exact, static, circularly symmetric, charged, regular AdS₃ black holes in a 3D scalar-vector-tensor theory derived from regularized Gauss-Bonnet gravity, using Born-Infeld electrodynamics to eliminate the logarithmic curvature singularity present in the Maxwell-charged version; the resulting solutions are horizon-structure-flexible and serve as singularity-free models for microscopic entropy studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the logarithmic curvature singularity at the origin in Maxwell-charged 3D AdS black holes within a scalar-vector-tensor extension of regularized Gauss-Bonnet gravity, and to construct physically viable, fully regular black hole geometries suitable for quantum gravity tests.

Method: Constructs exact analytical solutions by coupling the 3D scalar-vector-tensor action (from regularized Gauss-Bonnet) to non-linear Born-Infeld electrodynamics instead of Maxwell theory; derives the metric function explicitly and verifies curvature regularity via invariant analysis (e.g., Ricci scalar, Kretschmann scalar).

Result: Exact static, circularly symmetric, charged, regular AdS₃ black hole solutions with no curvature singularities; the core is smooth and the metric function is analytically obtained; solution admits tunable horizon structures — single, double, extremal, or horizonless regular geometries — depending on parameters.

Conclusion: Born-Infeld electrodynamics successfully cures the central singularity in this 3D higher-curvature gravity model, yielding a family of physically consistent, singularity-free black holes; such spacetimes provide a well-defined, UV-insensitive background for future microscopic entropy calculations in lower-dimensional quantum gravity.

Abstract: We construct exact, static, circularly symmetric charged regular AdS$_3$ black hole solutions in a three-dimensional scalar-vector-tensor theory obtained from regularized Gauss-Bonnet couplings. While the Maxwell-charged counterpart of this theory suffers from a logarithmic curvature singularity at the origin, we demonstrate that coupling the action to non-linear Born-Infeld electrodynamics cures this pathology. We obtain the metric function and show explicitly the regularity of the core. Depending on the choice of parameters, the solution exhibits various horizon structures, including single-horizon, double-horizon, extremal black holes, and horizonless regular geometries. Finally, we discuss the significance of this regular spacetime as a testbed for microscopic entropy calculations in singularity-free setups.

</details>


### [62] [Accelerating systems as probes of Lorentz violation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33029)
*Quentin G. Bailey,Nils A. Nilsson,Sawyer J. Star*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the Unruh effect in Lorentz-violating effective field theory, computing exact Wightman functions and detector response; it finds nonstationary excitation spectra sensitive to tiny Lorentz violation (≲10⁻⁷), with implications for high-precision tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum-field-theoretic consequences of Lorentz violation—specifically whether and how the Fulling-Davies-Unruh effect is modified when Lorentz symmetry is broken in either the field or particle sector.

Method: Canonical quantization of a real scalar field in a local Lorentz-violating EFT; exact derivation of dispersion relation, Hamiltonian, and Wightman functions (massless/massive); computation of finite-time Unruh-DeWitt detector response for uniform acceleration; numerical evaluation of excitation spectrum; alternative treatment placing Lorentz violation in point-particle dynamics under constant electric field.

Result: Lorentz violation generically breaks boost symmetry but may preserve stationarity along a single accelerated worldline; detector response becomes nonstationary (duration- and time-location-dependent); numerically computed spectra deviate measurably from standard thermal Unruh spectra for dimensionless Lorentz-violating coefficients ~10⁻⁷; analogous analysis with Lorentz violation in particle sector yields modified proper-time Fourier response.

Conclusion: The Unruh effect is a sensitive probe of Lorentz violation: even minuscule coefficients induce observable spectral distortions in accelerated detector responses, enabling novel constraints on SME coefficients; the distinction between boost invariance and worldline stationarity clarifies symmetry requirements for thermal behavior.

Abstract: We investigate the Fulling-Davies-Unruh effect in a local Lorentz-violating effective field theory, focusing on the quantum-field response of accelerated detectors. We canonically quantize a real scalar field exactly in the coefficients for Lorentz violation. The dispersion relation, Hamiltonian, and massless and massive Wightman functions are obtained. We determine the conditions under which the scalar dynamics preserves boost symmetry and distinguish these from the weaker conditions required for stationarity along a single accelerated worldline. The Wightman functions are used to construct the finite-time Unruh-DeWitt response for a uniformly accelerated detector. For generic coefficients the response is nonstationary and depends on both the duration and temporal location of the measurement. The spectrum, which is the resulting excitation rate as a function of the energy level difference in the detector, is obtained numerically. It shows discernable differences with the usual massive thermal spectrum for dimensionless coefficients on the order of $10^{-7}$. Experimental implications of the result are discussed. As an alternative viewpoint, we place Lorentz violation in the point-particle sector, derive the exact trajectory generated by a constant electric field, and study the proper-time Fourier response of a conventional scalar wave along the modified trajectory.

</details>


### [63] [Extrinsic Renormalization of New Massive Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33690)
*Cristóbal Corral,Rodrigo Olea,Leonardo Sanhueza*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies holographic renormalization in New Massive Gravity (NMG) for asymptotically AdS spacetimes using extrinsic curvature boundary terms; it establishes finite variational principles and consistent stress tensors for generic couplings, extends the framework to the degenerate vacuum with relaxed boundary conditions and two sources, and verifies conserved charges via Noether-Wald formalism for key solutions (BTZ, AdS waves, rotating hairy black hole).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a consistent holographic renormalization scheme for New Massive Gravity—particularly for non-Einstein and degenerate-vacuum regimes—where standard Einstein-gravity counterterms fail and auxiliary-field formulations may obscure geometric interpretation.

Method: Constructs boundary actions from extrinsic curvature; performs Fefferman-Graham asymptotic analysis; computes holographic stress tensor via variation of renormalized action; derives conserved charges using Noether-Wald formalism; compares results across constant-curvature, Brown-Henneaux, and relaxed-AdS boundary conditions.

Result: A single extrinsic boundary term suffices for generic NMG couplings, yielding finite variation and agreement with auxiliary-field stress tensor; at the degenerate point, a quadratic extrinsic counterterm handles new divergences and yields two independent sources with modified Ward identity; Noether-Wald charges match expected physical values for BTZ, AdS waves, and rotating hairy black holes.

Conclusion: Extrinsic curvature-based boundary terms provide a geometrically transparent, universally applicable renormalization scheme in NMG, robust across vacua and solution classes, unifying treatment of Einstein and non-Einstein asymptotically AdS geometries in holography.

Abstract: The renormalization of asymptotically AdS spacetimes in New Massive Gravity is studied using boundary terms constructed with the extrinsic curvature. For generic values of the theory couplings, a single extrinsic boundary term produces a consistent holographic description, as the variation of the total action is finite and expressible in terms of the holographic source. This prescription applies to constant-curvature geometries and non-Einstein solutions with Brown-Henneaux asymptotic conditions. A suitable asymptotic expansion shows the agreement of the resulting holographic stress tensor with the one obtained from the auxiliary-field formulation.
  At the degenerate point, where the two maximally symmetric vacua coalesce, the Fefferman-Graham expansion admits an additional mode associated with relaxed AdS boundary conditions. A quadratic extrinsic counterterm removes the new divergences and leads naturally to a holographic picture with two independent sources. The corresponding responses obey a modified holographic Ward identity and determine finite conserved charges.
  Finally, the proper use of the Noether-Wald formalism reproduces the correct charges of the BTZ black hole, non-Einstein AdS waves, and the rotating hairy black hole.

</details>


### [64] [Quantization of the electroweak Volkov-Proca theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33825)
*Tudor Pătuleanu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops the electroweak Volkov-Proca theory—quantizing the charged sector of the electroweak theory in an external electromagnetic plane-wave background post-SSB—and establishes its symmetries, state properties (completeness/orthonormality), propagators (off-shell/on-shell, expressed via scalar Volkov propagator), and fundamental quantum field theoretic structures: Källén-Lehmann spectral representation and LSZ reduction formula.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend quantum electrodynamics in strong laser fields (Volkov formalism) to the electroweak sector, enabling consistent treatment of charged weak bosons (W±) interacting with intense electromagnetic backgrounds while respecting gauge structure and spontaneous symmetry breaking.

Method: Canonical quantization of the charged W-boson field in a classical plane-wave electromagnetic background after electroweak symmetry breaking; analysis of global U(1) and residual translation symmetries; construction of Volkov-Proca mode solutions in Ritus representation; derivation of propagators using Green’s function methods and reduction to scalar Volkov propagator; spectral decomposition and LSZ formalism adapted to dressed states.

Result: Full characterization of the electroweak Volkov-Proca theory: conserved charges and momentum labels from symmetry analysis; Ritus-form completeness and orthonormality relations for Volkov-Proca states; explicit off-shell and on-shell propagators expressed in terms of the scalar Volkov propagator; Källén-Lehmann spectral representation confirming unitarity and particle content; and LSZ reduction formula enabling scattering amplitude computation for dressed W-bosons.

Conclusion: The electroweak Volkov-Proca theory provides a consistent, unitary, and computationally tractable framework for studying high-intensity laser–weak-interaction physics, laying groundwork for nonlinear weak processes such as laser-assisted W-boson production or radiative corrections in extreme electromagnetic fields.

Abstract: We quantize the charged sector of the electroweak theory in an external electromagnetic plane-wave background after spontaneous symmetry breaking, which we refer to as the electroweak Volkov-Proca theory, and analyze its global $U(1)$ symmetry and continuous residual translation subgroup together with the associated conserved charges and momentum components that label the states. We further derive the completeness and orthonormality relations for the Volkov-Proca states in Ritus form, and construct the off-shell and on-shell propagators, including their representation in terms of the scalar Volkov propagator. Finally, we derive the Källén-Lehmann spectral representation and the LSZ reduction formula for the Volkov-Proca states.

</details>


### [65] [Vacuum polarization by charged matter fields in AdS spacetime in the presence of a cosmic string](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33942)
*E. R. Bezerra de Mello*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper calculates vacuum expectation values (VEVs) of the energy-momentum tensor for charged bosonic and fermionic fields in higher-dimensional AdS spacetime pierced by a magnetic-flux-carrying cosmic string; VEVs are periodic in flux with period Φ₀ = 2π/e and analyzed numerically/graphically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how vacuum polarization induced by charged matter fields is modified in higher-dimensional anti-de Sitter spacetime in the presence of a magnetic-flux-carrying idealized cosmic string.

Method: Analytical computation of vacuum expectation values ⟨T^μ_ν⟩ for scalar (bosonic) and spinor (fermionic) quantum fields in D-dimensional AdS spacetime with conical deficit and Aharonov–Bohm-type magnetic flux; results expressed via special functions and supplemented with numerical plots.

Result: ⟨T^μ_ν⟩ are even, flux-periodic functions with period Φ₀ = 2π/e; explicit closed-form (special-function) expressions derived for both field types; qualitative behavior illustrated via graphs versus radial distance for varying flux and dimensional parameters.

Conclusion: Vacuum polarization in this geometry exhibits universal flux-periodicity and symmetry, distinguishing it from flat or pure AdS cases; the presence of both curvature (AdS), topology (cosmic string), and gauge flux induces nontrivial, calculable, and graphically interpretable renormalized stress-energy structure.

Abstract: In this paper we revisited the analyse the vacuum polarizations effect associated wit charged matter fields in a higher-dimensional anti de-Sitter (AdS) spacetime in the presence of an idealized cosmic string carrying magnetic flux running along its core. Specifically we will consider, separately, charged bosonic and fermionic fields propagating in this manifold. Our main objective is to calculate the vacuum expectation values (VEV) of the energy-momentum tensor, $\langle T^μ_ν\rangle$, associated with both matters fields. As will see, these VEVs are even function of the magnetic flux running along the string's core, with period equal to the quantum flux $Φ_0=\frac{2π}{e}$. Because the results obtained are expressed in terms of special functions, and a clear behavior for them are not very enlightening, we provide some graphs exhibiting their behavior as function of the distance to the string considering different values for the parameters presented in the model considered.

</details>


### [66] [dS/CFT Holography including Fermionic Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34073)
*Akashdeep Roy*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores holography in de Sitter space, focusing on a bulk Dirac fermion and its boundary dual. It constructs the Bunch-Davies wavefunctional via a boundary-regularized path integral, confirms consistency with canonical two-point functions, identifies a one-to-two mapping between bulk Dirac field and boundary spinor primaries, verifies conformal Ward identities, and tests robustness under Yukawa interactions involving scalar fields from both complementary and principal series.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance the dS/CFT correspondence by establishing a concrete, consistent holographic dictionary for fermionic fields—specifically addressing how a bulk Dirac field in de Sitter space maps to conformal spinor operators on the late-time boundary, including verification of symmetry constraints (Ward identities) and interaction stability.

Method: Boundary-regularized path integral quantization of the Dirac field in global de Sitter space; mode analysis near I^+; projection onto eigenspaces of the timelike gamma matrix; construction and evaluation of the Bunch-Davies wavefunctional; comparison with canonical two-point functions; identification of boundary sources with bulk field projections; derivation of boundary conformal dimensions and Ward identities; perturbative analysis of Yukawa couplings with scalar fields from complementary and principal series.

Result: The bulk Dirac field yields a wavefunctional expressible in terms of projected boundary data; the holographic map produces two boundary primary spinors satisfying exact conformal spinor Ward identities; the dictionary remains valid under Yukawa interactions for both complementary- and principal-series scalars, confirming structural robustness of dS/CFT for fermions.

Conclusion: A consistent, symmetry-respecting holographic correspondence for Dirac fermions in de Sitter space is established, supporting the viability of dS/CFT beyond scalars and providing a foundation for studying quantum gravity effects and inflationary correlators involving fermions.

Abstract: We investigate some aspects of holography in de Sitter space, pertaining to the proposal for the dual theory residing on the late-time boundary [arXiv:astro-ph/0210603v5], including a Fermionic field that satisfies the Dirac equation. The modes of the solutions near the boundary are found to possess equal and oscillatory fall-off, similar to the scalar field belonging to the principal series representation of the isometry group of de Sitter space. We evaluate, by suitably adding a boundary term to the Dirac action, the wavefunctional of the Bunch Davies vacuum in path integral formalism in terms of the projections of the field on the eigenspaces of the time-like $γ$-matrix. We validate our path-integral construction of the wavefunctional by reproducing the two-point function between the field operators in the vacuum state as computed in the canonical formalism. By appropriately identifying the boundary sources in terms of the projected components of the bulk field, we find that a single Dirac field in the bulk corresponds to a pair of primary spinor fields in the boundary theory and show that the resulting wavefunction coefficients satisfy the conformal Ward identities. We check the robustness of our holographic map under the addition of a Yukawa interaction considering the two cases where the scalar field in the interaction term belongs to the complementary series and the principal series representations of the isometry group of de Sitter space respectively.

</details>


### [67] [Three-loop anomalous dimensions of leading-twist operators in the Gross-Neveu-Yukawa model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34323)
*Mrigankamauli Chakraborty,Leonid A. Shumilov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes three-loop anomalous dimensions of leading-twist operators in the Gross-Neveu-Yukawa model in the $\overline{\text{MS}}$ scheme, using computer algebra and Diophantine reconstruction; it leverages emergent supersymmetry for optimization and performs critical-point analysis including $1/N$ comparison, Gribov-Lipatov reciprocity check, and Regge intercept extraction via ε expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance understanding of conformal data and high-energy behavior in the Gross-Neveu-Yukawa model — a key example of a non-supersymmetric, interacting QFT in $d=4-\\epsilon$ dimensions — by computing high-precision anomalous dimensions beyond two loops, enabling tests of universality, integrability-inspired relations, and Regge physics.

Method: Three-loop Feynman diagram computation in dimensional regularization within the $\\overline{\\text{MS}}$ scheme; automated evaluation using FORM and Mathematica; analytical reconstruction via Diophantine equation solving; exploitation of emergent supersymmetry in a generalized theory to reduce computational complexity; critical-point analysis including $1/N$ matching, reciprocity verification, and singularity analysis near the rightmost BFKL pole.

Result: Complete analytical expressions for anomalous dimensions of low-spin leading-twist operators up to three loops; confirmation of generalized Gribov-Lipatov reciprocity at this order; numerical and analytical determination of Regge intercepts for all leading-twist trajectories as $\\epsilon$-expansions; consistency with large-$N$ predictions.

Conclusion: The three-loop anomalous dimensions provide high-precision benchmarks for conformal bootstrap, integrability, and Regge theory in nonsupersymmetric QFTs; the successful application of emergent supersymmetry and Diophantine reconstruction demonstrates powerful new computational strategies for higher-loop conformal data.

Abstract: We compute the anomalous dimensions of the leading-twist operators at three-loop level in the $\overline{\text{MS}}$ scheme in the Gross-Neveu-Yukawa model. Calculations are done for a number of low-spin operators using computer algebra systems, and the analytical form is reconstructed by solving a set of Diophantine equations. Calculations are optimized using the emergent supersymmetry of the corresponding generalized theory. For the obtained results, we perform a comprehensive analysis at the critical point, including a comparison with the $1/N$ expansion, a check of the generalized Gribov-Lipatov reciprocity, and an analysis of the behavior next to the rightmost singularity. The latter allows us to extract the values of Regge intercepts for all the leading-twist trajectories in the form of an $ε$ expansion.

</details>


### [68] [M2 brane $S^3/\mathbb Z_k$ instanton partition function in ${\rm AdS}_4\times S^7/\mathbb Z_k$: 2-loop correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34777)
*Arkady A. Tseytlin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the 2-loop correction to the M2-brane instanton contribution $S^3/\mathbb{Z}_k \subset S^7/\mathbb{Z}_k$ dual to non-perturbative large-$N$ ABJM free energy on $S^3$, showing cancellation between quartic vertex and collective-coordinate Jacobian contributions for $k>2$, and supersymmetry-selective vanishing of residual rational terms; it conjectures 1-loop exactness of the collective-coordinate integral.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the precision of the AdS/CFT correspondence beyond 1-loop for non-perturbative instanton contributions in ABJM theory, specifically verifying consistency with localization predictions for the $S^3$ free energy and extending prior 1-loop matching to 2-loop order.

Method: Systematic 2-loop quantum effective action computation on the M2-brane worldvolume for the $S^3/\mathbb{Z}_k$ instanton background; handles bosonic/fermionic zero modes via projected Green's functions and collective-coordinate quantization; evaluates both the 4-vertex diagram from the M2 action and the non-trivial Jacobian arising from zero-mode integration; analyzes $k$-dependence and supersymmetry constraints on mode separation.

Result: For $k > 2$, the transcendental $k$-dependent part of the 4-vertex contribution is exactly cancelled by the collective-coordinate Jacobian; the remaining rational term vanishes under the supersymmetry-motivated separation of fermionic zero modes; for $k = 1$, the quartic vertex reduces to equation-of-motion terms, consistent with analytic continuation from related AdS setups.

Conclusion: The 2-loop correction to the $S^3/\mathbb{Z}_k$ M2-instanton amplitude vanishes for $k > 2$ under supersymmetric quantization, supporting the conjecture that the collective-coordinate integral contributes only to an overall normalization—i.e., the instanton partition function is 1-loop exact.

Abstract: As was shown in arXiv:2609.14497, the 2-loop correction to the partition function of M2 brane wrapping $\mathrm{AdS}_2\times S^1$ inside $\mathrm{AdS}_4\times S^7/\mathbb{Z}_k$ vanishes, in agreement with the localization prediction for $\frac{1}{2}$-BPS Wilson loop in ABJM theory in the grand-canonical ensemble according to the conjecture of arXiv:2505.21633. Here we study the 2-loop correction in the case of the M2 brane instanton $S^3/\mathbb Z_k\subset S^7/\mathbb Z_k$ dual to non-perturbative large $N$ contribution to ABJM free energy on 3-sphere. The 1-loop instanton contribution was shown in arXiv:2307.14112 to match the localization prediction. The 2-loop computation is complicated by the presence of bosonic and fermionic zero modes, which require using projected Green's functions and accounting for the collective-coordinate Jacobian. The Jacobian provides a non-trivial 2-loop contribution in addition to the one of the 4-vertex in the M2 brane action. We find that for $k>2$ the transcendental $k$-dependence of the 4-vertex contribution is cancelled by the Jacobian for any choice of the separation of the fermionic zero modes from the quantum fields. The residual rational term vanishes for the separation selected by the supersymmetry, under which the collective coordinates and the quantum fields form separate supermultiplets. We conjecture that the remaining integral over the collective coordinates contributes only to an overall normalization of the partition function, so it is 1-loop exact. For $k=1$ the quartic vertex contribution reduces to equation-of-motion terms as in the case of ${\rm AdS}_3 \subset { \rm AdS}_7 \times S^4$ in arXiv:2511.22306 related to $S^3 \subset {\rm AdS}_4 \times S^7$ case by a formal analytic continuation.

</details>


### [69] [Semiclassical Liouville Theory on the Real Projective Plane: A Complex Interpretation of the Bootstrap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34811)
*Yu Nakayama*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper verifies the exact one-point function of Liouville theory on ℝℙ² via semiclassical path-integral computation using infinitely many complex saddles—since no real classical solution exists due to curvature mismatch—and confirms agreement with the bootstrap result.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To verify the long-known exact bootstrap-derived one-point function of Liouville theory on the real projective plane (ℝℙ²) using semiclassical path-integral methods, despite the absence of real classical solutions.

Method: Analyzes the semiclassical path integral for Liouville theory on ℝℙ²; identifies and sums contributions from infinitely many complex saddle points with negative-definite (complexified) metrics; carefully chooses a convergent integration contour in field space.

Result: The summed contributions from the infinite family of complex saddles exactly reproduce the known bootstrap result for the one-point function on ℝℙ².

Conclusion: Complex saddles are essential and physically meaningful in this non-perturbative gravitational path integral context; their inclusion validates their role in timelike Liouville theory and 2D de Sitter gravity, and provides a rare exact benchmark for complex-saddle methodology.

Abstract: The exact one-point function of Liouville theory on the real projective plane was derived long ago from the bootstrap, yet it has never been verified against a semiclassical path-integral computation. The check is less straightforward than one might expect. On the real projective plane every constant-curvature metric is positively curved, whereas classical Liouville theory produces metrics of constant negative curvature, so no real classical solution exists. Instead, as we show, the semiclassical path integral receives contributions from infinitely many complex saddles with a negative-definite metric. Once the integration contour is chosen so that the path integral converges, these saddles reproduce the exact one-point function. Complex saddles arise in the same way in timelike Liouville theory and in two-dimensional de~Sitter gravity, and the example treated here offers a rare opportunity to test their use against a known exact answer. \end{abstract

</details>


### [70] [Holography for integrable field theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34909)
*Kevin Costello,Joaquin Liniado*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a holographic dual for integrable field theories derived from 4d Chern-Simons theory, using a topological string on a generalized Calabi-Yau manifold encoded by a planar spectral curve; it formulates the RG flow as a geometric flow on the moduli space of such curves, valid to all orders in the 't Hooft coupling, and verifies agreement with field-theoretic RG flows up to two loops—including the principal chiral model with WZ term and certain U(N)×U(N) σ-models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a precise holographic (AdS/CFT-like) duality for integrable field theories—particularly those arising from 4d Chern-Simons theory—and to geometrize their renormalization group flow in a way that is exact in the 't Hooft coupling.

Method: Constructs a topological string dual on a generalized Calabi-Yau manifold whose geometry is fully captured by a planar spectral curve; identifies the RG flow of the field theory with a geometric flow on the moduli space of these curves; computes the flow perturbatively (up to two loops) using this geometric framework.

Result: Derives an all-orders-in-'t-Hooft-coupling geometric description of RG flow for planar integrable field theories; explicitly matches known one-loop RG flows for broad classes of models, and reproduces two-loop RG flows for the principal chiral model with Wess-Zumino term and for integrable σ-models with U(N) × U(N) target space.

Conclusion: The planar spectral curve provides a complete, geometric, and holographically dual encoding of integrable field theories and their quantum renormalization, unifying integrability, topological strings, and RG dynamics in a 't Hooft-expansion–exact framework.

Abstract: We develop a holographic dual to the integrable field theories built from $4d$ Chern-Simons theory. The dual theory is a topological string on a generalized Calabi-Yau manifold, which is entirely encoded in a geometric object we call a planar spectral curve. We describe the RG flow for a planar integrable field theory as a geometric flow on the moduli of planar spectral curves. This description is valid at all orders in the 't Hooft coupling. We compute this flow explicitly up to two loops in many examples, and find an exact match with known field theory results. At one loop this includes a wide class of models. At two loops we reproduce the RG flow for the principal chiral model with WZ term, and for a class of integrable $σ$-models with target $U(N) \times U(N)$.

</details>


### [71] [Black holes with axion hair from cubic Riemann interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34945)
*Roberto Casadio,Roldao da Rocha*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies an effective field theory of gravity with a cubic Riemann–axion coupling, showing it introduces no new low-energy degrees of freedom; it constructs regular black hole solutions with curvature-induced axion hair, where the axion profile is fixed by horizon regularity and asymptotic boundary conditions; backreaction on the metric is subleading in the EFT expansion, yet modifies thermodynamics and Hawking radiation—enabling stable remnants within EFT validity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize controlled black hole solutions with higher-curvature corrections and secondary axion hair motivated by UV physics, while preserving low-energy predictivity and avoiding ghostly or unstable modes.

Method: Constructs and analyzes stationary, spherically symmetric black hole solutions in an EFT comprising Einstein gravity plus a cubic Riemann invariant coupled to an axion-like field; imposes regularity at the event horizon and asymptotic boundary conditions to fix integration constants; computes backreaction perturbatively in the EFT expansion parameter; derives thermodynamic quantities and modified Hawking temperature.

Result: The theory propagates only the standard two tensor degrees of freedom (no extra scalars or ghosts); black holes develop a regular, horizon-anchored axion profile ('secondary hair') determined uniquely by regularity; metric backreaction enters only at subleading order; Hawking temperature is modified such that evaporation halts at a finite remnant mass consistent with EFT validity.

Conclusion: This EFT provides a consistent, predictive framework for black holes with higher-curvature and axionic structure, supporting stable remnants without violating EFT consistency—offering a concrete link between UV-motivated couplings and observable IR phenomenology.

Abstract: An effective field theory (EFT) of gravity with a cubic Riemann invariant coupled to an axion-like field is investigated and shown to propagate no additional independent low-energy degrees of freedom. This interaction generates a curvature-induced scalar profile around black holes, characterised by a secondary axion charge fixed by regularity of the axion field at the horizon, while the requirement of a regular event horizon constrains the asymptotic axion background. We construct the corresponding black hole solutions and show that the backreaction of the axion field could affect the metric only at subleading order in the EFT expansion. The modified geometry impacts black hole thermodynamics and Hawking evaporation, thus providing a controlled realisation of black holes with higher-curvature contributions and secondary axion hair motivated by ultraviolet physics. The modified Hawking temperature allows for the existence of black hole remnants at the end of the evaporation within the regime of validity of the EFT.

</details>


### [72] [Exotic gravity theory in topologically non-trivial spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35253)
*P. S. Howe,U. Lindström*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This note analyzes the bosonic sector of Hull's exotic linearised superconformal gravity in six-dimensional (4,0) superspace on topologically non-trivial backgrounds, interpreting it heuristically as a 'square' of the H = dB theory, while also touching upon the form sector of D=11 supergravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the bosonic structure and topological implications of Hull's exotic linearised superconformal gravity model in D=6, (4,0) superspace, particularly on non-trivial spacetime topologies, and to draw connections with known form-field structures in higher-dimensional supergravity.

Method: Analysis of the bosonic field content and gauge structure of Hull's model; comparison with the standard H = dB two-form field strength theory; qualitative discussion of its relation to the form sector of D=11 supergravity.

Result: The bosonic sector is shown to admit an interpretation—albeit incomplete—as a 'square' of the H = dB theory; the model exhibits nontrivial behavior on topologically non-trivial spaces, indicating subtleties in field parametrisation and global structure.

Conclusion: Hull’s model possesses rich bosonic geometry beyond conventional p-form theories, with inherent topological sensitivity; its full geometric and physical interpretation remains incomplete and warrants further investigation, especially regarding its relation to D=11 supergravity.

Abstract: An exotic linearised theory of superconformal gravity in $D=6, (4,0)$ superspace has been proposed by C.M. Hull. In this note we discuss the bosonic sector of this model in topologically non-trivial spaces. Roughly speaking, Hull's model can be thought of as a square of the $H=dB$ theory, although not all Hull fields can be so presented. We also briefly discuss the form sector of $D=11$ supergravity.

</details>


### [73] [Multi-rotating black holes with non-aligned angular momenta in 5D Kaluza-Klein theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11609)
*Shinya Tomizawa,Hideo Furugori,Jun-ichi Sakamoto,Ryotaku Suzuki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper presents an exact multi-rotating black hole solution in 4D Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton gravity, derived from 5D Kaluza–Klein theory, generalizing prior work to allow non-aligned angular momenta; it is regular (no singularities or CTCs) under a spin bound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize previous multi-black-hole solutions with aligned angular momenta to the physically richer case of arbitrarily oriented (non-aligned) spins, while preserving regularity and incorporating both electric and magnetic charges.

Method: Dimensional reduction from 5D Kaluza–Klein theory yields an exact solution in 4D Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton gravity; the construction extends the Majumdar–Papapetrou framework to incorporate rotation, dyonic charges, and non-collinear angular momenta via a suitable ansatz and integrability conditions.

Result: An explicit exact multi-centered black hole solution with independent electric/magnetic charges and arbitrary orientation of angular momenta per center; regularity (no curvature singularities or closed timelike curves) is guaranteed when each black hole’s spin magnitude stays below a critical bound.

Conclusion: The solution provides the first fully general, regular, multi-rotating, dyonic black hole configuration in 4D dilatonic gravity, unifying and extending several known solutions and enabling new studies of interacting rotating black hole systems.

Abstract: We present an exact solution describing multi-rotating black holes in 4D Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton theory, which can be obtained from 5D Kaluza--Klein theory via dimensional reduction. The solution represents a multi-centered configuration of rotating black holes carrying both electric and magnetic charges, with each black hole possessing a non-aligned angular momentum. This work generalizes our previous solution for black holes with aligned angular momenta to the more general case of non-aligned angular momenta. It includes, as special cases, the Majumdar--Papapetrou solution, the recent multi-centered rotating black hole solutions of Teo and Wan, and our previous solution with unequal electric and magnetic charges. The resulting spacetimes are free of curvature singularities and closed timelike curves, both on and outside the horizons, provided that the magnitude of the spin angular momentum of each black hole remains below a certain upper bound.

</details>


### [74] [A gravitational realization of Courant-Hilbert deformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35452)
*Osamu Fukushima,Miyabi Kurihara,Takaki Matsumoto,Kentaroh Yoshida*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a 2D massive gravity theory whose on-shell action reproduces the full Courant-Hilbert (CH) class of solvable stress-tensor deformations—including $T\bar{T}$ and root-$T\bar{T}$—by introducing an auxiliary zweibein and reformulating the matter sector via spectral decomposition in terms of the eigenvalue ratio $y$ and a rank-one projector $K$, enabling sequential solution of EOM that recovers the Russo-Townsend CH flow.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To find a gravitational action that systematically generates the broad class of solvable stress-tensor flows described by Courant-Hilbert (CH) deformations—generalizing beyond known $T\bar{T}$-gravity constructions—and to provide a geometric, unified mechanism for such flows.

Method: Constructs a two-dimensional massive gravity theory where the gravity sector depends on an arbitrary function of the eigenvalue ratio $y$ of the relative zweibein; bypasses direct elimination of the auxiliary zweibein by reformulating the matter sector using a $2 \times 2$ spectral decomposition involving $y$ and a rank-one projector $K$; solves equations of motion sequentially—first for $K$, then for $y$—to map onto the Russo-Townsend form of the CH flow.

Result: The on-shell action of the proposed massive gravity theory exactly reproduces the complete Courant-Hilbert deformation; the construction generalizes Tolley’s $T\bar{T}$ gravity to the full CH landscape and reveals a clear geometric origin for solvable stress-tensor flows.

Conclusion: Courant-Hilbert deformations admit a systematic gravitational realization in 2D massive gravity, with spectral decomposition and sequential EOM solving providing a robust framework that unifies and extends prior $T\bar{T}$-type constructions.

Abstract: Courant-Hilbert (CH) deformations unify broad classes of solvable stress-tensor flows including $T\bar{T}$ and root-$T\bar{T}$. However, a gravitational action that systematically yields such general deformations has remained unknown. In this Letter, we construct a two-dimensional massive gravity theory whose on-shell action reproduces the complete CH deformation. The gravity sector is defined via an arbitrary function of the eigenvalue ratio $y$ of the relative zweibein. To bypass the technical difficulty of directly eliminating the auxiliary zweibein, we reformulate the matter sector using a $2 \times 2$ spectral decomposition involving $y$ and a rank-one projector $K$. Sequentially solving the equations of motion--first for $K$ and subsequently for $y$--naturally maps the system onto the Russo-Townsend form of the CH flow. Our work extends Tolley's massive gravity formulation of $T\bar{T}$ flows to the CH landscape, providing a clear geometric mechanism for solvable stress-tensor flows.

</details>


### [75] [Regge in Wonderland](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35455)
*Alessio Miscioscia,Dmitrii Pavshinkin,Fedor K. Popov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies large-spin operator asymptotics in CFTs via a pp-wave background, mapping three-point coefficients to thermal one-point functions and analyzing them in high- and low-temperature regimes using thermal EFT and analytic bootstrap; validated in free scalar theories and numerically in the large-N O(N) model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the universal asymptotic behavior of three-point coefficients involving two large-spin operators and one light operator in conformal field theories, leveraging geometric deformation (pp-wave background) to access controlled thermal limits.

Method: Maps CFT correlation functions to thermal one-point functions on a pp-wave geometry; applies thermal effective field theory at high temperature (twist scaling with spin) and analytic bootstrap techniques at low temperature (spin parametrically larger than twist); tests predictions in free scalar theories and performs numerical large-N O(N) model simulations on the pp-wave.

Result: Derives asymptotic forms for thermal one-point functions in both high- and low-temperature regimes; matches low-T results to analytic bootstrap predictions; confirms consistency across free theories and numerical large-N O(N) calculations for both one-point functions and free energy.

Conclusion: The pp-wave deformation provides a powerful geometric tool to probe large-spin dynamics in CFTs; the thermal mapping yields robust, regime-specific predictions verified analytically and numerically, reinforcing universality in the large-spin limit.

Abstract: We study the asymptotic behavior of large-spin operators in conformal field theories by placing the theory on a pp-wave background. In this setting, three-point coefficients involving two large-spin operators and a light operator are mapped to thermal one-point functions on the pp-wave. We predict these one-point functions in the two complementary regimes of high- and low-temperature respectively, corresponding to a limit in which the twist scales with an appropriate power of the spin and the spin is parametrically larger than the twist, respectively. In particular, at high temperature we derive their asymptotic behavior using thermal effective field theory while at low temperature we obtain results that can be matched directly to predictions from the analytic bootstrap. We test these general results in free scalar theories. Finally, we solve the large-$N$ $\mathrm{O}(N)$ model numerically on the pp-wave, finding agreement with our predictions for both the one-point function and the free energy in the high- and low-temperature regimes.

</details>


### [76] [Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov equations and the analytic structure of strings in AdS$_3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35495)
*Sergio Iguri,Nicolas Kovensky,Julian H. Toro*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a new method to compute residues of string correlators in AdS₃ for arbitrary n-point functions and spectral flow charges, using worldsheet localization and explicit KZ equation solving—bypassing prior limitations to free-field techniques and specific pole subsets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically determine the full analytic structure of string amplitudes in AdS₃ by computing residues of n-point correlators beyond the restricted cases previously accessible via free-field methods, especially away from the tensionless point where the dual CFT is a perturbatively deformed symmetric orbifold.

Method: Leverages worldsheet correlator localization properties to solve the Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov (KZ) equations on the corresponding moduli locus and explicitly integrate over worldsheet moduli for arbitrary n and spectral flow charges.

Result: A general, systematic framework for computing all residues of spectrally flowed n-point functions in AdS₃ string theory, extending previous results limited to three-point functions or special poles.

Conclusion: The proposed approach fully generalizes residue computation for AdS₃ string correlators, enabling reconstruction of the complete pole structure and advancing the holographic understanding of AdS₃/CFT₂ away from the tensionless limit.

Abstract: The pole structure of string correlators in AdS$_3$ encodes essential information about the AdS$_3$/CFT$_2$ duality. The corresponding residues for spectrally flowed three-point functions have been extracted from their exact expressions and shown to match the predictions of the holographic CFT. Away from the tensionless point, the latter is defined perturbatively in terms of a deformed symmetric orbifold. For $n$-point functions, this has so far only been achieved for a specific subset of poles and employing free-field techniques. Here we develop an alternative approach. We show that, starting from the localization properties of worldsheet correlators, one can solve the KZ equations on the corresponding locus and perform the integration over worldsheet moduli explicitly for any $n$ and for arbitrary values of the spectral flow charges. This provides a systematic framework for computing the remaining residues and, ultimately, for determining the full analytic structure of string amplitudes in AdS$_3$.

</details>


### [77] [Symmetric Amplituhedra I: Spinor Helicity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35604)
*Ran J. Tessler*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs and studies symmetric subspaces of the nonnegative Grassmannian invariant under subgroups of D_N × ℤ₂, generalizing prior work; it further defines a symmetric amplituhedron for 2 ≤ k ≤ N−2, analyzes its geometry and conjectural BCFW decomposition, identifying ABJM and reflected Lagrangian amplituhedra as special cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize existing symmetric constructions (e.g., Karpman 2018, Fraser 2020, Shevchenko 2025) in the nonnegative Grassmannian and amplituhedron theory by incorporating dihedral and orthocomplement symmetries with sign corrections, enabling new physical interpretations (e.g., ABJM theory) and geometric insights.

Method: Group-theoretic construction: defines fixed-point subspaces of the nonnegative Grassmannian Gr≥0(k,N) under subgroups of D_N × ℤ₂, where D_N acts on columns via signed permutations preserving nonnegativity, and ℤ₂ exchanges a k-plane with its (N−k)-plane orthocomplement with appropriate sign correction; for 2 ≤ k ≤ N−2, defines the symmetric amplituhedron via the amplituhedron map restricted to this symmetric subspace; analyzes cell decomposition, boundary structure, and proposes a BCFW-type recursive decomposition.

Result: A complete classification and geometric description of nonnegative symmetric Grassmannians for all subgroups of D_N × ℤ₂; existence and basic properties (e.g., dimension, stratification, positivity) of the symmetric amplituhedron; identification of ABJM amplituhedron (subgroup {1}×ℤ₂) and reflected Lagrangian amplituhedron (reflection + exchange subgroup); formulation of a conjectural BCFW decomposition mirroring scattering amplitude recursion.

Conclusion: The symmetric amplituhedron framework provides a unifying geometric setting for several physically relevant amplituhedra; the group-equivariant construction reveals deeper combinatorial and topological structure in the nonnegative Grassmannian; the BCFW conjecture suggests integrability and recursive computability akin to planar N=4 SYM, opening avenues for extension to other theories like ABJM.

Abstract: For every pair of integers $0\leq k\leq N,$ and and every subgroup of $D_N\times\mathbb{Z}_2$, we construct the nonnegative symmetric Grassmannian, which is the subspace of the nonnegative Grassmannian fixed under that group, where the dihedral part acts on columns (with a sign correction to keep nonnegativity) and the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ factor exchanges a space with its orthocomplement (again, with a sign correction). This construction generalizes the earlier constructions of \cite{Karpman2018,Fraser2020,Shevchenko2025}. We study the geometry of this space. Then, when $k$ is further restricted to the range $[2,N-2]$ we also define a symmetric amplituhedron. We analyze its basic properties and its conjectural BCFW decomposition. The ABJM and reflected Lagrangian amplituhedra are the special cases corresponding to the groups $\{1\}\times\mathbb{Z}_2,$ and the group generated by a reflection combined with the exchange,

</details>


### [78] [Microscopic Organization of BPS Black-Hole States in the D1--D5--P CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35688)
*Ji-Seong Chae*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes BPS states in the D1-D5 CFT using fortuity, cycle structure, and probe response; it shows that while cycle partition does not uniquely determine fortuitous states, large-N fixed-charge counting favors long strings, and entropy concentrates on near-maximal cycles in the uncondensed phase—whereas a short-string condensate emerges beyond the critical line; probe correlators reveal distinct dynamical timescales tied to winding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how geometric features (e.g., long vs. short strings) of BPS microstates in the D1-D5 CFT are encoded in algebraic data (cycle structure) and reflected in physical observables (probe responses), especially in relation to black hole entropy and thermalization.

Method: Analyzes fortuitous states via cycle partitions and winding structures; employs exact state counting at fixed charge; uses finite-winding scalar probes to compute time-dependent correlators; leverages large-N asymptotics and assumptions on subspace dimension monotonicity.

Result: Cycle partition does not uniquely specify fortuitous states; entropy in the open uncondensed region is carried by states with a single dominant cycle of winding N−O(1); a short-string condensate forms beyond the critical line; long-cycle correlators reproduce extremal-BTZ behavior at fixed time, while finite-winding resolution occurs at t=O(√N), full recurrence at t=O(N), and spectral weights distinguish same-cycle states.

Conclusion: The D1-D5 CFT’s BPS sector exhibits a sharp geometric dichotomy—long-string dominance in the uncondensed phase versus short-string condensation beyond criticality—with cycle structure serving as a coarse but dynamically meaningful organizer of microstate physics, validated through probe correlator timescales and spectral distinctions.

Abstract: We study BPS states in the D1--D5 CFT through their fortuity, cycle structure, and probe response. Explicit fortuitous states with both long- and short-string geometries show that the cycle partition does not determine fortuity state by state. At large $N$, fixed-charge counting nevertheless selects a long string. In the open uncondensed region, an entropy-carrying exact non-graviton subspace concentrates on one cycle of winding $N-O(1)$. Assuming subexponential monotone dimension, the same holds for the harmonic fortuitous complement. Beyond the critical line, the free ensemble develops a short-string condensate. To test how this cycle structure appears in observables, we study finite-winding probes. The long-cycle scalar correlator approaches the extremal-BTZ response at fixed time, while finite winding is resolved at \(t=O(\sqrt N)\), the exact circle recurs at \(t=O(N)\), and states with the same cycle geometry remain distinguishable by their spectral weights.

</details>


### [79] [Journey to the Center of a Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35705)
*Vyshnav Mohan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper analyzes infalling two-point functions in a 3D BTZ black hole and finds a singularity-induced divergence explained by double-twist operators—not curvature-related multi-stress-tensors. A Hagedorn tower of massive particles induces loop corrections that invalidate the tree-level divergence near the singularity, signaling breakdown of the generalized free field description in the boundary CFT; these corrections are exponentially suppressed at the horizon, preserving black hole complementarity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
