Motivation: To understand how bulk singularity physics is encoded in the boundary CFT, specifically whether divergences in infalling correlators reflect curvature singularities (as in higher dimensions) or alternative CFT data, and to assess the validity of semiclassical gravity near the singularity in light of quantum corrections.

Method: Analyzes the infalling scalar two-point function in BTZ geometry with one insertion outside and one on a timelike geodesic; performs OPE decomposition in the dual CFT2; identifies dominant operator contributions (double-twist vs. multi-stress-tensor); incorporates a Hagedorn-spectrum tower of massive bulk fields to compute leading loop corrections; examines scaling and suppression of corrections relative to horizon and singularity.

Result: The divergence at the singularity arises exclusively from double-twist operator exchange—not multi-stress-tensors; inclusion of a Hagedorn tower generates loop corrections that become O(1) near the singularity, invalidating the tree-level result; in the boundary theory, this corresponds to breakdown of the generalized free field approximation for the probe operator in the black hole state; corrections remain exponentially suppressed at the horizon.

Conclusion: Semiclassical correlators break down near the BTZ singularity due to Hagedorn-driven quantum effects, not curvature; the boundary CFT diagnosis via double-twist dominance and Hagedorn-induced non-perturbative effects supports black hole complementarity—since singularity-sensitive physics is parametrically decoupled from the horizon.

Abstract: An infalling two-point function of a scalar field in a three-dimensional BTZ black hole, with one operator inserted in the exterior and the other inserted along a timelike geodesic, diverges when the infalling insertion point reaches the black hole singularity. In the boundary description, this divergence comes entirely from the exchange of double-twist operators, rather than from the multi-stress-tensor sector that encodes the curvature singularities in higher dimensions. We show that the inclusion of a tower of massive particles with Hagedorn growth gives rise to loop corrections that become important near the singularity, where they trigger a Hagedorn transition that renders the tree-level divergence untrustworthy. In the boundary theory, the corresponding tower of primary operators leads to a breakdown of the generalized free field description of the probe operator in the black hole state. In a large black hole, these corrections become important only near the singularity and remain exponentially suppressed at the horizon. The region where these corrections dominate is therefore parametrically separated from the horizon, as anticipated by the principle of black hole complementarity.

</details>

### [80] [Impact of Helicity-Isospin Convention Matching on the Extraction of $α$ from $B^0\to(ρπ)^0$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31828)
*Radek Žlebčík*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Corrects a helicity convention mismatch in ρ→ππ decays used in time-dependent Dalitz-plot analyses of B⁰→(ρπ)⁰, reducing tension in the direct measurement of CKM angle α from 2.9σ to 2.1σ; combined with B→ππ and B→ρρ, yields α = 87.0⁺¹¹·⁸₋₄·₇°.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Resolve inconsistency between the ρ→ππ helicity convention employed in time-dependent Dalitz-plot analyses of B⁰→(ρπ)⁰ and that assumed in the isospin analysis, which caused significant tension in the extracted value of the unitarity-triangle angle α.

Method: Identify and correct the helicity convention mismatch in the ρ→ππ transition amplitude; reperform the isospin analysis using the consistent convention; combine updated α determination from B⁰→(ρπ)⁰ with existing measurements from B→ππ and B→ρρ decays.

Result: Tension between direct (B→ρπ, B→ππ, B→ρρ) and indirect (global CKM fits) determinations of α reduced from 2.9σ to 2.1σ; direct combined result: α = (87.0⁺¹¹·⁸₋₄·₇)°.

Conclusion: The helicity convention error was a significant systematic bias; its correction improves consistency between direct and indirect determinations of α, though residual tension remains, warranting further scrutiny of theoretical and experimental inputs.

Abstract: We show that the $ρ\toππ$ helicity convention used in the time-dependent Dalitz-plot measurements of $B^0\to(ρπ)^0$ decays does not match the convention assumed in the subsequent isospin analysis. Once corrected, the tension between the direct determination of the unitarity-triangle angle $α$ from these decays and its indirect determination from global fits decreases from $2.9σ$ to $2.1σ$. Combined with the $B\toππ$ and $B\toρρ$ modes, the direct determination becomes $α= (87.0^{+11.8}_{-4.7})^\circ$, compared with the original world average $(84.1^{+3.7}_{-3.0})^\circ$.

</details>


### [81] [Neutrino Direct Simulation Monte Carlo: Accurate modeling of out-of-equilibrium decaying cosmological relics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31833)
*Kensuke Akita,Miguel Escudero,Oleksii Ihnatenko,Maksym Ovchynnikov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the Neutrino Direct Simulation Monte Carlo (νDSMC) method to jointly simulate decaying relics, neutrino spectra, electromagnetic plasma, cosmic expansion, and Big Bang nucleosynthesis—enabling accurate, efficient, and physically comprehensive modeling of nonstandard cosmologies with MeV-scale energy injection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reliably model neutrino thermalization and its cosmological consequences in nonstandard scenarios where decaying relics inject energy at MeV temperatures—scenarios where standard approximations (e.g., momentum-averaged treatments) fail qualitatively.

Method: Extended νDSMC framework incorporating joint evolution of relics, neutrinos (with flavor conversion, quantum statistics), electromagnetic plasma, and Hubble expansion; coupled self-consistently to Big Bang nucleosynthesis; validated against quantum kinetic and quasi-classical Boltzmann solvers.

Result: νDSMC achieves excellent agreement with independent quantum kinetic/Boltzmann solutions while including more physics (e.g., pion production by energetic neutrinos, inverse decays) and running significantly faster for strongly nonthermal populations; momentum-averaged approximations yield wrong-sign ΔN_eff and erroneous light-element abundances when departures from equilibrium are large.

Conclusion: The extended νDSMC framework enables robust, high-fidelity cosmological tests of new physics involving MeV-scale energy injection, overcoming limitations of traditional approximations and making broad parameter scans feasible.

Abstract: We consider nonstandard cosmologies in which decaying relics inject energy at MeV temperatures, driving neutrinos out of equilibrium. The subsequent neutrino evolution shapes predictions for the cosmic radiation density and primordial light element abundances. Earlier work introduced Neutrino Direct Simulation Monte Carlo ($ν$DSMC), which describes neutrino transport through Monte Carlo sampling of particle interactions. Here, we extend this method by jointly evolving the relic population, neutrino spectra, electromagnetic plasma, and cosmic expansion, and coupling this evolution to Big Bang nucleosynthesis. The framework includes direct and inverse decays of the relic, neutrino flavor conversion, quantum statistics, and pion production by energetic neutrinos. To reveal the physical impact of these effects and independently test $ν$DSMC, we compare several approaches to neutrino evolution across a range of nonstandard cosmological scenarios. We find excellent agreement with independent solutions of quantum kinetic or quasi-classical Boltzmann equations. Compared with these calculations, $ν$DSMC includes a broader range of physical processes and is substantially faster for strongly nonthermal neutrino populations. This makes broad, robust parameter scans for nonstandard cosmologies practical. We show that when departures from thermal equilibrium are large, approximations that average over neutrino momenta can give qualitatively incorrect predictions, including the wrong sign of the change in the effective number of neutrino species and substantially incorrect primordial nuclear abundances. We conclude that the extended $ν$DSMC framework enables reliable cosmological tests of new physics by following neutrino thermalization and its observable consequences.

</details>


### [82] [Disentangling $V_{ud}$ from New Physics in Beta Decays with the FCC-ee](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31834)
*Lemonia Gialidi,Elie Hammou,Kamil Laurent,Vaisakh Plakkot,Juan Rojo,Jordy de Vries*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper determines the CKM element V_ud and SMEFT Wilson coefficients simultaneously using superallowed beta decays, LEP/LHC data, and future collider projections (HL-LHC, FCC-ee); FCC-ee uniquely breaks the V_ud–New Physics degeneracy, restoring SM precision and tightening SMEFT constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Superallowed β-decays provide the most precise determination of V_ud but are only sensitive to an effective combination of V_ud and possible New Physics in left-handed charged currents; low-energy data alone cannot disentangle them.

Method: Combined analysis within the SMEFiT framework: a dedicated likelihood for superallowed transitions (including radiative and nuclear-structure uncertainties) with LEP and LHC world data, plus HL-LHC and FCC-ee projections.

Result: Current collider data and HL-LHC projections only partially resolve the degeneracy between V_ud and New Physics; FCC-ee fully decouples V_ud from New Physics, recovering SM-level precision on V_ud and significantly tightening constraints on relevant SMEFT Wilson coefficients.

Conclusion: FCC-ee is essential to achieve a SMEFT-consistent, model-independent extraction of V_ud and resolve the Cabibbo angle anomaly; results underscore strong complementarity between high-precision low-energy experiments and future e+e− colliders.

Abstract: Superallowed nuclear $β$-decays enable the most precise determination of the CKM element $V_{ud}$, but they are only sensitive to an effective combination of $V_{ud}$ and New Physics contributions to left-handed charged currents, which low-energy data alone cannot disentangle. Here we determine $V_{ud}$ simultaneously with the relevant Wilson coefficients of the Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) by combining, within the SMEFiT framework, a dedicated likelihood for superallowed transitions, including radiative and nuclear-structure uncertainties, with LEP and LHC world data and HL-LHC and FCC-ee projections. Current collider data and HL-LHC projections only partially lift the degeneracy between $V_{ud}$ and possible New Physics effects. Instead, the FCC-ee fully decouples $V_{ud}$ from New Physics, recovering SM-level precision while significantly tightening the constraints on the SMEFT Wilson coefficients. This conclusion is robust against variations in flavour assumptions and theory uncertainties. Our results highlight the complementarity between future colliders and low-energy experiments, and pave the way towards a SMEFT-consistent interpretation of first-row CKM unitarity tests and the Cabibbo angle anomaly.

</details>


### [83] [Milky Way White Dwarfs as Inelastic Dark Matter Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31850)
*Javier F. Acevedo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes white dwarfs in the Galactic Center as probes for inelastic dark matter (IDM) annihilation, where IDM particles can be gravitationally captured on long-lived orbits without thermalizing, enabling external annihilation and detectable gamma-ray emission; constraints on IDM scattering cross-sections are derived from H.E.S.S. data, applied to dark photon-mediated and higgsino models with MeV-scale mass splittings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit white dwarfs as novel astrophysical targets for detecting inelastic dark matter via non-thermal gravitational capture and external annihilation — a regime not probed by conventional stellar or direct detection methods.

Method: Analytical modeling of IDM particle trajectories and capture in white dwarf gravitational potentials; calculation of bound-orbit population, external annihilation rate, and escaping gamma-ray flux; comparison with H.E.S.S. gamma-ray observations of the Galactic Center (within 1°); derivation of conservative cross-section limits assuming plausible local IDM density.

Result: Quantitative estimates of collective gamma-ray flux from external IDM annihilation around Galactic Center white dwarfs; derived upper limits on the IDM-nucleon scattering cross-section at ~TeV masses; mapped constraints onto specific benchmark models: dark photon-mediated IDM and higgsino-like IDM with ~MeV mass splittings.

Conclusion: White dwarfs provide a unique and sensitive probe for MeV-scale inelasticity in TeV-mass dark matter; current H.E.S.S. data already yield competitive, model-dependent constraints, demonstrating the viability of this astrophysical approach for testing non-standard dark matter interactions.

Abstract: We show that white dwarfs in the Galactic Center are a sensitive probe of inelastic dark matter (IDM) annihilation. IDM particles traversing a white dwarf may become gravitationally captured without fully settling in its interior if their energy drops below the kinematic threshold for inelastic scattering. In this regime, a fraction of the incoming particles remains bound on long-lived orbits that extend beyond the white dwarf's volume, enabling annihilation to partially proceed outside of the object. Some of the annihilation products subsequently escape, rather than being absorbed by the stellar material, producing a potentially detectable signal. We estimate the collective gamma-ray flux from the external annihilation sourced by these systems, as a function of the IDM parameters, and compare it to H.E.S.S. observations within the central degree of the galaxy. From this comparison, we derive tentative limits on the IDM scattering cross-section at the TeV mass scale, under largely conservative assumptions for the IDM density in this region. As a concrete application, we map these results into specific parameter constraints for dark photon-mediated and higgsino models with mass splittings at the MeV scale.

</details>


### [84] [Prompting Particle Physics: Tokenized Multi-modal Foundation Models for Combinatorially Many Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31862)
*Nilotpal Kakati,Daniel Murnane,Baran Hashemi,Samuel Klein,Jeffrey Krupa,Eilam Gross,Lukas Heinrich,Michael Kagan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a unified token-based model for collider experiment reconstruction and simulation, enabling multiple tasks (e.g., particle flow, detector simulation, charged energy subtraction) using shared weights and interpretable intermediate objects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify traditionally separate, specialized algorithms in collider reconstruction/simulation chains into a single, flexible, and interpretable model that retains physical intermediate representations (tracks, clusters, jets, etc.).

Method: Represents all jet-related objects (tracks, calorimeter cells, clusters, particles, jets) as tokens in a shared vocabulary; trains one transformer-based model to map any subset of input modalities to any subset of output modalities; supports both autoregressive and parallel decoding; uses modality-agnostic tokenisation for stable training.

Result: Both autoregressive and parallel models generate realistic, physically plausible reconstruction and simulation outputs; the autoregressive variant matches Geant4 simulation fidelity closely and outperforms a state-of-the-art particle flow algorithm on key jet reconstruction metrics (e.g., energy resolution, angular separation).

Conclusion: A single tokenised multimodal model can effectively replace multiple domain-specific algorithms in high-energy physics reconstruction and simulation pipelines while preserving interpretability and improving performance on standard benchmarks.

Abstract: Reconstruction and simulation at a collider experiment are long chains of specialised algorithms, each tuned to a single step. We explore how one model can serve many of those steps at once, while still producing the intermediate objects (tracks, calorimeter cells, clusters, particles and jets) that make the chain interpretable. To do so, we represent every object in a jet in a single shared token vocabulary and train one model to map any subset of these modalities to any other. A task is then only a choice of which modalities to provide and which to request: particle flow, detector simulation and charged energy subtraction are all directions through the same set of weights. We train over all modality combinations, with a decoder emitting tokens either autoregressively or in parallel. With tokenisation, both architectures train stably with little tuning. In evaluations, both models produce realistic reconstruction and simulation objects, with the autoregressive model particularly faithful to output from Geant4. The autoregressive model also outperforms a state-of-the-art particle flow algorithm on many typical jet reconstruction metrics.

</details>


### [85] [Landscape of pentaquark bound states with neural-network Variational Monte Carlo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32624)
*Jing-Zhe Song,Wei-Lin Wu,Shi-Lin Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses neural network variational Monte Carlo within a quark potential model to systematically study ground states of manifestly exotic, negative-parity pentaquarks; it identifies 21 shallow molecular bound states (no deeply bound ones), classifies their meson–baryon compositions, validates thresholds via scattering-state convergence, reproduces experimentally observed η_c N and η_c Λ scattering channels, and confirms all fully heavy pentaquarks are unbound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically identify manifestly exotic (non-mixing with conventional baryons) negative-parity pentaquark ground states—particularly those relevant to observed hidden-charm resonances—and resolve whether deeply bound or compact pentaquarks exist.

Method: Neural network variational Monte Carlo (NN-VMC) applied to the constituent quark potential model; exhaustive enumeration of 387 color–spin–isospin configurations for all manifestly exotic negative-parity pentaquark systems; binding energy computed relative to lowest baryon–meson threshold; cross-validation via convergence of unbound system energies to scattering thresholds.

Result: 21 shallowly bound molecular pentaquark candidates (binding energies 2–15 MeV); all bound states are meson–baryon molecules: QQqqq̄Q, ssqqq̄Q, Qsqqq̄Q, Qqqqq̄Q → heavy vector meson + baryon; QQqqq̄s, Qqqqq̄s → K meson + heavy baryon; unbound systems converge to S-wave baryon–meson thresholds; η_c N and η_c Λ scattering states reproduced; no bound fully heavy pentaquarks found.

Conclusion: No compact or deeply bound pentaquarks exist in this model; all exotic negative-parity pentaquark bound states are shallow hadronic molecules; results constrain experimental searches and support molecular interpretations of observed hidden-charm pentaquark signals.

Abstract: We present a systematic study of the ground states of pentaquark systems using the neural network variational Monte Carlo method within the constituent quark potential model. We investigate all manifestly exotic pentaquark systems with negative parity, which cannot mix with the conventional baryons through the creation and annihilation of light or strange quark pair. We exhaust 387 color, spin, and isospin configurations and identify 21 pentaquark bound state candidates with binding energies ranging from $2$ to $15$ MeV. A striking observation is that all of the bound states are shallowly bound molecular states, and a deeply bound pentaquark state does not exist. The $QQqq\bar{Q}$, $ssqq\bar{Q}$, $Qsqq\bar{Q}$ and $Qqqq\bar{Q}$ bound states are composed of a heavy vector meson and a baryon, while the $QQqq\bar{s}$ and $Qqqq\bar{s}$ bound states are composed of a $K$ meson and a heavy baryon. As a cross-validation, the ground-state energies of the unbound systems converge to the baryon--meson thresholds from above, which are consistent with the S-wave scattering states and provide a reliable baseline for the bound-state criterion. Especially, we reproduce the lowest scattering states $η_c N$ and $η_c Λ$ in the $qqqc\bar{c}$ and $qqsc\bar{c}$ channels respectively, where the hidden-charm pentaquark resonances were observed experimentally. We also find that all fully heavy pentaquark states are unbound. Our results provide valuable guidance for future pentaquark searches at LHCb, Belle II, and other experiments.

</details>


### [86] [The era of charmed resonance searches at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32054)
*Daniel Whiteson*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the feasibility and sensitivity of charm-tagged dijet resonance searches at the LHC, using ATLAS 13 TeV data (139 fb⁻¹), and finds that even with current loose charm-tagging performance, such searches can surpass inclusive dijet sensitivity for cc̄ resonances—subject to uncertainties in high-pₜ charm efficiency and simulated heavy-flavor background composition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: No dedicated search for dijet resonances decaying to charm-tagged jets has been performed, despite potential sensitivity gains from charm tagging analogous to those achieved with b-tagging; this study aims to establish feasibility and define required charm-tagging performance.

Method: Uses ATLAS 13 TeV dijet mass spectrum (139 fb⁻¹); predicts charm-tagged spectra via simulated jet-flavor fractions and parameterized charm-, bottom-, and light-jet tagging efficiencies; performs profile-likelihood search in exclusive one- and two-charm-tag categories.

Result: At the loosest working point of the current charm tagger, charm-tagged searches improve expected sensitivity to cc̄ resonances across the full mass range studied; however, quantitative sensitivity is limited by uncertainties in high-pₜ charm-tagging efficiency and simulated heavy-flavor background composition.

Conclusion: Charm-tagged dijet resonance searches are viable and potentially more sensitive than inclusive searches for cc̄ resonances, providing concrete performance targets for charm taggers and motivating dedicated experimental analyses at the LHC.

Abstract: The LHC dijet program has searched extensively for resonances decaying to inclusive jets and, more recently, to bottom-quark jets, where the sensitivity enhancement from suppression of the background outweighs the loss of signal efficiency in bottom-quark tagging. However, no dedicated search for dijet resonances containing charm-tagged jets has been performed. We study the charm-tagging performance required to make such searches competitive with the inclusive dijet search. Using the ATLAS 13 TeV dijet mass spectrum with 139 fb$^{-1}$, we predict the expected charm-tagged spectra from simulated jet-flavor fractions and parameterized charm-, bottom-, and light-jet tagging efficiencies, and estimate the sensitivity of a profile-likelihood search in exclusive one- and two-tag categories. At the loosest working point of the current tagger, charm-tagged searches improve the expected sensitivity to a resonance decaying to two charm quarks across the full resonance mass range considered, although the quantitative sensitivity depends on the poorly constrained high-$\pt$ behavior of charm-tagging efficiencies and on the simulated heavy-flavor composition of the dijet background. These results provide performance targets for charm taggers and motivate the development of dedicated charm-tagged dijet resonance searches at the LHC.

</details>


### [87] [Beam-polarization-enabled witness of four-dimensional entanglement in $Ω^-\barΩ^+$ pairs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32419)
*Qiu-Yan Zhang,Peng-Cheng Hong,Wei-Min Song*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates four-dimensional spin entanglement in Ω⁻Ω̄⁺ baryon pairs produced in e⁺e⁻ → ψ(3686), using beam polarization to control helicity coherence and the negativity criterion (N > 1) to witness entanglement; critical polarizations (~0.65 transverse, ~0.57 longitudinal) are identified, with near-maximal negativity (N_peak ≈ 1.50) under full transverse polarization, and angular sensitivity to relative phases is highlighted via constituent-quark models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the spin entanglement of spin-3/2 Ω⁻Ω̄⁺ baryon pairs requires the full four-dimensional spin Hilbert space — i.e., genuine high-dimensional (beyond qubit) entanglement — and to explore how beam polarization manipulates helicity interference to probe it.

Method: Applies the negativity entanglement witness (N > 1) to the helicity density matrix derived from BESIII-measured helicity parameters; analytically and numerically evaluates N as a function of transverse/longitudinal beam polarization; identifies critical polarizations and optimal production angles; compares predictions with constituent-quark model benchmarks to assess phase sensitivity.

Result: Critical beam polarizations for entanglement witnessing are found: ~0.65 (equal transverse) and ~0.57 (equal-and-opposite longitudinal); full transverse polarization yields near-maximal negativity (N_peak ≈ 1.50) with 60% angular coverage satisfying N > 1; destructive interference at optimal angles suppresses unequal-helicity terms, yielding nearly equal Schmidt coefficients; constituent-quark analysis shows angular extent of witness is more phase-sensitive than peak negativity.

Conclusion: The observed entanglement cannot be described within reduced spin subspaces — it genuinely requires the full 4D spin space of each spin-3/2 baryon; beam polarization provides a controllable handle on helicity coherence to generate and witness high-dimensional entanglement in heavy-baryon production.

Abstract: We study the entanglement structure of $Ω^-\barΩ^+$ pairs produced in $e^+e^-\toψ(3686)$, focusing on whether the entanglement requires the full four-dimensional spin space of each spin-$3/2$ baryon. Beam polarization controls the parent-state populations and coherence, thereby modifying helicity interference. Using the negativity criterion $\Neg>1$ and the measured BESIII helicity parameters, we find critical polarizations of about $0.65$ per beam for equal transverse polarization and $0.57$ per beam for equal-and-opposite longitudinal polarization. Full transverse polarization gives a near-maximal peak negativity, $\Neg_{\rm peak}\simeq1.50$, with a production-rate-weighted angular fraction of $0.60$ satisfying the witness. At the optimal production directions, destructive interference removes the unequal-helicity channels, while the near equality of the relevant equal-helicity amplitudes yields nearly equal Schmidt coefficients. Constituent-quark benchmarks further show that the angular extent of the witness is more sensitive to relative phases than the peak negativity. These results link polarization-controlled helicity interference to four-dimensional entanglement in high-spin baryon production.

</details>


### [88] [Heavy Inert Doublet Reconciles LZ and IceCube](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28819)
*Hitoshi Murayama,Bea Noether*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that inert doublet dark matter (IDM) with mass 11.3–20.3 TeV can simultaneously explain the LZ direct detection signal and satisfy IceCube neutrino constraints, leveraging velocity-dependent scattering and suppressed solar capture, while requiring large Higgs-IDM couplings compatible with electroweak precision data and Landau pole considerations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve tension between the tentative LZ direct detection signal and stringent IceCube neutrino limits on dark matter annihilation in the Sun, which disfavor conventional higgsino-like WIMPs.

Method: Analytical and phenomenological analysis of inert doublet model parameter space: computing direct detection rates (LZ), solar capture and annihilation rates (IceCube), freeze-out relic density, Landau pole constraints, and electroweak precision observables (S, T parameters).

Result: IDM with mass 11.3–20.3 TeV and enhanced Higgs coupling reconciles LZ signal and IceCube bounds; solar capture is suppressed due to high infall velocity reducing thermalization; electroweak precision tests remain satisfied; viable parameter space lies ~1 order of magnitude below the Landau pole.

Conclusion: Heavy inert doublet dark matter provides a consistent and testable explanation for the anomalous LZ events without violating IceCube or electroweak constraints, identifying a narrow but physically viable region in the IDM parameter space.

Abstract: We show that the inert doublet dark matter can reconcile the purported LZ direct detection signal and IceCube constraints. The preferred dark matter mass is 11.3--20.3~TeV, an order of magnitude heavier than the standard higgsino target. The smaller number density at a high mass can reproduce the LZ event given the exponential sensitivity to the velocity, while suppressing the capture rate in the Sun because of the higher infall velocity. On the other hand, the high mass requires a large coupling between the inert doublet and Higgs boson to have the correct annihilation cross section for the freeze-out. There is about an order of magnitude of validity range below the Landau pole, beyond which a UV completion is needed. Electroweak precision observables are below the experimental limits despite the large coupling.

</details>


### [89] [Escaping Solar Capture: Majoron mediated Elastic Dark Matter for the LZ 248~keV Event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32435)
*Salvador Centelles Chuliá,Ayan Chattaraj,Rahul Srivastava,Surabhi Srivastava*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper interprets LZ's 248 keVnr nuclear-recoil candidate as elastic scattering of dark matter mediated by a light Majoron—a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson from spontaneous lepton number violation—linking the signal to neutrino mass generation. It proposes a scotogenic UV completion where the lightest singlet Majorana fermion is DM, with couplings and mass generated via symmetry breaking; a vector-like up-type quark and type-I Dirac seesaw enable Majoron–up-quark coupling, yielding low-energy operator O₆ that fits the recoil spectrum while suppressing solar capture (especially on heavy elements and hydrogen) and reproducing thermal relic abundance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous 248 keVnr nuclear-recoil event observed by the LZ experiment using a theoretically motivated, minimal dark matter framework tied to neutrino mass generation—specifically, via a light Majoron arising from spontaneous lepton number breaking.

Method: Performs a model-independent analysis of Majoron-mediated DM–nucleus scattering, then constructs a concrete scotogenic ultraviolet completion: includes a light singlet Majorana fermion DM, a Majoron, a vector-like up-type quark, and a type-I Dirac seesaw in the quark sector to generate the required pseudoscalar DM–quark interaction; computes low-energy effective operator (O₆), evaluates nuclear recoil spectrum, solar capture rates (on H and heavy elements), and thermal relic density.

Result: The constructed model successfully reproduces the LZ 248 keVnr event via O₆’s momentum-dependent cross section without overpopulating lower-energy bins; suppresses solar capture on heavy elements (due to spin structure) and on hydrogen (due to kinematic mismatch and small momentum transfer); simultaneously yields correct thermal relic abundance.

Conclusion: A light Majoron-mediated scattering scenario within a scotogenic UV framework provides a consistent, testable explanation for the LZ anomaly—uniquely connecting dark matter phenomenology, neutrino mass origin, and direct detection signals while evading strong solar capture constraints.

Abstract: We interpret the $248~\mathrm{keV_{nr}}$ nuclear-recoil candidate reported by LZ through elastic dark matter scattering mediated by a light Majoron. The Majoron provides a natural origin for the required pseudoscalar interaction: it is the pseudo Nambu-Goldstone boson associated with spontaneous lepton number breaking, directly connecting the recoil signal to neutrino mass generation. After a model independent analysis of this idea, we present a concrete realization in a scotogenic ultraviolet completion in which the lightest singlet Majorana fermion is dark matter and acquires both its mass and Majoron coupling from the symmetry breaking dynamics. A vector like up type quark and a type-I Dirac seesaw in quark sector communicate the Majoron interaction to the up quark. At low energies, the resulting portal generates the nonrelativistic operator $\mathcal O_6$, whose momentum dependence reproduces the high-recoil event without overpopulating the lower energy bins. Its spin structure removes the dominant heavy element solar capture channels, while capture on hydrogen is suppressed by poor kinematic matching and small momentum transfer. This construction simultaneously reproduces the LZ recoil spectrum and the observed thermal relic abundance while strongly suppressing solar capture.

</details>


### [90] [Wide-Mass-Scanning-Range Setup and Phase-Resolved Protocol for Axion Dark Matter Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32554)
*A. Cottet,T. Kontos*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a quantum-enhanced interferometric method using non-linear cavities and gyromagnetic modes to detect axion dark matter, improving sensitivity by ≥4 orders of magnitude and mass coverage by ≥2 orders over standard haloscopes—applicable also to dark photons and galactic masers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Overcoming limitations of conventional power-based haloscopes in sensitivity and mass range for axion dark matter detection.

Method: Interferometric measurement of axion amplitude and phase at the quantum limit using a non-linear cavity; integration of gyromagnetic modes as broadband transducers compatible with existing haloscope designs.

Result: Projected improvement of ≥4 orders of magnitude in figure of merit and ≥2 orders in accessible axion mass window; broad applicability to dark photon and galactic maser searches.

Conclusion: The proposed paradigm enables significantly faster and wider-ranging searches for ultralight dark matter and related cosmological signals, leveraging quantum metrology and scalable transduction.

Abstract: We propose a paradigm for quantum enhanced axion dark matter search, which does not rely on power measurements. We propose to measure directly the axion amplitude and phase in an interferometric protocol at the quantum limit, using a non-linear cavity. In addition, we introduce gyromagnetic modes as wide mass range transducers for axion signals compatible with standard haloscope designs. We expect this scheme to offer an improvement of at least 4 orders of magnitude in figure of merit and at least 2 orders of magnitude in mass window with respect to standard haloscopes. Owing to its generality, our proposed protocol has the potential to speed up axion search but also the search for dark photons or other cosmological objects, such as galactic masers.

</details>


### [91] [Dissecting the moat regime at low energies II: Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32563)
*Fabian Rennecke,Shi Yin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how the 'moat regime'—a feature in the quark-meson model—affects meson and quark correlation functions and the quark-antiquark potential in low-energy QCD. It identifies moat-induced oscillations as distinct from Friedel oscillations, arising from unphysical-sheet poles; shows broad mesonic sensitivity (including vector mesons and η), enhancing spacelike spectral functions, while quarks remain insensitive—potentially shifting the chiral phase boundary and critical endpoint.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the moat regime—a nonperturbative infrared feature in the quark-meson model—affects hadronic and quark correlations, especially near high density and zero temperature, and to assess its implications for QCD phase structure, including the chiral critical endpoint.

Method: Uses the two-flavor quark-meson model within the random-phase approximation (RPA) to compute meson and quark correlation functions, analyze analytic structure of the quark-antiquark potential, and study spectral functions across physical and unphysical Riemann sheets.

Result: Moat-induced oscillations in the quark-antiquark potential are shown to originate from poles on unphysical Riemann sheets—not Friedel oscillations; vector mesons and η, beyond pions and sigma, exhibit enhanced spacelike spectral weight; quark propagators remain largely unaffected; thus, the chiral phase boundary and critical endpoint may shift even without inhomogeneous instabilities.

Conclusion: The moat regime is a qualitatively important, previously underappreciated feature influencing mesonic dynamics and QCD phase structure at high baryon density, with negligible direct impact on quark propagation—highlighting the need to incorporate such nonperturbative infrared effects in effective models of dense matter.

Abstract: We study the effects of the moat regime on meson and quark correlation functions and the quark-antiquark potential in low-energy QCD, building on the two-flavor quark meson model in a random-phase approximation. We investigate the relation between the moat regime and Friedel oscillations in the quark-antiquark potential at large density and zero temperature. As it turns out, oscillations from the moat regime are a distinct phenomenon, arising from poles on unphysical Riemann sheets in this region. Furthermore, we find that in addition to pions and the sigma meson, also other mesons, including vector mesons and the eta, are sensitive to the moat regime. This leads in particular to an enhancement of the meson spectral function in the spacelike region. The quarks, on the other hand, appear to be essentially insensitive. This can have far-reaching consequences for the phase diagram, as for example the chiral phase boundary, including the location of the critical endpoint, can be affected in the moat regime even in absence of inhomogeneous instabilities.

</details>


### [92] [Identification of $D_{s1}(2933)$ as the $2P_{1}$ state in the quark model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32586)
*Jun Wang,Qiang Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the internal structure of the newly observed charm-strange meson $D_{s1}(2933)^+$, interpreting it as the $2P_1$ excited state of the $c\bar{s}$ system within the quark model, incorporating coupled-channel corrections from multiple intermediate hadronic channels; it predicts a partner state at 3012 MeV, a mixing angle of -12.9°, and a three-body branching ratio consistent with LHCb data, supporting the $2P_1$ assignment with significant final-state interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the nature of the newly observed $D_{s1}(2933)^+$ meson reported by LHCb in $B^0 \to D^+ D^- K^+ \pi^-$, specifically whether it corresponds to the predicted $2P_1$ excited state of the $c\bar{s}$ system, and to assess the role of coupled-channel effects and final-state interactions.

Method: Quark model analysis treating $D_{s1}(2933)^+$ as the $2P_1$ $c\bar{s}$ state; evaluation of coupled-channel corrections to the ${}^{1}P_1$--${}^{3}P_1$ mass matrix via two-point self-energy loops involving $DK^*$, $D^*K$, $D^*K^*$, $D_s\phi$, $D_s^*\phi$, and $D_s^*\eta$ intermediate states; fitting the cutoff parameter to reproduce the experimental mass; computing the three-body branching ratio fraction $R_{\text{th}}$ for $D_{s1}(2933) \to D^+ K^+ \pi^-$ via $DK^*$ and $D_2^*K$ intermediates.

Result: A cutoff scale of ~0.82 GeV is obtained; a $2P_1'$ partner state is predicted at 3012 MeV; the mixing angle is $-12.9^\circ$, deviating significantly from heavy-quark ideal mixing; the theoretical three-body branching ratio fraction $R_{\text{th}} = 1.98$ agrees with the experimental value $R_{\text{exp}} = 1.27^{+1.29}_{-0.95}$ within uncertainties.

Conclusion: The $D_{s1}(2933)^+$ is consistently interpreted as the $2P_1$ excited state of the $c\bar{s}$ system, with coupled-channel effects—particularly final-state interactions—playing a crucial role in its mass and decay properties.

Abstract: We investigate the internal structure of the newly observed charm-strange meson $D_{s1}(2933)^{+}$, which was reported by the LHCb Collaboration in $B^{0}\to D^{+}D^{-}K^{+}π^{-}$, in the quark model by treating it as the $2P_{1}$ state of the $c\bar{s}$ system. The coupled-channel corrections to the ${}^{1}P_1$--${}^{3}P_1$ mass matrix are evaluated through the two-point self-energy loops involving the $DK^{*}$, $D^{*}K$, $D^{*}K^{*}$, $D_{s}φ$, $D_{s}^{*}φ$ and $D_{s}^{*}η$ intermediate channels. Requiring the corrected eigenvalue to reproduce the measured mass yields a cutoff scale of about $0.82~\mathrm{GeV}$, a $2P_{1}^{\prime}$ partner state at $3012~\mathrm{MeV}$, and a mixing angle of $-12.9^{\circ}$ that deviates significantly from the heavy-quark ideal-mixing angle. Moreover, the predicted three-body branching ratio fraction $R_{\text{th}}=1.98$ for $D_{s1}(2933)\to D^{+}K^{+}π^{-}$ via the $DK^{*}$ and $D_{2}^{*}K$ intermediate states is consistent with the experimental value $R_{\text{exp}}=1.27^{+1.29}_{-0.95}$ within uncertainties. These results support the assignment of $D_{s1}(2933)$ as the $2P_{1}$ excited state of the $c\bar{s}$ system with final state interactions.

</details>


### [93] [Quantum tomography at the Electron-Ion Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32800)
*Michael Fucilla,Yuxun Guo,Yoshitaka Hatta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes quantum state tomography of quark-antiquark pairs at the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) using azimuthal angular correlations of hadron and dihadron pairs, enabling measurement of the full spin density matrix and probing entanglement and Bell-nonlocality—particularly highlighting maximal entanglement in the heavy-quark sector via deviation from the standard Collins $\cos(\phi+\phi')$ modulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable experimental access to quantum information properties (e.g., entanglement, Bell-nonlocality) of quark-antiquark pairs in high-energy collisions, leveraging the unique capabilities of the future Electron-Ion Collider.

Method: Quantum state tomography via azimuthal angular correlations between hadrons and dihadrons produced through Collins and dihadron fragmentation functions; reconstruction of the full spin density matrix from measurable azimuthal modulations.

Result: All components of the spin density matrix for massless and massive $q\\bar{q}$ pairs are accessible; maximal entanglement in the heavy-quark sector manifests as a strong deviation from the canonical Collins $\cos(\\phi+\\phi')$ angular dependence.

Conclusion: Quantum state tomography of $q\\bar{q}$ pairs is feasible at the EIC using fragmentation-based azimuthal correlations, opening a new avenue to test foundational quantum phenomena—entanglement and nonlocality—in QCD processes.

Abstract: We initiate the study of quantum state tomography---the experimental reconstruction of the complete spin density matrix---of massless and massive quark-antiquark pairs produced at the future Electron-Ion Collider (EIC). We show that all components of the spin density matrix can be accessed through azimuthal angular correlations between hadron and dihadron pairs generated by the Collins and dihadron fragmentation functions. This enables the experimental investigation of a variety of quantum information properties at the EIC, such as entanglement and Bell-nonlocality. We predict that the recently discovered maximal entanglement in the heavy-quark sector is signaled by a strong modification of the usual Collins angular dependence $\cos(φ+φ')$

</details>


### [94] [Machine learning for the LHC physics program: a 2025-2026 stocktake](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32874)
*Jesse Thaler*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews ML applications in LHC physics (May 2025–May 2026) using an AI-driven living review process, concluding that ML has transitioned from a tool-building research area to mission-critical infrastructure—integral to ATLAS/CMS physics results and legacy data reuse. It identifies six key claims spanning infrastructure adoption, convergence of simulation-based inference and foundation models, emergence of AI agents, rising trust/uncertainty focus, slowing of certain subfields (e.g., equivariance), and theory ML’s capability leap.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically assess the evolution and maturity of machine learning in high-energy physics (HEPML) over a defined 12-month period, moving beyond anecdotal or manual surveys toward scalable, reproducible, AI-assisted scientific meta-analysis.

Method: An agentic AI pipeline processed 569 post-May-2025 HEPML papers from the HEPML Living Review: abstracts were ranked by citations, recency, theme, and collaboration; 103 papers (95 recent + 8 baseline) were read in full; structured notes were generated, synthesized into six claims, validated by independent reviewer agents, and cross-checked against source material.

Result: Six empirically grounded claims emerged: (1) ML is now essential infrastructure for LHC physics; (2) simulation-based inference and foundation models are converging; (3) AI agents represent a novel, rapidly growing but unvalidated frontier (47 papers, no adopted measurements); (4) trust-related topics (uncertainty, calibration, interpretability) dominate ~20% of recent work; (5) slowdowns in equivariance and model-specific phenomenology reflect successful absorption into standard tooling; (6) theory ML achieved threshold-level capabilities across multiple domains.

Conclusion: HEPML has matured into foundational infrastructure, with AI not only accelerating analysis but reshaping how physics knowledge is produced, validated, and archived—raising critical questions about agency, verification, and epistemic responsibility in AI-augmented science.

Abstract: The first sentence of this abstract--and the introduction to these proceedings--was authored by a human, but the bulk of this document was generated by an agentic AI system. In this talk, I take stock of machine learning (ML) for the LHC physics program over the twelve months from May 2025 to May 2026. The corpus is the HEPML Living Review, split at May 2025 into 1,756 earlier papers and 569 later ones. An AI pipeline surveyed the 569 abstracts, ranked them by citations, recency, theme, and collaboration involvement, and read 103 papers in full (95 from after the split, plus 8 earlier baseline papers), producing a structured note for each. The notes were then synthesized into six claims about the state of the field, checked by independent reviewer agents, and re-verified against the source papers. The headline claim is that (1) ML for high-energy physics (HEPML) stopped being a research area that builds tools and became infrastructure that the LHC physics program depends on: ATLAS and CMS now publish physics results that depend on neural networks, and the archived ALEPH data have re-entered production. The other five claims are: (2) simulation-based inference and foundation models are two revolutions starting to merge; (3) AI agents are the genuinely new front, with 47 papers in twelve months and no adopted measurement yet; (4) "do we trust it?" is the fastest-growing agenda, with one recent paper in five about uncertainty, calibration, or interpretability; (5) what is slowing down is informative, since equivariance was absorbed into a tool and model-specific phenomenology ceded ground to model-agnostic searches; and (6) theory ML crossed a capability threshold in multiple research areas. I close with what is settled, what is incoming, and what is open, and briefly discuss the concerns raised by this way of working with AI.

</details>


### [95] [Constraining Ultra-Light Vector Bosons via Solar Neutrino Oscillations: The Gauged $L_μ- L_τ$ Model and Prospects for JUNO and XLZD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33877)
*Ilídio Lopes*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} extension of the Standard Model with an ultra-light Z′ boson, using solar neutrino data to constrain its mass < 5.7×10⁻¹⁷ eV and coupling; it improves χ² over standard 3ν oscillations and links to ultra-light dark matter and small-scale structure problems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an independent phenomenological framework for solar neutrino anomalies, motivated historically by the muon g−2 discrepancy and recently by potential resolutions to small-scale structure problems in cold dark matter simulations via ultra-light vector bosons.

Method: Introduces a gauged U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} model with anomaly-free ultra-light Z′; computes its effective matter potential in the Sun via photon–Z′ kinetic mixing; performs global fit to Borexino, SNO, and Super-Kamiokande solar neutrino data using an updated standard solar model and MSW flavor evolution.

Result: Mass bound m_{Z′} < 5.7×10⁻¹⁷ eV; best-fit coupling η₀ ≈ −2 yields χ²_ν = 2.7 (vs. 3.1 in standard 3ν); derived effective four-fermion coupling g_{Z′}²ε/m_{Z′}² ≈ 6.6×10⁻²³ eV⁻²; projects improved constraints from JUNO (operational since Aug 2025) and XLZD.

Conclusion: The U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} Z′ boson—acting as an ultra-light dark matter candidate—can meaningfully modify solar neutrino oscillations and is tightly constrained by current data; future high-precision experiments will decisively test this parameter space, bridging neutrino physics, particle cosmology, and galaxy formation.

Abstract: We present an extension of the Standard Model based upon the gauged $U(1)_{L_μ- L_τ}$ symmetry, featuring an ultra-light vector boson that couples to the difference of muon and tau lepton numbers. This anomaly-free construction, historically motivated by the muon's anomalous magnetic moment, is employed here as an independent phenomenological framework for solar neutrino physics. Through kinetic mixing with the photon, the $Z^\prime$ boson generates an effective matter potential within the solar interior, thereby modifying neutrino flavour oscillations via the Mikheyev--Smirnov--Wolfenstein mechanism. Utilising recent solar neutrino measurements from Borexino, SNO, and Super-Kamiokande, together with an up-to-date standard solar model, we constrain the boson mass to be below $5.7\times 10^{-17}\,{\rm eV}$, a potential ultra-light dark matter candidate. The optimal model, with coupling parameter $η_o \approx -2$, yields $χ_ν^2 = 2.7$, compared with $χ_ν^2 = 3.1$ for the standard three-flavour scenario. This best fit corresponds to an effective four-fermion coupling $g_{Z^\prime}^2 \varepsilon / m_{Z^\prime}^2 \approx 6.6 \times 10^{-23}\,{\rm eV}^{-2}$, approximately $5.7$ times the Fermi constant, equivalent for $\varepsilon \approx 1$ and a reference mediator mass of $1\,{\rm eV}$ to a gauge coupling $g_{Z^\prime} \approx 8\times 10^{-12}$. We also present projections for forthcoming experiments: the Jiangmen Underground Neutrino Observatory (JUNO), operating since August 2025 with world-leading precision on oscillation parameters, together with the XLZD consortium, should substantially refine these constraints. Such ultra-light particles may address long-standing discrepancies in galaxy simulations within the cold dark matter paradigm, whilst terrestrial long-baseline neutrino experiments provide complementary probes of this parameter space.

</details>


### [96] [$ \bar K^0 d $ Bound State and Correlation Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32986)
*M. Bayar,P. Encarnación,A. Feijoo,E. Oset*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the $\bar{K}^0d$ system using a unitary fixed-center approximation, predicting a bound state interpretable as a $Λ(1380)N$ configuration—complementary to the $K^-pp$ state linked to the higher $Λ(1405)$ pole—and computes its correlation function as a Coulomb-free observable testable in ALICE.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the nature of the $Λ(1405)$ resonance and its possible manifestations in few-body kaonic systems, particularly seeking complementary configurations to the $K^-pp$ state and identifying robust, experimentally accessible signatures.

Method: A new formulation of the fixed-center approximation (FCA) is employed for the $\bar{K}^0d$ system, explicitly preserving elastic unitarity; scattering amplitude and bound-state properties are derived; $\bar{K}^0d$ correlation function is computed; calculations are cross-checked with two distinct $\bar{K}N$ interaction models.

Result: A bound state below threshold is predicted in the $\bar{K}^0d$ system, interpreted as a $Λ(1380)N$ configuration; scattering parameters are consistent with recent $K^-d$ data; the correlation function shows a distinct peak structure due to the bound state; results are robust across two $\bar{K}N$ models.

Conclusion: The $\bar{K}^0d$ system hosts a bound state tied to the lower $Λ(1405)$ pole's dynamics, offering a complementary few-body manifestation to $K^-pp$; its correlation function provides a clean, Coulomb-free probe for experimental validation—e.g., in ALICE—supporting the role of chiral dynamics and unitarity in kaonic nuclear systems.

Abstract: We study the $\bar{K}^0d$ system using a new formulation of the fixed-center approximation that preserves elastic unitarity. The resulting scattering amplitude predicts a bound state below threshold, which can be interpreted as a $Λ(1380)N$ configuration complementary to the $K^-pp$ state associated with the higher $Λ(1405)$ pole. The interaction is attractive, with scattering parameters of the same order as recent $K^-d$ determinations. We further calculate the $\bar{K}^0d$ correlation function, which exhibits a characteristic structure associated with the bound state and provides a Coulomb-free observable that can be tested in future ALICE measurements. Results obtained with two different $\bar{K}N$ models are very similar, indicating the robustness of the predictions against the choice of the underlying interaction.

</details>


### [97] [Gottfried-Jackson polarization filters for holographic dilaton and transverse-traceless gluon responses in near-threshold $J/ψ$ photoproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33908)
*Arkadiy I. Syamtomov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Hatta-Yang holographic graviton-dilaton amplitude for near-threshold J/ψ photoproduction, identifying dilaton-null spin-projection channels and using polarization observables to isolate tensor structures; it finds the original fixed-shape holographic model fails to globally describe modern GlueX/CLAS12 data, so polarization measurements serve as tests of the production tensor rather than model predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the validity and predictive power of the Hatta-Yang holographic graviton-dilaton amplitude for near-threshold J/ψ photoproduction by examining its spin structure and confronting it with modern experimental data.

Method: Analysis of the holographic graviton-dilaton amplitude in the Gottfried-Jackson frame; identification of dilaton-null spin-projection channels (m=0, m=−λ_γ); decomposition of amplitudes under photon polarization; derivation of spin-density-matrix elements sensitive to tensor-tensor (TT) and dilaton-TT interference; comparison with GlueX and CLAS12 differential cross sections.

Result: The m=0 and m=−λ_γ amplitudes are dilaton-null; for out-of-plane photon polarization, m=0 isolates A_g(t)+B_g(t) and requires nucleon spin transfer; beam-polarization observables yield TT-only constraints and a bilinear encoding linear dilaton-TT interference; the fixed-shape holographic amplitude cannot fit full near-threshold GlueX/CLAS12 data with a single normalization.

Conclusion: Polarization observables act as stringent, model-independent tests of the production tensor structure, but the original Hatta-Yang amplitude lacks global descriptive power—thus, current data constrain the reaction mechanism beyond the minimal holographic ansatz.

Abstract: Within the Hatta-Yang holographic graviton-dilaton amplitude for near-threshold $J/ψ$ photoproduction, we identify exact dilaton-null spin-projection channels in the Gottfried-Jackson basis. The $m=0$ and $m=-λ_γ$ amplitudes receive no dilaton contribution; for photon polarization normal to the reaction plane the $m=0$ channel further isolates $A_g(t)+B_g(t)$ and requires nucleon spin transfer. Standard beam-polarization spin-density-matrix elements then furnish TT-only controls and a bilinear containing linear dilaton-TT interference. A comparison with modern GlueX and CLAS12 cross sections shows that the original fixed-shape holographic amplitude cannot describe the full near-threshold data with one overall normalization, making the polarization filters tests of the production tensor rather than predictions of a globally validated reaction model.

</details>


### [98] [Resonantly-Enhanced Baryogenesis through Asymmetric Capture by Primordial Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33031)
*Fayez Abu-Ajamieh,Xiaoyan Huo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a resonant CP-violation mechanism involving two nearly degenerate unstable fermions (Y₁, Y₂) to enhance baryon asymmetry generation via primordial black hole (PBH) capture—overcoming previous loop-suppression limitations—and achieves agreement with the observed baryon asymmetry (η_B = 8.72×10⁻¹¹) after including thermal and continuous evaporation effects; it also predicts detectable MHz-frequency gravitational-wave signatures from PBH-forming primordial fluctuations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous PBH-based baryogenesis mechanisms produce insufficient baryon asymmetry due to loop-suppressed CP-violating scattering asymmetries; the paper seeks a resonant enhancement of CP violation that avoids loop suppression while preserving baryon number conservation at the Lagrangian level.

Method: Introduces a mixed system of two nearly degenerate unstable fermions Y₁, Y₂ with mass splitting ∼ decay widths; leverages coherent Y₁–Y₂ state production and CP-asymmetric return/loss decays to generate O(0.1) integrated decay probability difference; embeds this resonant CP source into PBH capture-transport dynamics, incorporating thermal corrections and continuous PBH evaporation/entropy production; computes resulting η_B and associated scalar-induced gravitational-wave spectra from PBH-forming curvature perturbations.

Result: Achieves η_B = 8.72×10⁻¹¹ (matching observed BAU) after full thermal + continuous-evaporation treatment; finds residual η_B^no evap ≈ 9.1×10⁻¹² even without PBH evaporation; predicts peak gravitational-wave frequency f ∼ 0.16–0.17 MHz with h²Ω_GW^peak ∼ (3–7)×10⁻⁸, detectable by U-DECIGO at SNR > 10 under ideal sensitivity.

Conclusion: Resonant CP violation in a quasi-degenerate fermion system provides a viable, non-loop-suppressed pathway for PBH-mediated baryogenesis consistent with observation; the model yields testable high-frequency gravitational-wave predictions linked to the same primordial fluctuations seeding PBHs.

Abstract: Primordial black holes (PBHs) can generate the Baryon Asymmetry of the Universe (BAU) through asymmetric capture of baryon-charge-carrying particles, but previous realizations of this mechanism typically yield an asymmetry well below the observed value because the relevant CP-violating scattering asymmetry is loop suppressed. We show that this limitation can be overcome through resonant CP violation in a mixed system of two nearly degenerate unstable fermions, $Y_{1,2}$, whose mass splitting is comparable to their decay widths. Rare production of the coherent $Y_1$--$Y_2$ state, followed by CP-asymmetric return and loss decays, generates an $\mathcal{O}(0.1)$ difference in integrated decay probabilities while preserving the global baryon charge at the Lagrangian level. Embedding this resonant source in the PBH-capture transport system yields the observed baryon asymmetry. We first reproduce the mechanism in a zero-temperature, sudden-evaporation treatment, which gives $η_B^{T=0}\simeq2.37\times10^{-10}\simeq2.72 η_B^{\rm obs}$. We find that after including thermal corrections and continuous PBH evaporation and entropy production, the resulting BAU becomes $η_B = 8.72\times 10^{-11}$ in agreement with the observed value. An interesting finding of the model is that even if PBH absorption remains active, we would still have BAU $η_{B}^{\text{no evap}} \sim 9.1 \times 10^{-12} \sim 0.105 η_B^{\rm obs}$. We further investigate gravitational-wave signatures associated with the primordial fluctuations responsible for PBH formation. The corresponding scalar-induced gravitational-wave spectra peak at $f \sim 0.16\text{--}0.17~\mathrm{MHz}$, with $h^2Ω_{\rm GW}^{\rm peak} \sim (3\text{--}7)\times 10^{-8}$. Under idealized sensitivity assumptions, U-DECIGO can achieve signal-to-noise ratios above 10 for the benchmark spectra.

</details>


### [99] [Meson photoproduction at Jefferson Lab: from two-meson final states to meson-baryon spin-density matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35045)
*Vincent Mathieu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent progress in meson photoproduction, particularly ηπ and ρ⁻Δ⁺⁺ channels, to probe exotic hybrid mesons like π₁(1600); it derives the full angular distribution for γp → ρ⁻Δ⁺⁺ with linearly polarized photons, identifying 56 spin-density matrix elements, reducible to 14 under Regge pole factorization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To search for exotic hybrid mesons—especially the π₁(1600), predicted to decay dominantly to b₁π—by studying photoproduction reactions with favorable quantum numbers and recoil baryons (e.g., Δ⁺⁺), using high-precision data from GlueX and CLAS.

Method: Derivation of the complete angular distribution for γp → ρ⁻Δ⁺⁺ with linearly polarized photons; construction of the 56 double-index spin-density matrix elements encoding spin correlations between ρ and Δ decays; analysis under factorization assumptions (e.g., single Regge pole exchange) to reduce parameter count.

Result: The full intensity distribution for γp → ρ⁻Δ⁺⁺ is expressed in terms of 56 spin-density matrix elements; under Regge pole factorization, it simplifies to a product of independent ρ and Δ decay distributions depending on only 14 parameters.

Conclusion: The formalism provides a rigorous framework for analyzing ρΔ final states as proxies for b₁πΔ channels in exotic meson searches; successful application hinges on experimental extraction of the reduced 14-parameter set, enabling tests of production mechanisms and sensitivity to non-Regge or exotic contributions.

Abstract: Recent progress in the understanding of meson photoproduction, obtained from the joint effort of the Joint Physics Analysis Center (JPAC) and the GlueX and CLAS experiments at Jefferson Lab, is reviewed. The photoproduction of the $ηπ$ system is now well understood, from the $a_2(1320)$ resonance region to the high-mass region dominated by double Regge exchanges. Since the lightest hybrid meson, the $π_1(1600)$, is expected to decay predominantly into $b_1π$, reactions with a recoiling $Δ^{++}$ are promising channels for exotic searches. As a first step towards $γp \to b_1 πΔ^{++}$, the complete angular distribution of $γp \to ρ^-Δ^{++}$ with a linearly polarized photon beam is derived. It involves 56 double-index spin-density matrix elements, which describe the correlations between the $ρ$ and the $Δ$ decays. When the production amplitudes factorize, as is the case for the exchange of a single Regge pole, the intensity reduces to the product of the standard $ρ$ and $Δ$ decay distributions and depends on only 14 parameters.

</details>


### [100] [Stress in static force fields and the sign of the D-term](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33151)
*Adam Freese*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the sign of the D-term in hadrons, showing it is determined by the spin of the mediating force field (negative for spin-even, positive for spin-odd), not mechanical stability; hadronic negative D-terms arise from spin-one gluons and may be explained by color confinement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the persistent but misleading speculation that the negative D-term of hadrons reflects a mechanical stability condition, especially given counterexamples like the hydrogen atom's positive D-term.

Method: Analytical derivation of D-term contributions from static abelian force fields, classified by field spin parity (spin-even vs. spin-odd), and comparison with quantum field signatures and binding mechanisms.

Result: Spin-even fields yield negative D-term contributions; spin-odd fields (e.g., electromagnetic) yield positive ones; identical potentials from different-spin fields produce identical bound-state wave functions yet opposite D-term signs—thus disproving stability as the origin.

Conclusion: The D-term sign is dictated by the spin of the mediating field, not stability; the negative D-term of hadrons stems from spin-one gluon exchange and likely requires color confinement for full explanation.

Abstract: The sign of the D-term is negative for a variety of hadrons, including the proton and pion. Speculation that this is the result of a mechanical stability condition has persisted despite counterexamples, including the positive D-term of the hydrogen atom. In this work, I explore how the sign of the D-term is influenced by the stress carried by static abelian force fields, finding the contributions from spin-even fields to be negative and the contributions from spin-odd fields (such as the electromagnetic field) to be positive. These contributions correlate with the signs of the D-terms of the fields' respective quanta. Since fields with different spin can produce identical potentials---thus resulting in equally-stable composite systems with identical wave functions---the sign of the D-term has nothing to do with stability. Since hadrons are bound by spin-one gluons, their negative D-terms appear atypical and require explanation. I briefly speculate on how color flux confinement could produce a negative hadronic D-term.

</details>


### [101] [Probing Solar Chameleons with XENONnT Ionization-Only Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33198)
*Guan-Wen Yuan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents the first search for solar chameleons using low-energy S2-only data from XENONnT (7.83 tonne-year), probing screened scalar field models of dark energy; it sets an upper limit log₁₀β_eff < −3.64 in the disformal-dominated regime, establishing ionization-only sub-keV data as a new channel despite weaker constraints due to falling signal rate and rising background at low energies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe screened scalar field models (e.g., chameleons) linking dark energy to laboratory-scale physics, leveraging solar production as a direct source and extending sensitivity into the sub-keV electronic-recoil regime via ionization-only (S2-only) detection in liquid-xenon detectors.

Method: Analysis of XENONnT’s 7.83 tonne-year ionization-only (S2-only) dataset across science runs SR0–SR2; modeling of predicted solar chameleon-induced electronic-recoil spectrum folded through run-dependent detector response; joint binned likelihood over seven corrected-S2 (cS2) bins; Bayesian sampling of five-dimensional parameter space {βₑ, βᵧ, Mₑ, Λ, n}; explicit modeling of few-electron response and instrumental backgrounds down to ~0.04 keVₑₑ.

Result: Upper limit log₁₀β_eff < −3.64 (where β_eff ≡ βᵧ(eV/Mₑ)⁴) in the disformal-dominated regime; achieved recoil-energy threshold ~0.04 keVₑₑ — lower than previous XENONnT electronic-recoil analysis — but yielding weaker constraints due to rapid decline of disformal absorption rate and increasing background at low energies.

Conclusion: This work establishes low-energy ionization-only data as a novel experimental channel for testing solar chameleons and screened dark-energy models; while current limits are less stringent than prior analyses, it opens a path for future improvements with better low-energy background control and response modeling.

Abstract: Screened scalar fields provide a possible connection between dark energy and laboratory-scale new physics while remaining compatible with existing fifth-force constraints. Solar production offers a direct probe of these models, and ionization-only measurements extend the sensitivity of liquid-xenon detectors into the sub-keV regime. We present the first search for solar chameleons using low-energy S2-only data, based on the 7.83 tonne-year XENONnT ionization-only data set. The predicted electronic-recoil spectrum is folded through the run-dependent detector response and tested directly in corrected-S2 (cS2) space. A joint binned likelihood combines the seven cS2 bins of science runs SR0, SR1, and SR2. We sample the five-dimensional parameter space $\{β_e,β_γ,M_e,Λ,n\}$ and derive posterior constraints on the conformal and disformal electron couplings. The data extend the recoil-energy reach to approximately $0.04~{\rm keV}_{ee}$, where the few-electron detector response and instrumental backgrounds must be modeled explicitly. In the disformal-dominated regime, the result is expressed in terms of $β_{\rm eff}\equivβ_γ({\rm eV}/M_e)^4$. We obtain the upper limit $\log_{10}β_{\rm eff}<-3.64$. Although the present analysis reaches a lower energy threshold than our previous XENONnT electronic-recoil analysis, the resulting constraint is weaker, primarily because the disformal absorption rate decreases rapidly toward low recoil energies, where the background rate also becomes larger. This analysis establishes low-energy ionization data as a new experimental channel for testing solar chameleons and screened dark-energy models.

</details>


### [102] [Thermal masses in the Standard Model at three loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33448)
*Mikael Chala*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes Higgs thermal mass and electroweak/colour Debye masses at full O(g⁶) accuracy in dimensional reduction, introducing novel techniques for evaluating previously intractable mixed-signature sum-integrals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve higher-order precision in finite-temperature quantum field theory predictions—particularly for electroweak phase transition and non-perturbative lattice studies—requiring accurate thermal mass parameters beyond known orders.

Method: Dimensional reduction combined with systematic weak-coupling expansion; development and application of new analytical methods to evaluate mixed-signature Matsubara sum-integrals at O(g⁶).

Result: Full O(g⁶) expressions for the Higgs thermal mass and the electroweak and colour Debye mass parameters in the high-temperature plasma of the Standard Model.

Conclusion: The computation closes a long-standing gap in thermal perturbation theory, enabling more reliable continuum extrapolations in dimensionally reduced lattice simulations and improving theoretical control over thermodynamic and transport properties near the electroweak scale.

Abstract: We compute the Higgs thermal mass and the electroweak and colour Debye mass parameters to full $\mathcal{O}(g^6)$ in dimensional reduction, where $g$ represents a gauge coupling or the top Yukawa. We employ new methods to evaluate previously unknown mixed-signature sum-integrals.

</details>


### [103] [Mono-Z signal for dark matter searches at future lepton colliders and cosmological interpretation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33514)
*Anupam Ghosh,Partha Kumar Paul,Abhik Sarkar,Rituparna Ghosh,Rachit Sharma,and,Subhaditya Bhattacharya*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies mono-Z signatures at future e+e- colliders within the DMEFT framework for scalar, fermionic, and vector DM up to dimension-six operators; it identifies viable parameter space using relic density, direct/indirect detection, Higgs invisible decay, and LHC constraints, then evaluates discovery prospects with full simulation—including ISR/FSR, detector effects, and both Z decay modes—finding strong sensitivity for fermionic dipole, scalar leptophilic, and vector Higgs/gauge-portal operators up to multi-TeV scales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe dark matter candidates via mono-Z signatures at future electron-positron colliders, leveraging the clean environment and precise kinematics to complement LHC and astroparticle searches, while systematically covering all renormalizable and dimension-six DMEFT operators for scalar, fermionic, and vector DM.

Method: Classified dimension-six DMEFT operators by production mechanism; imposed cosmological (Planck relic density) and experimental constraints (XENONnT, Fermi-LAT, Higgs invisible width, recast LHC mono-Z); performed full simulation of e+e- → DM + Z including ISR/FSR, GEANT4-based detector simulation, reconstruction of leptonic/hadronic Z decays; used multivariate observables—missing energy, p_T^miss, recoil mass, Z pseudorapidity—and beam polarization for background suppression.

Result: Fermionic dipole, scalar leptophilic, and vector Higgs-portal and gauge-portal operators survive all constraints and yield >5σ discovery reach at √s = 250–1000 GeV with 1–5 ab⁻¹ luminosity, probing effective scales up to ~5 TeV; hadronic Z channels enhance sensitivity for high-mass DM due to superior mass resolution and lower SM background.

Conclusion: Future e+e- colliders provide uniquely powerful and complementary probes of DM effective interactions via mono-Z, especially for leptophilic and electroweak-coupled operators inaccessible or poorly constrained at hadron colliders and underground experiments.

Abstract: We investigate mono-$Z$ signature at the future electron-positron collider within the dark matter effective field theory (DMEFT) framework, considering operators up to dimension six for scalar, fermionic, and vector dark matter (DM) candidates. The operators are classified according to their underlying production mechanisms. We identify the phenomenologically viable parameter space by imposing the observed DM relic abundance together with direct and indirect detection constraints, Higgs invisible decay limits, and LHC mono-$Z$ bounds obtained through a recasting analysis. We study the discovery prospects of the surviving DM operators at the future $e^-e^+$ collider including initial and final state radiation, detector simulation, and the reconstruction of both leptonic and hadronic $Z$-boson decay modes. We exploit missing energy, missing transverse momentum, recoil mass, $Z$-boson pseudorapidity, together with beam polarization, which play a significant role to distinguish the signal from the Standard Model background. We find that fermionic dipole, scalar leptophilic, vector Higgs-portal, and gauge-portal DM operators offer robust mono-$Z$ discovery potential, probing multi-TeV effective scales.

</details>


### [104] [Scheme Transformations in Non-Linear QCD Evolution: Towards a Stable NLO BK Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33821)
*Renaud Boussarie,Paul Caucal,Piotr Korcyl,Yacine Mehtar-Tani*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a scheme-transformation framework using Doi-Peliti formalism to eliminate the double collinear logarithm instability in the NLO Balitsky-Kovchegov (BK) equation, yielding a numerically stable evolution up to rapidity Y=10.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the instability in high-energy QCD NLO evolution caused by large double collinear logarithms in the BK equation, which spoil perturbative convergence.

Method: Applies the Doi-Peliti formalism to relate projectile (k⁺) and target (k⁻) factorization schemes; performs systematic operator and kernel transformations to generate cancelling NLO contributions.

Result: Derives a modified NLO BK equation free of double collinear logarithms—retaining only single collinear and double anti-collinear enhancements—and demonstrates numerical stability up to Y = 10 for both GBW and MV initial conditions.

Conclusion: The proposed scheme-transformation provides a robust, stable NLO BK evolution framework, enabling consistent future incorporation of rotated NLO impact factors.

Abstract: High-energy QCD evolution at NLO suffers from large collinear logarithms that make the perturbative expansion of the Balitsky-Kovchegov (BK) equation unstable. We develop a systematic scheme-transformation framework using the Doi-Peliti formalism to relate projectile ($k^+$) and target ($k^-$) factorization schemes. The transformation of high-energy operators and the evolution kernel generates NLO terms that cancel the problematic double collinear logarithm. The resulting NLO BK equation is free of this instability, with only single collinear and double anti-collinear enhancements remaining. Numerical solutions show stable evolution up to $Y=10$ for both Golec-Biernat-Wusthoff and McLerran-Venugopalan initial conditions, providing a consistent path toward combining stable NLO evolution with rotated NLO impact factors.

</details>


### [105] [Heavy Higgsino Dark Matter With Low Reheating in View of the LZ High-Recoil Event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33869)
*C. Pallis*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A heavy higgsino (~0.1 PeV) can be a viable cold dark matter candidate if produced non-thermally via out-of-equilibrium decay of a heavy modulus, whose scalar component generates the μ-term in MSSM; consistency with LZ experiment and relic abundance requires reheating temperature ≲ 595 GeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reconcile a heavy higgsino dark matter candidate with null results from direct (LUX-ZEPLIN) and indirect detection experiments while satisfying observed cold dark matter abundance.

Method: Non-thermal production of higgsinos via out-of-equilibrium decay of a heavy modulus field φ, where φ is identified with the scalar partner of the goldstino superfield generating the μ-parameter via the Giudice-Masiero mechanism; reheating temperature and branching ratio are constrained to ensure correct relic density and evading detection bounds.

Result: A higgsino with mass ~0.1 PeV can account for full cold dark matter abundance provided reheating temperature is T_rh ≲ 595 GeV, enabled by non-thermal production from modulus decay with m_φ ≫ m_h̃ and non-zero BR(φ → h̃h̃).

Conclusion: Heavy higgsino dark matter remains viable under current experimental constraints when produced non-thermally via modulus decay tied to MSSM μ-generation; this links dark matter physics to supersymmetry breaking and early-universe cosmology.
