Abstract: We show that a heavy higgsino $\tilde h$ with mass $m_{\tilde h}\sim0.1~{\rm PeV}$, which can be made consistent with the recent LUX-ZEPLIN event avoiding constraints from direct and indirect detection, can account for the present cold dark matter abundance of the universe, if the reheating temperature is bounded as $T_{\rm rh}\lesssim 595~{\rm GeV}$. These values can be achieved by a possible out-of-equilibrium decay of a modulus $φ$ with mass $m_φ\gg m_{\tilde h}$ and a non-vanishing branching ratio into $\tilde h$'s which plays a decisive role in reducing $T_{\rm rh}$ below its upper limit via the non-thermal $\tilde h$ production. The modulus $φ$ can be identified with the scalar component of the goldstino superfield which is involved in the generation of the $μ$ parameter of MSSM (with $μ\simeq m_{\tilde h}$) via the Giudice-Masiero mechanism.

</details>


### [106] [Sub-MeV gamma-ray lines from an endothermic interpretation of the LZ recoil candidate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33952)
*Maíra Dutra,Jacinto P. Neto,Clarissa Siqueira*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that endothermic upscattering of heavy dark matter explains the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) high-energy nuclear-recoil signal, linking the recoil energy to a dark matter mass splitting δ; radiative decay of the heavier state would produce a corresponding sub-MeV gamma-ray line (Eγ ≈ δ), testable by COSI in 2027.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the high-energy nuclear-recoil candidate observed by LZ via a theoretically and observationally viable dark matter model, while satisfying cosmological and experimental constraints—particularly the need to suppress the primordial abundance of the heavier dark matter state.

Method: Theoretical modeling of endothermic upscattering in a two-state dark matter scenario, combined with analysis of radiative decay kinematics and consistency checks against cosmological freeze-out, halo regeneration, and current experimental limits.

Result: A predictive link between the LZ recoil energy scale and a sub-MeV monochromatic gamma-ray line (Eγ ≈ δ); benchmark parameter space falls within COSI’s projected sensitivity.

Conclusion: If the LZ event is confirmed as dark matter, it strongly motivates targeted searches for sub-MeV gamma-ray lines—COSI offers a near-future, high-sensitivity probe to test this specific upscattering + radiative decay interpretation.

Abstract: Endothermic upscattering of heavy dark matter offers a compelling interpretation of the high-energy nuclear-recoil candidate recently reported by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ), with a characteristic recoil energy $E_R \propto δ$, where $δ$ is the mass splitting between the dark matter states. Cosmological and experimental constraints motivate the depletion of the primordial heavier state, which can be regenerated in the Galactic halo. We show that, if the heavier state decays radiatively, the same mass splitting sets the energy of a gamma-ray line, $E_γ\simeq δ$. For our benchmark, this leads to a sub-MeV line within the projected reach of the Compton Spectrometer and Imager (COSI), scheduled for launch in 2027. The confirmation of the LZ candidate event as a dark matter signal would therefore motivate dedicated searches for sub-MeV gamma-ray lines, with COSI providing a timely opportunity to test this interpretation.

</details>


### [107] [Finite-Size Effects on Symmetry Restoration in a Rotating Scalar Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34038)
*Jorge David Castaño-Yepes,Larry A. Cerón-Suárez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how rigid rotation affects Z2 symmetry restoration in a finite-sized λφ⁴ quantum field theory, using Dirichlet boundary conditions and one-loop effective potential with ring resummation; it finds that rotation modifies the critical temperature in a way explicitly dependent on transverse size and discrete spectrum—not just light-cylinder causality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how finite-size effects and rigid rotation jointly influence spontaneous symmetry breaking and restoration in quantum field theory—particularly whether rotational effects are governed solely by causal limits (e.g., light cylinder) or also by geometric quantization of transverse modes.

Method: Applies background-field method to compute the one-loop effective potential in a finite cylindrical geometry with Dirichlet boundary conditions; incorporates finite-temperature rotation via angular-momentum-dependent thermal mode shifts; constructs position-dependent thermal self-energy from coincident-point propagator; performs ring resummation to obtain spatially resolved effective potential; averages spatially to extract symmetry-restoration temperature.

Result: The symmetry-restoration temperature exhibits explicit dependence on both angular velocity and transverse radius—not reducible to light-cylinder scaling alone—due to coupling between rotational quantum numbers and the discrete Fourier-Bessel spectrum induced by finite geometry.

Conclusion: Rotational modification of Z2 symmetry restoration in confined systems is intrinsically tied to the spectral structure of the finite transverse geometry, challenging the notion that rotational effects are universally constrained only by causality (light cylinder).

Abstract: We investigate $\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetry restoration in a real $λφ^4$ theory confined to a finite cylindrical region undergoing rigid rotation. The finite transverse extent is imposed through Dirichlet boundary conditions, resulting in a discrete Fourier-Bessel spectrum and an explicit radial dependence of the fluctuation propagator. Using the background-field method, we derive the one-loop effective potential while retaining the spatial structure induced by the finite geometry. At finite temperature, rotation enters through the angular-momentum-dependent shifts of the thermal mode energies, coupling the rotational state to the discrete transverse spectrum. The resulting coincident-point propagator defines a position-dependent thermal self-energy, which is incorporated into the effective potential through ring resummation. This yields a spatially resolved effective potential whose structure depends simultaneously on the background field, temperature, angular velocity, and transverse size. We then use its spatial average to determine the symmetry-restoration temperature and study its dependence on the rotational state and the finite-volume spectrum. The results show that the rotational modification of the transition temperature retains an explicit dependence on the transverse size, reflecting the spectral structure of the finite rotating system rather than solely the causal constraint associated with the light cylinder.

</details>


### [108] [Pseudospectra in Holographic QCD Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34084)
*Luiz F. Ferreira*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes spectral stability of quasinormal modes (QNMs) in nonconformal holographic QCD models (soft-wall and analytic IHQCD) using pseudospectral methods; finds temperature-dependent instability growth, linking increasing Im(ω) to hadronic decoherence and melting, with pseudospectrum offering a complementary diagnostic to spectral functions for dissociation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the spectral stability of quasinormal modes in nonconformal holographic QCD models—where intrinsic dimensionful scales (e.g., confinement scale, temperature) break conformal invariance—and to assess whether pseudospectral analysis can provide a robust, perturbation-sensitive diagnostic of hadronic melting beyond standard spectral function analysis.

Method: Pseudospectral analysis applied to linear eigenvalue problems derived from equations of motion for scalar/vector fields (soft-wall model) and tensor glueball fluctuations (analytic IHQCD); computation of ε-pseudospectra to quantify sensitivity of QNM frequencies to small perturbations; comparison with thermal spectral functions.

Result: Quasinormal mode spectra exhibit nontrivial temperature-dependent pseudospectral instability: |Im(ω)| increases with T, signaling enhanced decay and loss of coherence; at high T, ω ∼ cT asymptotically—but only after hadronic states have melted; pseudospectral broadening correlates with and complements spectral function broadening as an indicator of dissociation.

Conclusion: Pseudospectral instability provides a quantitatively refined, perturbation-resilient characterization of spectral degradation in holographic QCD, capturing early signatures of hadronic melting that may precede or enrich interpretations based solely on spectral functions.

Abstract: We investigate the spectral stability of quasinormal modes in nonconformal holographic QCD models using pseudospectral analysis. We first consider the soft-wall model, focusing on scalar and vector fields, and then extend the analysis to the analytic Improved Holographic QCD (IHQCD) model, where we study tensor glueball fluctuations. In both cases, the equations of motion are formulated as linear eigenvalue problems, allowing the instability of the quasinormal spectrum to small perturbations to be quantified through the pseudospectrum. We find that the spectral stability of the quasinormal modes exhibits a nontrivial dependence on temperature, reflecting the presence of intrinsic dimensionful scales in holographic QCD models. As the temperature increases, the magnitude of the imaginary part of the quasinormal frequencies grows, signaling an increasing decay rate and the progressive loss of coherence of the corresponding hadronic excitations. At sufficiently high temperatures, the quasinormal frequencies approach an approximately linear dependence on temperature, although this regime is reached only after the associated hadronic states have melted. We further compare the pseudospectral instability with the behavior of the corresponding spectral functions. Our results indicate that the growth of pseudospectral instability provides a complementary characterization of the loss of spectral stability and can serve as a useful diagnostic of hadronic dissociation and melting in holographic QCD.

</details>


### [109] [Freeze-in of anapole and charge-radius inelastic dark matter: X-ray probes of a decoupled vector portal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34230)
*Krzysztof Jodłowski,Chih-Ting Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies freeze-in production of inelastic dark matter (iDM) via dimension-6 anapole and charge-radius operators from a heavy dark photon portal, focusing on sub-dielectron-threshold mass splittings where the excited state χ₁ decays dominantly via χ₁ → χ₀ + 3γ; it computes the three-photon decay rate and spectrum (analytically in Euler–Heisenberg limit + full one-loop corrections), and combines X-ray (INTEGRAL/SPI, NuSTAR), direct detection (LZ), and accelerator constraints to probe freeze-in parameter space and reheating temperature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore cosmological and experimental signatures of inelastic dark matter produced via freeze-in through a decoupled vector portal, especially in the regime where the mass splitting Δ is below the dielectron threshold—enabling long-lived χ₁ with distinctive multi-photon decays—and to assess the sensitivity of current X-ray and direct detection experiments to such scenarios, including potential explanation of the LZ excess.

Method: Analytical calculation of the χ₁ → χ₀ + 3γ decay width and photon spectrum in the Euler–Heisenberg effective theory; inclusion of finite-electron-mass corrections via full one-loop QED amplitude; numerical evaluation of freeze-in yield as function of reheating temperature T_R and coupling; joint likelihood analysis combining X-ray spectral limits (INTEGRAL/SPI, NuSTAR), relic density requirement (Ωh² ≈ 0.12), direct detection (exothermic downscattering in LZ), and accelerator constraints on dark photon mediators.

Result: Three-photon decay dominates for Δ < 2m_e; INTEGRAL/SPI strongly constrains freeze-in parameter space at Δ = 900 keV, probing Boltzmann-suppressed high-mass branches; NuSTAR limits at Δ = 100 keV are weak due to ∝ Δ⁹ dependence of decay width; exothermic scattering in LZ is viable for specific iDM masses and couplings; X-ray observations provide competitive, complementary probes of both freeze-in production and T_R.

Conclusion: X-ray telescopes—especially INTEGRAL/SPI—are powerful probes of freeze-in-generated inelastic dark matter through its loop-induced three-photon decay, offering unique sensitivity to the reheating temperature and portal decoupling scale; combined with direct detection signals like LZ’s excess, this framework yields testable, multi-messenger predictions for light, feebly coupled iDM.

Abstract: We study freeze-in production and experimental probes of inelastic dark matter (iDM) coupled to the Standard Model through dimension-6 transition anapole and charge-radius operators. These interactions arise upon integrating out the heavy dark photon of a broken $U(1)_D$ vector portal. We focus on mass splittings below the dielectron threshold, where the excited-state decay is cosmologically long-lived and decays predominantly through $χ_1\toχ_0+3γ$, producing a distinctive keV--MeV photon spectrum. We calculate the decay rate and photon spectrum analytically in the Euler--Heisenberg limit and evaluate finite-electron-mass corrections using the full one-loop amplitude. We then combine X-ray limits with freeze-in relic density targets and complementary direct detection (DD) and accelerator constraints. The surviving $χ_1$ population also leads to exothermic downscattering signatures at DD experiments, including the possibility of explaining the LZ signal, while the three-photon decay is constrained by X-ray data. At $Δ=900\,\mathrm{keV}$, INTEGRAL/SPI probes the Boltzmann-suppressed high-mass branches of the freeze-in targets, whereas the NuSTAR limits at $Δ=100\,\mathrm{keV}$ are substantially weaker because of the steep splitting dependence of the decay width. Our results demonstrate the potential of X-ray observations as a probe of freeze-in through a decoupled vector portal and of the reheating temperature governing the iDM production.

</details>


### [110] [Next-to-Next-to-Leading Order QCD Corrections to $η_t \to HZ$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34364)
*Cai-Ping Jia,Yan-Qing Ma,Huai-Min Yu,Yu-Jie Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the NNLO QCD correction to the short-distance coefficient for the decay η_t → HZ within NRQCD, retaining full Higgs and Z boson mass dependence; it finds slow perturbative convergence (NLO: −29%, NNLO: −19% suppression of LO width), validates the result via three consistency checks, and provides a key input for probing top-Higgs coupling near the tt̄ threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent LHC observations of a threshold enhancement in the tt̄ system suggestive of toponium-like dynamics motivate precision study of the pseudoscalar toponium state η_t decay into HZ, to probe the top-Higgs interaction.

Method: Computes the NNLO QCD correction to the hard short-distance coefficient for η_t → HZ within the NRQCD factorization framework, including full m_H and m_Z dependence, two-loop virtual corrections, and real double-gluon emission; performs consistency checks against large-mass limit, γ_5 prescription independence, and RG-logarithm structure.

Result: At μ_R = μ_Λ = m_t, NLO and NNLO corrections suppress the LO width by ~29% and ~19%, respectively; the two-loop contribution is ~2/3 the one-loop effect and same sign, leading to slow convergence; NNLO prediction is ~52% of LO width.

Conclusion: The computed NNLO coefficient is a necessary ingredient for future threshold studies of η_t → HZ and enhances sensitivity to the top-Higgs Yukawa coupling; its accuracy is robustly validated by multiple theoretical consistency checks.

Abstract: Motivated by recent LHC observations of a threshold enhancement in the $t\bar t$ system consistent with toponium-like dynamics, we compute the next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO) QCD correction to the hard short-distance coefficient for $η_t\to HZ$, where $η_t$ denotes the pseudoscalar color-singlet configuration $t\bar t({}^1S_0^{[1]})$. Within the NRQCD factorization framework, we retain the full dependence on the Higgs- and $Z$-boson masses and include both the two-loop virtual corrections and the real double-gluon-emission channel. At next-to-leading order (NLO), the finite-mass result remains close to its large-mass limit. The NNLO coefficient develops logarithms of both the renormalization scale $μ_R$ and the NRQCD factorization scale $μ_Λ$. For $μ_R=μ_Λ=m_t$, the NLO and NNLO terms suppress the leading-order width by about $29\%$ and $19\%$, respectively; the two-loop contribution is about two thirds of the one-loop effect and of the same sign, so the perturbative series converges slowly and the NNLO prediction amounts to about $52\%$ of the leading-order width. The resulting coefficient provides a necessary ingredient for future threshold studies of $η_t\to HZ$ and for assessing the sensitivity of this channel to the top-Higgs interaction. The accuracy of the result is further supported by three checks: reproduction of the known large-mass limit at one loop, independence of the extracted coefficient on the $γ_5$ prescription, and consistency of its scale logarithms with the renormalization-group structure.

</details>


### [111] [Radial Excitation Spectra of Light Pseudoscalar and Vector Mesons in Light-Front Holographic QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34644)
*Abhisth Srivastava*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper improves Light-Front Holographic QCD by extending the transverse confining potential with Coulombic and logarithmic terms, reducing average mass deviation for radial meson excitations from 7.6% to 5.2% versus experiment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The standard soft-wall LFHQCD model shows ~17–18% deviations for radial pion excitations; improved description of short- and intermediate-range confinement is needed.

Method: Extended transverse confining potential (soft-wall + Coulombic + logarithmic terms); numerical solution of light-front Schrödinger equation; combined with Brodsky-de Téramond longitudinal prescription to compute full meson mass spectra.

Result: Average deviation from experimental masses reduced from 7.6% to 5.2% for nine radially excited states (π, ρ, K*); Regge trajectories better reproduced; Coulombic (short-range) and logarithmic (intermediate-range) contributions are essential.

Conclusion: Adding Coulombic and logarithmic corrections to the transverse potential significantly enhances LFHQCD’s predictive power for radial meson excitations, validating refined holographic confinement modeling.

Abstract: The non-perturbative regime of Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) poses significant challenges for analytical treatment. Light-Front Holographic QCD (LFHQCD) offers an effective semiclassical framework for exploring hadronic structure through a holographic dual description of confinement. While the standard soft-wall model, along with the Brodsky-de Téramond (BdT) longitudinal prescription, provides satisfactory results for meson ground states, it shows deviations of around 17 to $18\%$ for radial excitations of the pion family. In this work, we extend the transverse confining potential by adding Coulombic and logarithmic contributions beyond the standard soft-wall framework. We solve the resulting light-front Schrödinger equation numerically, and we combine the transverse mass spectrum with the BdT longitudinal prescription to obtain the complete meson mass spectrum. We compute the radial excitation spectra of the $π$, $ρ$, and $K^{*}$ meson families and analyze their Regge trajectories. The modified framework reduces the overall average deviation from experimental data from about $7.6\%$ to $5.2\%$ compared to the standard soft-wall model, representing a $\sim 32\%$ improvement for the fitted nine-state dataset in describing radially excited states. The results show that short-range Coulombic and intermediate-range logarithmic contributions are important for refining the radial meson spectrum within the light-front holographic approach.

</details>


### [112] [Soft-Collinear Chiral Perturbation Theory for $B \to π$ form factors at large recoil](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34696)
*Thorsten Feldmann,Jack Jenkins,Jaime del Palacio Lirola*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a hadronic effective theory for B→π decays at large recoil by combining heavy-quark and chiral symmetries with SCET factorization, introduces local soft/collinear chiral symmetries to match non-local SCET operators onto hadronic ones, and computes chiral-logarithmic corrections to form factors—finding disagreement with Hard-Pion χPT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve inconsistencies in the chiral-logarithmic behavior of B→π form factors at large recoil, particularly the incompatibility between SCET-based non-local dynamics and existing Hard-Pion Chiral Perturbation Theory.

Method: Constructs a hadronic effective theory embedding quasi-static heavy-meson fields and soft/collinear pions; exploits SCET factorization to identify emergent duplicated chiral symmetries; promotes soft and collinear chiral transformations to local symmetries to systematically match non-local SCET four-quark operators onto local hadronic operators below ΛχSB; computes one-loop matrix elements of leading operators to extract pion-mass dependence.

Result: Derives novel chiral-logarithmic corrections to B→π form factors arising from non-local transition currents in the hadronic theory; demonstrates that this pattern contradicts predictions of Hard-Pion χPT.

Conclusion: The proposed hadronic SCET-chiral framework provides a more consistent symmetry-based description of soft-overlap contributions in B→π transitions at large recoil, exposing limitations of Hard-Pion χPT and enabling systematic higher-order calculations.

Abstract: We develop an effective hadronic theory for heavy-meson decays into energetic pions, organized by the approximate heavy-quark and chiral symmetries. The dynamical degrees of freedom of the theory consist of quasi-static heavy-meson fields coupled to soft and collinear pions, relevant for exclusive $B \to π$ transitions at large recoil energy ${E_π\simeq M_B/2}$. Our approach exploits the factorization of soft and collinear quarks in Soft-Collinear Effective Theory (SCET), leading to an emergent duplication of chiral symmetry in the two kinematic sectors. Promoting the soft and collinear chiral transformations to local symmetries enables us to systematically match non-local four-quark operators in SCET -- governing the soft-overlap contribution to heavy-to-light form factors -- onto effective operators constructed from hadronic fields below the scale of chiral symmetry breaking. As a first application, we compute the logarithmically enhanced pion-mass dependence of the $B \to π$ form factors at large recoil by calculating the one-loop matrix elements of the leading hadronic operators. We find that the non-local transition currents in our effective theory lead to a pattern of chiral-logarithmic corrections that is not compatible with the existing formulation provided by Hard-Pion Chiral Perturbation Theory.

</details>


### [113] [Termination of the Radial Ladder in Mixed-Flavor Heavy Mesons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34734)
*Wen-Xuan Zhang,Wen-Nian Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether radial excitations of heavy mesons (Ds, Bs, Bc) terminate due to confinement screening by light-quark pair creation, using a screened Godfrey–Isgur Hamiltonian solved via Gaussian expansion; it predicts finite termination masses (~3.36, ~6.78, ~8.19 GeV), identifies safe radial quantum number ranges (n_safe ≈ 4–6, 5–7, 9–11), and identifies unobservable mass windows above ~3.49, ~6.86, ~8.24 GeV—testable at BESIII, Belle II, LHCb.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the open question of whether the radial excitation ladder of heavy mixed-flavor mesons terminates due to string breaking (confinement screening by light-quark pair creation), which challenges conventional quark model expectations.

Method: Solves a screened Godfrey–Isgur Hamiltonian using the Gaussian expansion method; screening length is determined from threshold-dependent string-breaking analysis and calibrated to charmonium and bottomonium spectroscopic anchors.

Result: Predicts saturation masses of radial spectra at 3.36 GeV (Ds), 6.78 GeV (Bs), and 8.19 GeV (Bc); RMS radii diverge (several fm) and level spacings drop below 10 MeV near saturation; mass-radius decoupling defines n_safe = 4–6 (Ds), 5–7 (Bs), 9–11 (Bc); states above 3.49, 6.86, 8.24 GeV are unobservable.

Conclusion: The radial excitation ladder terminates finitely in all three meson families due to confinement screening, with earliest termination in Ds and latest in Bc; this provides a concrete, falsifiable prediction for current and upcoming high-statistics experiments.

Abstract: A fundamental open question in hadron spectroscopy is whether the radial excitation ladder of heavy mesons terminates at a finite level once confinement is screened by light-quark pair creation. We address this question for the mixed-flavor mesons $D_s$, $B_s$, and $B_c$ by solving a screened Godfrey--Isgur Hamiltonian with the Gaussian expansion method. The screening length is fixed by threshold-dependent string breaking analysis, and is matched to the charmonium and bottomonium anchors. The calculated spectra saturate at $3.36$, $6.78$, and $8.19~{\rm GeV}$ in $D_s$, $B_s$, and $B_c$ families, while the RMS radii grow by several fm and mass gaps fall below $10~{\rm MeV}$. Mass-radius decoupling places the end of the safe radial ladder at $n_{\mathrm{safe}}\approx 4$--$6$ for $D_s$, $5$--$7$ for $B_s$, and $9$--$11$ for $B_c$, so the ladder terminates first in $D_s$, then in $B_s$, and last in $B_c$. Above the limiting-mass windows, at $3.49$, $6.86$, and $8.24~{\rm GeV}$, states of the radial ladder cannot be discovered. This termination is directly testable with forthcoming high-statistics data from BESIII, Belle II, and LHCb.

</details>


### [114] [On the status of the 4-loop splitting functions in QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34837)
*S. Moch,A. Vogt*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reports on recent progress in computing four-loop splitting functions governing the scale dependence of parton distribution functions (PDFs); it confirms known non-singlet results, identifies novel small-$x$ double logarithms and new analytic structures in singlet cases, and quantifies sub-1% numerical impact (except for total valence quarks) at $x \sim 10^{-4}$ and $\alpha_s = 0.2$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance precision QCD phenomenology by completing the four-loop perturbative input for DGLAP evolution—specifically, the splitting functions—required for high-accuracy global PDF fits and collider predictions.

Method: Analytic computation of four-loop massless splitting functions in dimensional regularization using integration-by-parts reduction, symmetry constraints, and consistency checks with known lower-order results and small-$x$/large-$x$ limits.

Result: Full determination of three non-singlet splitting functions; partial but structurally revealing results for flavour-singlet cases revealing previously unseen analytic features (e.g., new transcendental structures or branch cuts); quantitative assessment showing $\lesssim 1\%$ effect on scale derivatives down to $x \sim 10^{-4}$, except for the total valence distribution where effects are larger.

Conclusion: Four-loop splitting functions are largely consistent with theoretical expectations but expose subtle new dynamics—especially in the singlet sector and at small $x$—and their inclusion improves PDF evolution accuracy at the percent level, warranting incorporation into next-generation global analyses.

Abstract: We briefly discuss recent results on the four-loop splitting functions for the scale dependence of the parton distributions of hadrons. The three non-singlet cases are now completely known. The results agree with almost all expectations, but exhibit some small-$x$ double logarithms not encountered at lower orders. The flavour-singlet cases include, as already obvious from the present partial results, analytical structures not seen in the splitting functions up to now. The numerical effects (and their uncertainties, in the singlet cases) of the four-loop contributions to the scale derivatives amount to about $1\%$ or less down to momentum fractions $x$ as low as about $10^{-4}$ at a standard benchmark point with $α_{\rm s} = 0.2$, with the exception of the total valence quark distribution.

</details>


### [115] [Twist-4 GTMDs of sea quarks at zero skewness in the light-cone quark model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34937)
*Xiaoyan Luan,Zhun Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes twist-4 GTMDs for anti-u and anti-d quarks in the proton at zero skewness using light-cone wave functions from a baryon-meson fluctuation model, and explores their relations to GPDs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance understanding of quark-gluon structure beyond leading twist by accessing twist-4 generalized transverse momentum dependent parton distributions (GTMDs), particularly for sea antiquarks, which encode rich 3D nucleon structure information not accessible via standard PDFs or lower-twist GPDs/TMDs.

Method: Adopts the overlap representation in light-cone formalism; employs proton light-cone wave functions derived from the baryon-meson fluctuation model based on |q\bar{q}B> Fock components; computes twist-4 GTMDs for \bar{u} and \bar{d} quarks at zero skewness; analyzes connections between twist-4 GTMDs and twist-4 GPDs.

Result: Numerical results for twist-4 GTMDs and corresponding twist-4 GPDs of \bar{u} and \bar{d} quarks at zero skewness are obtained and presented; nontrivial correlations between GTMDs and GPDs are identified.

Conclusion: Twist-4 GTMDs for sea antiquarks are quantitatively accessible within this Fock-state-based light-cone framework; their relation to twist-4 GPDs reveals structural links between transverse-momentum and longitudinal-momentum/spin entanglement in the proton, supporting future experimental probes at EIC and JLab.

Abstract: We investigate the twist-4 generalized transverse momentum dependent parton distributions (GTMDs) of $\bar{u}$ and $\bar{d}$ quarks inside the proton at zero skewness by adopting the overlap representation within the light-cone formalism. Using the light-cone wave functions of the proton derived from the baryon-meson fluctuation model in terms of the $|q\bar{q}B\rangle$ Fock states, we calculate the twist-4 GTMDs of $\bar{u}$ and $\bar{d}$ quarks. Additionally, the intricate relations between the GTMDs and the generalized parton distributions are also explored in our research. Numerical results for these twist-4 GTMDs and twist-4 GPDs at zero skewness are analyzed and presented.

</details>


### [116] [A First Glimpse at Super-Leading Logarithms in Monte Carlo Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34984)
*Nicolas Schalch*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the Marzili Monte Carlo framework to resum super-leading logarithms from two-Glauber-exchange contributions—the first such Monte Carlo resummation—enabling joint study of super-leading and non-global logarithms and paving the way for Glauber exchange in parton showers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Super-leading logarithms from Glauber exchange hinder non-global observable resummation at hadron colliders; existing tools lack Monte Carlo treatment of these effects.

Method: Extension of the Marzili Monte Carlo framework—based on effective field theory—to include the leading nonzero colour contribution from two Glauber exchanges.

Result: First Monte Carlo resummation of super-leading logarithms; enables simultaneous resummation of super-leading and non-global logarithms.

Conclusion: The extended Marzili framework provides a practical, event-generator-compatible path toward incorporating Glauber exchange into parton showers and studying its interplay with non-global dynamics.

Abstract: Super-leading logarithms from Glauber exchange complicate the resummation of non-global observables at hadron colliders. In this Letter, we extend Marzili, a Monte Carlo framework for non-global resummation based on effective field theory, to resum the leading nonzero colour contribution involving two Glauber exchanges. This is the first Monte Carlo resummation of super-leading logarithms. Together with Marzili's treatment of non-global logarithms, this opens a path towards studying their interplay and incorporating Glauber exchange into parton showers.

</details>


### [117] [Parameter degeneracy and information loss in inverse flavor mapping for high-energy astrophysical neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35132)
*Zi-Qiang Chen,Zhi-zhong Xing,Ye-Ling Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the inverse problem of reconstructing astrophysical neutrino source flavor ratios from observed flavor ratios at detectors like IceCube, focusing on degeneracies and information loss arising when the flavor transition matrix P becomes singular (det P = 0), including under μ–τ reflection symmetry; it derives constraints and tests feasibility using IceCube data (5 TeV–10 PeV).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the fundamental limitation in inferring astrophysical neutrino source flavor composition due to parameter degeneracy and singular behavior of the oscillation-induced flavor mapping matrix P, especially near det P = 0 — a region permitted by current neutrino oscillation data.

Method: Analytical derivation of the determinant-zero condition for the 3×3 neutrino flavor transition matrix P in terms of standard oscillation parameters; identification of correlations among source (η) and observed (f) flavor ratios under det P = 0; incorporation of μ–τ reflection symmetry as a special case; application to IceCube all-sky high-energy neutrino data (5 TeV–10 PeV) assuming common source flavor composition.

Result: Explicit analytical conditions for det P = 0 and corresponding linear constraints linking f_e, f_μ to η_e, η_μ; demonstration that source flavor ratios cannot be uniquely reconstructed when det P ≈ 0; quantitative assessment showing limited but non-negligible constraining power on η using current IceCube data under common-composition assumption.

Conclusion: The inverse flavor mapping η = P⁻¹f is fundamentally ill-posed near det P = 0, leading to intrinsic degeneracies; while IceCube data impose some bounds on possible source flavor ratios, definitive reconstruction requires either improved statistics, energy-dependent analysis, or independent constraints on oscillation parameters or source models.

Abstract: Given a high-energy astrophysical neutrino flux, its source flavor ratios $η= \{η^{}_e, η^{}_μ, η^{}_τ\}$ are correlated with the ones $f = \{f^{}_e, f^{}_μ, f^{}_τ\}$ measured at a neutrino telescope via $f = P η$, where the elements of $P$ consist of four lepton flavor mixing parameters. But realistic {\it inverse} flavor mapping $η= P^{-1} f$ encounters unavoidable parameter degeneracy and information loss, especially in or near the $\det P = 0$ limit allowed by current neutrino oscillation data. In this work we explore the parameter correlation condition for $\det P = 0$ including the $μ$-$τ$ reflection symmetry case, formulate the corresponding constraints on $f^{}_e$ and $f^{}_μ$ versus $η^{}_e$ and $η^{}_μ$, and illustrate to what extent the source flavor distribution can be mapped by using the recent IceCube all-sky neutrino flux data ranging from 5 TeV to 10 PeV under the assumption that the relevant sources have a common flavor composition.

</details>


### [118] [The $Dπ$ and $D^*π$ femtoscopy puzzle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35133)
*Pablo Encarnación,Miguel Albaladejo,Albert Feijoo,Juan Nieves*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a relativistic momentum-space framework for femtoscopic correlation functions incorporating strong and Coulomb interactions in coupled channels, applies it to Dπ and D*π systems, identifies inconsistencies between theory and ALICE experimental extractions due to flawed asymptotic assumptions, and calls for reanalysis to establish femtoscopy as a quantitative tool.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies between theoretical predictions and ALICE experimental femtoscopic extractions of Dπ correlations, arising from inadequate treatment of Coulomb asymptotics and pole contributions in the correlation function analysis.

Method: Extends the Vincent--Phatak prescription to the 3D Bethe--Salpeter equation with wave-function matching to ensure correct relativistic and Coulomb asymptotics; employs a coupled-channel heavy-meson chiral interaction reproducing the two-pole structure of D₀*(2300); performs systematic fitting-window variation to assess robustness of scattering parameter extraction.

Result: Theoretical Dπ correlation functions disagree significantly with ALICE extractions due to the unjustified assumption that the correlation function reaches unity above 100 MeV — a region still influenced by the lower D₀*(2300) pole; extracted scattering parameters show strong dependence on fitting window; similar issues appear in D*π, linked to heavy-quark spin symmetry; unresolved tension also exists with ALICE Kπ data.

Conclusion: Current experimental extraction procedures for hadronic femtoscopy are not robust; a dedicated reanalysis of ALICE data using proper relativistic, coupled-channel, and Coulomb-inclusive frameworks is essential to validate femtoscopy as a precision tool for low-energy hadron interactions.

Abstract: We present a relativistic momentum-space framework for femtoscopic correlation functions including strong and Coulomb interactions in coupled channels. The approach extends the Vincent--Phatak prescription to the three-dimensional Bethe--Salpeter equation, combining a fully relativistic treatment with the correct Coulomb asymptotics through wave-function matching. We apply it to the $Dπ$ system using a coupled-channel heavy-meson chiral interaction that reproduces the established two-pole structure of the $D_0^*(2300)$. Our correlation functions agree with previous theoretical calculations but differ significantly from those extracted by the ALICE Collaboration. We trace this tension to the assumption made in the experimental analysis that the genuine correlation function reaches unity above a relative momentum of $100$ MeV, whereas this region remains affected by the lower $D_0^*(2300)$ pole. Repeating the extraction over different fitting windows reveals a strong dependence of the extracted signal on the chosen range, demonstrating that the resulting $Dπ$ scattering parameters are not robust and motivating a dedicated reanalysis of the experimental data. We extend the analysis to the $D^*π$ system, where the same issue arises in close connection with heavy-quark spin symmetry. Together with the unresolved tension between theoretical calculations and the ALICE $Kπ$ femtoscopic measurements, this highlights the need to clarify the origin of this puzzle, with the aim of establishing femtoscopy as a reliable quantitative tool for precision studies of low-energy hadron interactions.

</details>


### [119] [Perturbative QCD correction to the nonleptonic weak rate in dense quark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35165)
*Matti Heikinheimo,Hanna Lempiäinen,Risto Paatelainen,Tomi Ruosteoja,Kimmo Tuominen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the first leading-order QCD correction to the nonleptonic weak flavor-equilibration rate in dense quark matter using real-time thermal field theory and the Kadanoff-Baym equation; the result is gauge-invariant, finite, enhances the tree-level rate by 30–40% at low temperatures relevant to compact stars, and improves bulk-viscosity modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the accuracy of the nonleptonic weak flavor-equilibration rate in dense quark matter—previously modeled only at tree level for over 30 years—by including leading perturbative QCD corrections essential for reliable bulk-viscosity calculations in compact-star physics.

Method: Uses the Kadanoff-Baym kinetic equation within real-time thermal field theory to compute the leading-order QCD correction; ensures gauge invariance and finiteness by systematically canceling soft and collinear divergences between real and virtual diagrams, consistent with the Kinoshita-Lee-Nauenberg theorem.

Result: The leading QCD correction is fully finite and gauge invariant; in the low-temperature, high-density regime applicable to compact stars, it enhances the tree-level equilibration rate by 30–40%.

Conclusion: The corrected flavor-equilibration rate supersedes the long-standing tree-level approximation, providing a more accurate, first-principles microscopic input for transport property calculations—especially bulk viscosity—in dense quark matter.

Abstract: We present the first calculation of the leading perturbative quantum chromodynamics (QCD) correction to the nonleptonic weak flavor-equilibration rate in dense quark matter. The computation is based on the Kadanoff-Baym kinetic equation and performed using real-time thermal field theory. The resulting QCD correction is both gauge invariant and fully finite. In particular, all soft and collinear divergences cancel after combining real and virtual corrections, in accordance with the Kinoshita-Lee-Nauenberg theorem. In the low-temperature regime relevant to compact-star dynamics, the correction enhances the tree-level rate by $30 - 40\%$ over the high-density range where perturbative QCD is applicable. The corrected rate improves on the tree-level result used in phenomenological calculations for more than three decades, providing a more accurate microscopic input for bulk-viscosity calculations in dense quark matter.

</details>


### [120] [Purely flavon driven leptogenesis for exactly degenerate Dirac or Majorana neutrino mass matrix in the type-I seesaw model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35299)
*Yan Shao,Zhen-hua Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes flavon fields—introduced in flavor-symmetry-based neutrino mass models—as a natural solution to restore viable leptogenesis when conventional CP asymmetry vanishes due to degenerate Dirac ($M_D \propto I$) or Majorana ($M_R \propto I$) neutrino mass matrices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Flavor symmetries explain neutrino mixing but often predict highly constrained mass structures (e.g., $M_D \propto I$ or $M_R \propto I$) that suppress CP asymmetry in standard leptogenesis, threatening its viability.

Method: The authors introduce flavon–RHN interactions for $M_D \propto I$, enabling new two-body decays $N_I \to N_J \phi$ with CP violation; for $M_R \propto I$, they employ three-body decays $N_I \to L_\alpha H \phi$ mediated by heavy vectorlike fermions, generating CP asymmetry despite exact RHN mass degeneracy.

Result: Both scenarios yield nonvanishing CP asymmetry, enabling successful leptogenesis without lifting the flavor-symmetry-imposed degeneracy; flavons thus bridge neutrino flavor structure and baryogenesis.

Conclusion: Flavon fields, already required for generating realistic neutrino masses and mixings in flavor models, naturally resolve the leptogenesis suppression problem, providing a unified and predictive framework linking low-energy neutrino phenomenology and high-scale baryogenesis.

Abstract: While the type-I seesaw model provides an attractive framework for simultaneously explaining the origin of neutrino masses and the baryon asymmetry of the Universe, flavor symmetries offer a natural approach to understanding the observed neutrino mixing pattern. However, the highly constrained neutrino mass structures predicted by flavor symmetries may also prevent conventional leptogenesis to work. In particular, when the Dirac neutrino mass matrix $M^{}_{\rm D}$ or the Majorana right-handed-neutrino (RHN) mass matrix $M^{}_{\rm R}$ is exactly degenerate (namely, they are proportional to the identity matrix $I$), the CP asymmetry of RHN decays will vanish either due to the orthogonality of different neutrino Yukawa coupling columns or the exact degeneracy of RHN masses. In this work, we propose that the flavon fields (which are inherently present in flavor-symmetry neutrino mass models to be responsible for generating the nontrivial neutrino flavor structure) can naturally overcome these obstacles. For the case of $M^{}_{\rm D} \propto I$, flavon--RHN interactions induce additional decay channels $N_I \to N_J φ$ and generate new CP-violating contributions to RHN decays. For the case of $M^{}_{\rm R} \propto I$, three-body decays $N_I\rightarrow L^{}_αHφ$ mediated by heavy vectorlike fermions provide a CP asymmetry source even when the RHN masses are exactly degenerate. Our results demonstrate that the flavon sector provides a natural connection between neutrino flavor structure and leptogenesis, offering a new mechanism for leptogenesis in flavor-symmetry-based neutrino mass models.

</details>


### [121] [System-size dependence of jet quenching from light to heavy-ion collisions at LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35321)
*Man Xie,Xiang-Yu Wu,Han-Zhong Zhang,Xin-Nian Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies single-inclusive hadron suppression across O+O, Ne+Ne, Xe+Xe, and Pb+Pb collisions at LHC energies using an NLO pQCD model with nuclear PDFs, higher-twist energy loss, and a temperature-dependent jet transport coefficient q̂/T³ constrained by Bayesian analyses of RHIC/LHC data; it successfully describes R_AA across all systems and shows system-size-independent scaling of jet quenching observables, suggesting QGP formation even in small systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate system-size dependence of jet quenching—motivated by recent LHC light-ion (O+O, Ne+Ne) collision data—and to test whether QGP formation occurs even in the smallest collision systems.

Method: Next-to-leading-order perturbative QCD framework incorporating: (i) nuclear-modified initial-state parton distribution functions (nPDFs), (ii) final-state parton energy loss via the higher-twist formalism, and (iii) temperature-dependent jet transport coefficient q̂/T³ calibrated via Bayesian analysis of prior RHIC and LHC R_AA data.

Result: The model simultaneously reproduces measured R_AA for charged hadrons across O+O, Ne+Ne, Xe+Xe, and Pb+Pb collisions over broad centrality ranges and minimum-bias events; the ratio R_NeNe/R_OO and R_AA vs ⟨N_coll⟩ exhibit universal scaling at fixed p_T, indicating consistent jet quenching behavior independent of system size.

Conclusion: The observed system-size universality of jet quenching observables strongly supports the formation of a QGP-like medium—even in light-ion collisions such as O+O and Ne+Ne—challenging traditional thresholds for QGP creation.

Abstract: Single inclusive hadron suppression in high-energy heavy-ion collisions provides a sensitive probe of parton energy loss and jet transport coefficient in quark-gluon plasma (QGP). System size dependence of jet quenching can provide further constraints on the dynamics of jet quenching and parton energy loss. Motivated by recent experiments on light-ion collisions at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC), we perform a systematic study of single-inclusive hadron suppression in O+O, Ne+Ne, Xe+Xe and Pb+Pb collisions at the LHC energies. The calculations are carried out within the next-to-leading-order perturbative QCD model, incorporating the nuclear modified initial-state parton distributions, final-state parton energy loss in the higher-twist approach and the temperature dependence of the jet transport coefficient $\hat q/T^3$ from the Bayesian analyses of previous experimental data at the Relativistic Heavy-ion Collider (RHIC) and LHC. Our model calculations can simultaneously describe the measured single-inclusive charged hadron $R_{AA}$ in O+O, Ne+Ne, Xe+Xe and Pb+Pb collisions over a broad range of event centralities and in the minimum-bias events. We further illustrate the system size dependence of jet quenching through the ratio $R_{\mathrm{NeNe}}/R_{\mathrm{OO}}$ and the modification factors $R_{AA}$ as a function of $\langle N_{\mathrm{coll}} \rangle$ which follow a common behavior at fixed $p_{\rm T}$, indicating the formation of QGP even in the smallest collision systems.

</details>


### [122] [Resummation-scale uncertainties in PDF determinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35344)
*Hamed Abdolmaleki,Valerio Bertone,Giuseppe Bozzi,Francesco Giuli,Alexander Glazov,Valentina Guglielmi,Francesco Hautmann,Fred Olness,Pavel Starovoitov,Oleksandr Zenaiev*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper quantifies the theoretical uncertainty from resummation-scale variations in PDF determinations—previously unaccounted for—and shows it significantly affects LHC top-pair production predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Resummation-scale variations introduce a previously neglected source of theoretical uncertainty in PDF evolution and α_s determination, impacting precision QCD predictions.

Method: The authors use the open-source xFitter framework to perform PDF fits to HERA deep-inelastic scattering data while varying the resummation scale, then assess the resulting uncertainty on LHC top-quark pair production cross sections.

Result: Resummation-scale variations induce sizable uncertainties in PDFs and lead to non-negligible shifts in predicted top-pair production rates at the LHC.

Conclusion: Resummation-scale uncertainty must be included in modern PDF fits and precision QCD analyses to ensure reliable theoretical error estimates.

Abstract: We investigate the impact of resummation-scale variations on parton distribution function (PDF) determinations. These variations introduce an additional source of theoretical uncertainty related to the evolution of PDFs and strong coupling $α_s$ that has never previously been accounted for in PDF fits. Using the open-source xFitter framework, we quantify this uncertainty in fits to HERA deep-inelastic scattering data and examine its implications for predictions of top-quark pair production at the LHC. We show that the resulting resummation-scale uncertainties have a sizable impact on LHC observables, highlighting the importance of including this source of uncertainty in precision QCD analyses.

</details>


### [123] [Differential Higgs production at small-$x$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35458)
*Benedetta Bernardini*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Preliminary extension of the HELL differential resummation framework to Higgs boson production via gluon fusion, targeting high-energy precision physics with $x = Q^2/s$ logarithms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve precision in high-energy collider physics by resumming large double-logarithmic terms in $x = Q^2/s$, which become numerically dominant at small $x$ (high energies).

Method: Extension of the HELL (High-Energy Logarithms) differential resummation formalism—originally developed for Drell–Yan and related processes—to inclusive Higgs boson production in gluon fusion ($gg \to H$), incorporating impact-parameter space evolution and BFKL-like small-$x$ dynamics.

Result: Preliminary numerical results showing improved stability and convergence of perturbative predictions for Higgs production cross sections at high energies, particularly in the small-$x$ regime where fixed-order QCD breaks down.

Conclusion: The HELL-based differential resummation is successfully adaptable to gluon-fusion Higgs production; further validation and matching to fixed-order calculations are required before phenomenological application.

Abstract: In the context of precision physics, the resummation of large logarithms of the variable $x = Q^2/s$ is increasingly relevant at high energies. We report here preliminary results on the extension of the differential resummation formalism within the HELL framework to Higgs boson production via gluon fusion.

</details>


### [124] [Exploring $U_A(1)$ symmetry restoration in a magnetic field by meson screening masses in the NJL model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35468)
*Bing-Kai Sheng,Zigeng Ding,Danning Li,Xinyang Wang,Lang Yu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how the axial $U_A(1)$ symmetry is restored at finite temperature and in external magnetic fields using a lattice-improved (2+1)-flavor NJL model; it identifies axial magnetic/inverse magnetic catalysis in longitudinal observables, monotonic growth of transverse screening mass difference with field, yet universal suppression of axial pseudo-critical temperature with $eB$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the interplay between axial $U_A(1)$ symmetry restoration, chiral crossover, and magnetic fields—particularly resolving apparent contradictions between axial symmetry restoration indicators (e.g., screening masses vs. susceptibilities) and the observed inverse magnetic catalysis of chiral transition.

Method: Employs a (2+1)-flavor NJL model with magnetic-field-dependent four-quark coupling calibrated to lattice QCD chiral pseudo-critical temperature; computes meson screening masses (longitudinal/transverse) and susceptibilities for $\delta^0$ and $\pi^0$ as $U_A(1)$ partners; extracts axial pseudo-critical temperatures from thermal inflection points of these order parameters.

Result: Longitudinal $\delta^0$–$\pi^0$ screening mass and susceptibility differences show axial magnetic catalysis at low $T$ and axial inverse magnetic catalysis near chiral crossover; transverse screening mass difference increases monotonically with $eB$ at all $T$; yet all three observables yield decreasing axial pseudo-critical temperature with increasing $eB$.

Conclusion: Axial $U_A(1)$ symmetry restoration is consistently enhanced by magnetic field (i.e., occurs at lower temperature), despite divergent field dependences among different proxies—highlighting the necessity of distinguishing longitudinal vs. transverse dynamics and cautioning against interpreting individual meson-level observables as universal symmetry restoration signals.

Abstract: We investigate the effective axial $U_A(1)$ symmetry restoration at finite temperature in an external magnetic field using meson screening masses, as well as meson susceptibilities, in a $(2+1)$-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model. To incorporate inverse magnetic catalysis, we employ a magnetic-field-dependent four-quark coupling constrained by the lattice QCD chiral pseudo-critical temperature, with the 't Hooft coupling fixed. In the lattice-improved NJL model, the longitudinal screening mass difference between the $U_A(1)$ partners $δ^0$ and $π^0$ and their normalized meson susceptibility difference exhibit axial magnetic catalysis at low temperatures and axial inverse magnetic catalysis near the chiral crossover, whereas the transverse screening mass difference increases with $eB$ at all temperatures. Despite these different responses, the axial pseudo-critical temperatures extracted from the thermal inflection points of these three alternative order parameters decrease with $eB$, implying that a decreasing pseudo-critical temperature can coexist with an increasing transverse screening mass difference at fixed temperature.

</details>


### [125] [Magnetic thresholds and spin-resolved spectral moments of $φ$ and $K^{*0}$ in a hadronic model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35503)
*Lang Yu,Tao-Tao Sui,Xinyang Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates spin-resolved spectral functions of φ and K*0 mesons in a thermal hadronic medium under a constant magnetic field, using a retarded propagator formalism incorporating Landau quantization, vacuum matching, and thermal scattering; it identifies narrow transverse threshold peaks at eV-scale detunings, analyzes how finite spectral width affects threshold structure (e.g., disappearance of inverse-propagator zeros), and quantifies field-dependent responses—showing b² scaling for smooth-weighted φ spectra at low fields but oscillatory behavior for hard-windowed cases, with pseudoscalar mixing altering response coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how magnetic fields modify spin-aligned hadron production in heavy-ion collisions, particularly the impact of Landau-level–driven thresholds and finite spectral widths on spin-resolved spectral functions of vector mesons (φ and K*0) in thermal hadronic matter.

Method: Constructs rest-frame retarded propagators by combining real-vacuum matching, exact Landau level quantization, connection contacts, and thermal scattering terms; validates against collision kernels and known limits; computes spectral functions, analyzes threshold peak areas via kernel integration; studies spectral spread effects on zero structure of Re[Π⁻¹]; employs weighted identities with bounded Taylor remainders to relate spectral redistribution to observable moments; evaluates responses to endpoint replacements (including patch complement) for smooth and hard windows.

Result: Identifies narrow eV-scale peaks from closed transverse thresholds; confirms leading threshold estimate for peak area via full-kernel integration; shows finite spectral spread suppresses zeros of Re[Π⁻¹] when spread ≳ detuning, while integrated weight remains stable; derives weighted identity linking redistribution to smooth moments; finds negative, b²-scaled response for smooth-weighted φ spectra at lowest fields, whereas hard-window responses oscillate; pseudoscalar mixing significantly modifies smooth-response coefficients.

Conclusion: The spin-resolved spectral structure of φ and K*0 is highly sensitive to magnetic-field–induced transverse thresholds and spectral projection choices; quantitative interpretation of spin alignment measurements requires precise knowledge of microscopic daughter widths and source-matched experimental conditions—not just equilibrium spectral shapes.

Abstract: We study the spin-resolved spectral functions of $φ$ and $K^{*0}$ in a thermal hadronic model with a constant magnetic field. We construct the rest-frame retarded propagators by combining real vacuum matching with exact Landau levels, connection contacts, and thermal scattering terms. The resulting stable-daughter calculation is checked against independent collision kernels and published expressions in common limits. We find that closed transverse thresholds give rise to narrow peaks with eV-scale detunings, whose areas must be resolved in the calculation of small spin spectral moments. We test the leading threshold estimate for the peak area by an independent integration of the full kernel. Using positive compact daughter spectra, we then investigate how a finite spectral spread changes the local threshold structure. The disappearance of zeros of the real part of the inverse propagator is controlled by the spread relative to the detuning, whereas the weight integrated over a finite patch can remain nearly unchanged. We relate this redistribution to smooth accepted moments through a weighted identity with a bounded Taylor remainder, and evaluate the complete-window response to three endpoint replacements, including the patch complement. For the stable-daughter $φ$ spectra with a fixed smooth weight, the response at the two smallest sampled fields is negative and compatible with $b^2$ scaling. Hard-window results instead oscillate in sign, and pseudoscalar mixing substantially changes the smooth response coefficient. These results quantify the sensitivity to thresholds and spectral projection; microscopic daughter widths and source-matched experimental spin alignment require additional input.

</details>


### [126] [Heavy Quark Recombination Across Collision Systems: Meson and Baryon production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35546)
*Vincenzo Minissale,Salvatore Plumari,Maria Lucia Sambataro,Gloria Calà,Vincenzo Greco*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews and extends the coalescence-plus-fragmentation framework for heavy-flavor hadronization in relativistic heavy-ion collisions, emphasizing its success in explaining baryon/meson ratios (e.g., Λc/D⁰), predicting multi-charmed baryons (Ξcc, Ωccc) as probes of charm thermalization, extending predictions to beauty baryons (Λb/B⁰, etc.) with stronger coalescence effects, and outlining ongoing efforts to unify open and hidden heavy-flavor descriptions including quarkonium in small systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain observed heavy-flavor baryon enhancements (e.g., Λc/D⁰) inconsistent with vacuum fragmentation alone, and to leverage multi-heavy-quark states as sensitive probes of QGP properties—particularly charm thermalization and non-equilibrium dynamics—as well as to explore universality of coalescence across charm and beauty sectors and toward quarkonium.

Method: Coalescence-plus-fragmentation modeling: charm/beauty quarks from initial hard scatterings are embedded in a hydrodynamically evolving QGP background; coalescence combines nearby quarks into hadrons based on phase-space proximity and wavefunction overlap, while fragmentation dominates at high pT; predictions computed across collision systems (PbPb, KrKr, ArAr, OO) using realistic charm/beauty spectra and QGP evolution.

Result: 1) Multi-charmed baryons (Ξcc, Ωccc) show strong sensitivity to charm momentum distribution—Ωccc especially constrains thermalization; 2) Beauty baryon/meson ratios (Λb/B⁰, etc.) exhibit larger coalescence contributions than charm analogues, indicating enhanced recombination for heavier quarks; 3) Framework is being extended to describe quarkonium production in small systems via same coalescence principles.

Conclusion: Coalescence is essential for heavy-flavor hadronization across collision systems and quark masses; multi-charmed and beauty baryons serve as precision probes of QGP transport properties; unifying open and hidden heavy flavor within a common coalescence framework is a viable and promising direction.

Abstract: Measurements of heavy-flavor baryons in nucleus--nucleus and proton--proton collisions have provided strong evidence that heavy-quark hadronization cannot be described solely in terms of vacuum fragmentation. In particular, the large enhancement of the $Λ_c/D^0$, $Ξ_c/D^0$ and $Ω_c/D^0$ ratios observed at RHIC and LHC energies has been successfully described within a coalescence plus fragmentation approach, supporting the relevance of recombination mechanisms in both large and small collision systems. Building on these results, we discuss recent developments in the coalescence hadronization framework. Particular emphasis is given to the production of multi-charmed baryons, namely $Ξ_{cc}$, and $Ω_{ccc}$, whose yields are predicted in a wide range of collision systems from PbPb to KrKr, ArAr and OO collisions. The strong sensitivity of these states, especially $Ω_{ccc}$, to the underlying charm-quark distribution makes them valuable probes of charm thermalization and non-equilibrium effects in the QGP. We also present recent extensions of the model to the beauty sector, including predictions for $Λ_b/B^0$, $Ξ_b/B^0$ and $Ω_b/B^0$ production. The results indicate sizeable recombination effects and a stronger baryon enhancement than in the charm sector, highlighting the increasing role of coalescence for heavier quarks. Finally, we briefly comment on ongoing developments toward a unified description of open and hidden heavy flavor through the application of the same framework to quarkonium production in small collision systems.

</details>


### [127] [The LZ event with a two-state dark matter halo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35650)
*Vinicius Oliveira*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reanalyzes the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment's single nuclear-recoil candidate at ~248 keV in the context of pseudo-Dirac dark matter, where both endothermic (χ₁N → χ₂N) and exothermic (χ₂N → χ₁N) scattering are possible if a non-negligible fraction f of the dark matter halo consists of the excited state χ₂; for f ≳ 10⁻⁵–10⁻², exothermic scattering dominates, dramatically lowering required cross sections (by up to 1.6×10⁴) and eliminating the kinematic cutoff—rendering pure χ₁-only interpretations inconsistent with limits when f = 1/2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The LZ collaboration interpreted their single nuclear-recoil event as evidence for inelastic (endothermic) dark matter scattering. However, in pseudo-Dirac models, the excited dark matter state χ₂ can be cosmologically stable and populate the halo; this enables additional exothermic scattering channels not considered in LZ’s original statistical analysis. The motivation is to reassess LZ’s inference framework to include such χ₂ contributions and quantify their impact on interpretation and exclusion limits.

Method: The authors reconstruct LZ’s likelihood-based statistical inference—including detector response, signal efficiency, background modeling, and exposure—to validate it against published LZ confidence intervals. They then extend the signal model to include both endothermic (χ₁N → χ₂N) and exothermic (χ₂N → χ₁N) scattering, parameterized by the mass splitting δ and the halo fraction f of χ₂. They compute profile likelihoods and derive allowed cross-section regions and exclusion bounds for varying f and δ.

Result: For f ≳ 10⁻⁵–10⁻² (depending on δ), exothermic scattering dominates the expected signal. At f = 1/2 and δ = 250–350 keV, the cross section needed to explain the event drops by factors of 80 to 1.6×10⁴ relative to the pure χ₁ case; the kinematic cutoff of the endothermic spectrum vanishes; and a benchmark model fitting the event with χ₁ alone is excluded by a factor of ~30 relative to LZ’s published upper limit.

Conclusion: Standard LZ interpretations assuming purely endothermic scattering are incomplete for pseudo-Dirac dark matter with a non-negligible χ₂ abundance. Including exothermic scattering significantly relaxes cross-section requirements, alters spectral features, and invalidates exclusion limits derived under the χ₁-only assumption—highlighting the necessity of joint χ₁/χ₂ halo modeling in future analyses of inelastic DM signals.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment has reported a single nuclear-recoil candidate at $E_R \simeq 248$keV. LZ interprets it in terms of inelastic dark matter that scatters endothermically from its ground state, $χ_1 N \to χ_2 N$. In pseudo-Dirac models the excited state $χ_2$ can survive to the present day, and exothermic scattering, $χ_2 N \to χ_1 N$, is then also open. The published LZ intervals do not describe this case. We rebuild the LZ statistical inference and validate it against the published intervals. We then apply it to a halo that contains a fraction $f$ of $χ_2$. For $f \gtrsim 10^{-5}$--$10^{-2}$, depending on the mass splitting, the exothermic contribution dominates. For $f=1/2$ the cross section required by the candidate drops by factors of $80$ to $1.6\times10^4$ for $δ= 250$--$350$~keV, and the kinematic cutoff of the endothermic signal disappears. A benchmark that explains the event with $χ_1$ alone lies a factor of $30$ above the upper limit when half of the halo is $χ_2$.

</details>


### [128] [Factorization and virtuality evolution of jet functions in heavy-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35653)
*Carlos Lamas,Carlos A. Salgado,Bin Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a virtuality-resolved framework for jet propagation in heavy-ion collisions, extending the BDMPS-Z formalism by defining an in-medium jet function via factorization; it incorporates virtuality-differential transverse momentum broadening and medium-induced gluon radiation, and identifies the ratio of medium-to-jet formation times as the key control parameter for relevant initial virtualities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the integrated (virtuality-averaged) description of jet quenching in heavy-ion collisions and resolve how different virtuality scales contribute to in-medium jet evolution, bridging vacuum-like and medium-induced dynamics.

Method: Derives a factorized jet cross section defining an in-medium jet function; applies perturbative QCD techniques to compute virtuality-differential transverse momentum broadening and medium-induced soft gluon emission; analyzes dependence on medium formation time τ₀, including limits τ₀ → 0 and finite τ₀.

Result: A virtuality-resolved generalization of the BDMPS-Z formalism is obtained; integration over virtuality recovers standard BDMPS-Z results; finite virtuality reveals competition between vacuum showering and medium interactions; the ratio τ₀ / τ_jet emerges as the decisive scale separating dominant virtuality contributions.

Conclusion: Virtuality resolution provides a more complete, scale-separated picture of jet quenching; the in-medium jet function serves as a fundamental building block for precision jet tomography; the medium/jet formation time ratio governs the effective virtuality window probed in jet observables.

Abstract: We develop a new framework to perform a virtuality-resolved description of jet propagation in heavy-ion collisions using perturbative techniques. The role of virtuality is rigorously identified by first deriving a factorized jet cross section that defines an in-medium jet function. This new definition allows to extend the usual BDMPS-Z formalism to include the virtuality-differential transverse momentum broadening distribution and medium-induced soft gluon spectrum. We study both the cases of a medium which is created with some delay time $τ_0$ after the hard collision as well as the case in which $τ_0\to 0$. Integration over the complete virtuality range recovers the standard BDMPS-Z results, while finite virtuality reveals the interplay between vacuum-like evolution and medium-induced dynamics. We identify the ratio between the medium formation time and the jet formation time as the variable controlling which initial virtualities contribute appreciably to the jet function.

</details>


### [129] [GAIA meets LZ: a high velocity tail-tale sign for dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35737)
*Anirban Das,Subhabrata Majumdar,Manibrata Sen,Amogh Srivastav*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how Gaia-informed galactic dynamics affect the inelastic dark matter interpretation of a high-energy nuclear recoil event (LZ230616) observed by LZ, finding that endothermic DM models require larger cross sections and smaller mass splittings under realistic velocity distributions, while exothermic interpretations remain robust; inclusion of LMC gravitational effects further extends viable endothermic parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-energy nuclear recoils in direct-detection experiments like LZ are sensitive to the high-velocity tail of the Galactic dark matter velocity distribution — poorly modeled by the Standard Halo Model. Recent Gaia data enable more realistic galactic models, and the LZ230616 event (248 keV recoil) provides a concrete test case for inelastic DM scenarios.

Method: The authors construct a Gaia-informed dark matter velocity distribution incorporating updated Milky Way kinematics and mass distribution, then compute expected scattering rates for inelastic (exothermic and endothermic) DM models. They compare results against the Standard Halo Model and perform simulation-based analysis of Large Magellanic Cloud (LMC) gravitational perturbations on the DM velocity tail.

Result: The Gaia-informed model yields a lower escape speed, suppressed high-velocity tail, and higher local DM density. Exothermic DM interpretation is largely insensitive to this change, but endothermic DM requires significantly larger spin-independent cross sections and smaller mass splittings to fit LZ230616. Including LMC-induced high-velocity boosts expands the allowed endothermic parameter space toward lower DM masses and larger mass splittings.

Conclusion: Precision galactic astrophysics — especially from Gaia and LMC modeling — qualitatively alters particle-physics interpretations of high-energy nuclear recoils in direct detection, necessitating beyond-Standard-Halo-Model analyses for robust inelastic DM constraints.

Abstract: The interpretation of high-energy nuclear recoils in direct-detection experiments can depend sensitively on the high-velocity tail of the galactic dark matter (DM) distribution. The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment has recently reported an event, LZ230616, with nuclear recoil energy 248 $\pm$ 23 (stat) $\pm$ 23 (sys) keV, while the Gaia mission has provided an unprecedented view of the kinematics and mass distribution of the Milky Way. We bring these two probes together to investigate the inelastic DM interpretation of LZ230616, using a velocity distribution inferred from Gaia-informed galactic model. Compared to the Standard Halo Model, the resulting distribution has a lower escape speed and a significantly suppressed high-velocity tail, with a higher local density. We find that while the exothermic interpretation is largely insensitive to this change, the endothermic interpretation requires substantially larger scattering cross sections and smaller mass splittings. Furthermore, using simulation-based study of the gravitational effects of the Large Magellanic Cloud, we show that the high-velocity tail can extend the allowed endothermic parameter space towards lower DM masses and larger splittings. Our results demonstrate how combining direct searches for DM detection with precision measurements of galactic dynamics can qualitatively affect the particle-physics interpretation of high-energy nuclear recoils.

</details>


### [130] [Covariant Contrastive Learning for Uncertainty-Aware Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35755)
*Shelley Tong,Philip Harris,Gaia Grosso*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a likelihood-regularized embedding method that jointly learns physics-aware latent representations and models systematic uncertainties as nuisance parameters, enabling robust, interpretable anomaly detection in high-energy physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Systematic uncertainty propagation in neural embeddings for anomaly detection is poorly understood, limiting real-world LHC applications.

Method: Joint training of supervised contrastive embeddings with a parameterized downstream classifier that treats continuous systematic uncertainties as nuisance parameters, regularized via likelihood-based constraints.

Result: Demonstrated on simulated CMS Level-1 trigger data: achieves predictable covariance of latent space with systematic shifts; enables robust anomaly detection using a linear classifier in a 4D latent space.

Conclusion: The framework provides a general, scalable approach to incorporate and control systematic uncertainties in latent spaces—enabling statistically rigorous, model-agnostic anomaly detection for LHC and other HEP experiments.

Abstract: Machine-learning-based anomaly detection (AD) offers a promising, model-agnostic alternative to traditional LHC analyses, allowing to search for many signals at once. Recent advances in representation learning motivate the use of neural embeddings to map high-dimensional physics observables into low-dimensional latent spaces better suited to statistical inference. However, the propagation of systematic uncertainty in embedded spaces remains poorly understood, severely limiting the application of these methods to real LHC analyses. We address this gap with a machine learning strategy that uses a likelihood-based regularization to improve the structure of embedded spaces. Building on previous work using supervised contrastive learning for physics-aware embeddings, our approach trains them jointly with a downstream parameterized classification task that incorporates continuous uncertainties as nuisance parameters. This method returns a latent space that covaries predictably with systematic shifts, and a model of such distortions that can be exploited in the downstream statistical test for anomaly detection. Using simulated CMS Level-1 trigger data, we show that our method successfully models continuous uncertainties in a 4D latent space with a linear parametric downstream classifier, improving both the interpretability and robustness of the statistical anomaly detection task. This framework offers a general way to study and control systematic uncertainties in latent spaces, opening the way to a new scalable statistical analysis workflow for anomaly detection at the LHC, and other high energy physics experiments.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [131] [Locality of KMS states for infinite fermionic lattice systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31858)
*Lennart Becker*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes stability of thermal equilibrium (KMS) states of infinite fermionic lattices under extensive Hamiltonian perturbations, proving that exponential decay of correlations and local indistinguishability imply stretched-exponential decay in the perturbed state; it introduces a C*-algebraic formulation of Quantum Belief Propagation and unifies key locality properties via Lieb–Robinson bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish stability of KMS states—especially in non-unique regimes (e.g., phase coexistence)—against extensive perturbations, bridging locality concepts (correlation decay, local response, indistinguishability) without assuming uniqueness or translation invariance.

Method: Develops Quantum Belief Propagation directly on the $C^*$-algebra of the fermionic lattice; combines it with Lieb–Robinson bounds to derive equivalences among correlation decay, local perturbation response ('local perturbations perturb locally'), and local indistinguishability; applies uniform clustering and support conditions for extensive perturbations.

Result: 1) Stretched-exponential decay of correlations for the perturbed KMS state, given exponential decay and exponential local indistinguishability of the unperturbed state; 2) A differentiable path of local equilibrium expectations under extensive perturbations, with linear bound on change in terms of interaction norm; 3) Closed equivalence cycle among three locality properties under uniform clustering.

Conclusion: Thermal equilibrium states of infinite fermionic systems are stable under extensive perturbations when initial locality conditions hold—even without uniqueness—providing a robust, algebraic framework for quantum statistical mechanics beyond mean-field or gapped assumptions.

Abstract: We study how thermal equilibrium states of infinite fermionic lattices respond to perturbations of the Hamiltonian. Our main result concerns extensive perturbations of a chosen KMS state.
