We show that exponential decay of correlations and exponential local indistinguishability under the extensive perturbation yield stretched-exponential decay of correlations for the perturbed state. Neither the initial nor the perturbed state is required to be unique, allowing stability to be studied in the presence of phase coexistence. Our approach uses Quantum Belief Propagation (QBP), which we establish directly on the underlying $C^*$-algebra. Combining QBP with Lieb--Robinson bounds, we show implications between decay of correlations, the principle that local perturbations perturb locally, and local indistinguishability, closing the cycle for perturbations of the tracial state under the stated decay, support and uniformity assumptions. For extensive perturbations under uniform clustering conditions, we also obtain a differentiable path of local equilibrium expectations and bound their change linearly in the interaction norm of the perturbation.

</details>


### [132] [Local Determinant Defects and Low-Energy Subspace Stability in Semifinite von Neumann Algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32095)
*Seyed Mahmoud Manjegani*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a finite-temperature stability test for low-energy spectral subspaces using a local determinant defect of the alpha-z Rényi kernel between two Gibbs states, bounding their tracial L² distance; it relies on a spectral-flattening principle in semifinite von Neumann algebras, applies to trace-class Gibbs operators without requiring Hamiltonian proximity in operator norm, and yields exact ground-state overlap for qubits, principal-angle remainders for matrices, and uniform error bounds for effective low-energy models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a robust, finite-temperature stability criterion for low-energy spectral subspaces—especially when Hamiltonians are not close in operator norm—enabling rigorous analysis of thermal perturbations and effective low-energy models in quantum statistical mechanics and quantum information.

Method: Develops a spectral-flattening principle for bounded positive injective operators in semifinite von Neumann algebras; uses it to reduce the local determinant defect of the alpha-z Rényi kernel between two Gibbs states to an exact two-projection formula; derives bounds on the tracial L² distance between low-energy projections; proves determinant inequalities, equality conditions, and z-monotonicity across matrix, finite, and semifinite settings.

Result: A finite-temperature stability bound expressed via the local determinant defect; exact ground-state overlap formula for qubits; sum-of-principal-angles remainder for finite-dimensional matrices; uniform error bound for bounded observables in effective low-energy models; extension to unbounded Hamiltonians with trace-class Gibbs operators; validity without operator-norm proximity of Hamiltonians.

Conclusion: The proposed determinant-based defect provides a versatile, norm-agnostic thermal stability criterion for low-energy subspaces, unifying and generalizing prior results across finite, infinite, and semifinite settings, with concrete implications for quantum many-body systems, quantum error correction, and approximate ground-state simulation.

Abstract: We give a finite-temperature test for the stability of low-energy spectral subspaces. The test uses a local determinant defect for the alpha-z Renyi kernel of two Gibbs states and bounds the tracial L2 distance between their low-energy projections. The main tool is a spectral-flattening principle for bounded positive injective operators in a semifinite von Neumann algebra. It reduces the local product defect to an exact two-projection formula. The thermal result applies to lower-bounded affiliated Hamiltonians with trace-class Gibbs operators and does not require the Hamiltonians to be close in operator norm. We give an infinite-level example with unbounded Hamiltonians. For matrices, the result gives a sum of principal-angle remainders. For qubits, the defect and the thermal gaps determine the ground-state overlap exactly. The same estimate gives a uniform error bound for bounded observables in an effective low-energy model. We also prove the determinant inequalities, their equality cases, and z-monotonicity in matrix, finite, and semifinite settings.

</details>


### [133] [The Hamilton--Jacobi and Symplectic Analysis for Extended Hořava Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32218)
*Diana Vanessa Castro-Luna,Alberto Escalante*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs Hamilton-Jacobi and Faddeev-Jackiw analyses of a modified linearized non-projectable λR Hořava–Lifshitz gravity model, including BPS and Cotton-squared terms; it derives full constraint structure, generalized brackets, gauge symmetries, and the quantum path integral measure, highlighting critical differences at λ = 1/3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically analyze the constrained structure and quantization of an extended linearized non-projectable λR Hořava–Lifshitz gravity model—incorporating both the Blas-Pujolàs-Sibiryakov (BPS) term and the Cotton tensor squared term—and to clarify the special role of the λ = 1/3 value in its quantum consistency.

Method: Applied the Hamilton-Jacobi formalism (yielding complete set of Hamiltonians, generalized brackets, characteristic equations, and gauge transformations) and the Faddeev-Jackiw symplectic approach (constructing the symplectic two-form, deriving fundamental brackets, and computing the path integral measure); compared results for generic λ ≠ 1/3 and the special case λ = 1/3.

Result: Obtained full integrable Hamilton-Jacobi structure and symplectic tensor; confirmed equivalence of HJ and FJ brackets; computed explicit path integral measure; identified qualitative differences in constraint algebra and measure behavior between λ ≠ 1/3 and λ = 1/3, indicating a structural change in the quantum theory at this critical value.

Conclusion: The extended λR model remains consistently quantizable via both HJ and FJ methods, but λ = 1/3 marks a singular point where the constraint structure and quantum measure exhibit distinct behavior—suggesting potential implications for renormalizability or unitarity in the full nonlinear theory.

Abstract: The Hamilton-Jacobi [HJ] analysis and the Faddeev-Jackiw [FJ] quantization for the linearized, non-projectable $λR$ model, extended by the lowest-order term of Blas, Pujolàs, and Sibiryakov and the higher-order term involving the square of the Cotton tensor, were carried out. The cases $λ\neq \frac{1}{3}$ and $λ= \frac{1}{3}$ are analyzed. Within the Hamilton-Jacobi framework, we obtain the complete set of Hamiltonians, generalized brackets, fundamental differential, characteristic equations, and gauge transformations of the theory. In the Faddeev-Jackiw framework, a symplectic tensor is constructed from which the fundamental brackets are identified. The HJ and FJ brackets coincide, and from the symplectic tensor, the measure for the quantum path integral is calculated. The differences between the cases $λ\neq \frac{1}{3}$ and $λ= \frac{1}{3}$ at the quantum path integral are discussed.

</details>


### [134] [Absolute and relative invariants for low-dimensional topological insulators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32442)
*Federico Manzoni,Domenico Monaco,Gabriele Peluso*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies projection-valued maps on 1D and 2D tori under AZC symmetries, comparing Murray-von Neumann, unitary, and homotopy equivalences; it identifies absolute invariants (Chern number ℤ, Fu-Kane-Mele ℤ₂) for the first two, and relative homotopy invariants in minimal Hilbert spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivated by topological insulators, the paper aims to systematically classify gapped quantum systems via projection-valued maps on d-dimensional tori (d ≤ 2) respecting Altland-Zirnbauer-Cartan (AZC) symmetries.

Method: Constructs symmetric bases for projection ranges under pseudo-periodicity constraints; analyzes phase accumulation upon looping basis vectors around toroidal directions; distinguishes equivalence relations using operator-algebraic (Murray-von Neumann), symmetry-preserving (unitary), and topological (homotopy) criteria.

Result: For d = 1: all AZC classes admit fully periodic symmetric bases → no topological obstruction. For d = 2: topological obstructions yield absolute invariants — ℤ (Chern number) or ℤ₂ (Fu-Kane-Mele). Homotopy equivalence yields relative invariants only in minimal ambient Hilbert spaces; otherwise reduces to unitary equivalence.

Conclusion: Provides a unified geometric framework linking equivalence relations to specific absolute (ℤ, ℤ₂) and relative topological invariants, clarifying the role of Hilbert space dimension and symmetry in classifying low-dimensional gapped quantum systems.

Abstract: [An extended abstract is provided in-text]
  Motivated by topological insulators, this paper analyzes classification schemes for projection-valued maps on $d$-dimensional tori, $d \le 2$, subject to Altland-Zirnbauer-Cartan (AZC) symmetries (time-reversal, particle-hole, and chiral). The main objective is to compare three notions of equivalence for these maps: Murray-von Neumann, unitary, and homotopy equivalence. The analysis relies on constructing symmetric bases for the projection ranges under a pseudo-periodicity constraint, where select basis vectors pick up a phase when looped around the torus. If $d=1$, fully periodic symmetric bases exist across all AZC classes. If $d=2$, topological obstructions appear as non-trivial phases characterized by $\mathbb{Z}$ (Chern number) or $\mathbb{Z}_2$ (Fu-Kane-Mele) invariants. These serve as absolute invariants that classify both Murray-von Neumann and unitary equivalences. For homotopy equivalence, the behavior depends on the ambient Hilbert space: if this has non-minimal dimension, homotopy equivalence reduces directly to unitary equivalence, while in minimal ambient spaces additional relative homotopy invariants emerge. Their precise values change under unitary conjugation, leaving only relative topological phases between maps well-defined. Ultimately, this provides a unified geometric framework for gapped quantum systems in low dimensions, demonstrating how distinct equivalence relations generate specific absolute and relative topological invariants.

</details>


### [135] [Poisson eigenvalue statistics and dynamical delocalization for fractional and long-range Anderson models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33084)
*Peter D. Hislop,Rodrigo Matos,Constanza Rojas-Molina*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that long-range Anderson models (including the fractional Anderson model) in the strong disorder regime exhibit Poisson local eigenvalue statistics and dynamical delocalization — contrary to the usual Anderson model — provided β > d/2 (or equivalently, for fractional Laplacian exponent α ∈ (1/2, 1) in d = 1).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the spectral and dynamical localization behavior of long-range random operators, particularly testing the Disertori–et-al conjecture on absence of dynamical localization in fractional Anderson models, and contrasting them with short-range Anderson models.

Method: Analysis of fractional moments of Green's functions; derivation of sharp lower bounds on these moments; use of Poisson statistics criteria via level repulsion and localization length estimates; spectral analysis of long-range hopping operators with decay |T(n,m)| ∼ ‖n−m‖^{−(d+2β)}; application to fractional Laplacian (−Δ)^α in d = 1.

Result: In strong disorder and for β > d/2: (i) local eigenvalue statistics are Poissonian with intensity n(E); (ii) dynamical localization fails — i.e., large moments of position operator diverge, implying dynamical delocalization; (iii) specifically, 1D fractional Anderson model with α ∈ (1/2, 1) shows Poisson statistics and dynamical delocalization.

Conclusion: Long-range randomness fundamentally alters localization phenomenology: strong disorder no longer implies dynamical localization, and eigenvalue statistics remain uncorrelated (Poisson), unlike the localized GUE/GOE-type statistics in short-range models. This establishes a rigorous distinction between short- and long-range disordered quantum systems.

Abstract: We study local eigenvalue statistics and dynamical (de)-localization properties for long-range Anderson models, including the fractional Anderson model. These systems are random Anderson-type perturbations of operators with long-range (non random) hopping terms of the form $|T(n,m)| \sim \|n-m\|^{-(d+2β)}$ for $β>0$, on the $d$-dimensional lattice. In the presence of a strong enough random potential, these models exhibit dense pure point spectrum with polynomially decaying eigenvectors, almost surely. We show that in this strong disorder regime, and for $β>d/2$, the local eigenvalue statistics centered at any $E$ in the deterministic spectrum is a Poisson point process with intensity given by the density of states function $n(E)$. Moreover, we prove that, at strong disorder, there is no dynamical localization for these models, verifying a conjecture of Disertori et al. We achieve this by establishing explicit, sharp lower bounds on the Green's functions fractional moments which imply that large moments of the position operator are infinite. Hence, these models exhibit Poisson eigenvalue statistics and dynamical delocalization, that is, the absence of dynamical localization. In particular, this shows that in dimension $d=1$, the fractional Anderson model, a random perturbation of the fractional Laplacian $(-Δ)^α$ with $α\in (0,1)$, exhibits Poisson eigenvalue statistics and dynamical delocalization if the disorder is strong and the exponent $\frac{1}{2}<α<1$. This is in stark contrast with what is known for the usual Anderson model that has only nearest-neighbor hopping.

</details>


### [136] [Minimal output entropy for the channels that add or remove a box of a Young diagram, and a Pauli principle for every permutation symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33498)
*Robin Reuvers*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes quantum channels connecting irreducible representations of U(d) whose Young diagrams differ by one box, proving coherent states maximize output majorization (hence minimize output entropy), and computes optimal outputs via hook lengths; it also characterizes all entropy minimizers and applies results to derive sharp spectral bounds for single-particle reduced density matrices under permutation symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand extremal behavior—particularly output majorization and entropy minimization—of covariant quantum channels linking adjacent U(d) irreps, and to obtain exact spectral constraints on reduced states of symmetric multi-particle systems.

Method: Representation-theoretic analysis of U(d) covariant channels using Young diagrams and hook-length formulas; application of majorization theory and convex geometry of spectra; explicit computation of channel outputs on coherent states and classification of all minimizers via symmetry and extremal point analysis.

Result: Coherent states majorize all other outputs for four canonical U(d)-covariant channels differing by one box; output entropy is minimized uniquely (up to equivalence) by coherent states for two channels, but non-coherent pure states can also minimize for the other two; explicit closed-form expressions for optimal outputs in terms of hook lengths; sharp spectral bounds for single-particle RDMs of N-particle symmetric/antisymmetric states, including full characterization of achievable spectra (recovering Pauli principle and Coleman's theorem for fermions).

Conclusion: Coherent states are universally optimal for majorization in these representation-linked quantum channels, but entropy minimization admits richer structure depending on channel type; the framework yields exact, representation-theoretically grounded constraints on quantum marginal spectra.

Abstract: Two irreducible representations of $U(d)$ whose Young diagrams differ by a single box are connected by four covariant quantum channels: the box can be removed or added, and one keeps either the new diagram or the box ($\mathbb{C}^d$ or its dual). We prove that for each of these channels the output of a coherent state majorizes every other output, so that coherent states minimize the output entropy, and we compute the optimal output explicitly in terms of hook lengths. For the two channels that keep the box we also determine all minimizers; these need not be coherent, even when they are pure. As an application we consider $N$ particles with a given permutation symmetry and find sharp bounds on the spectrum of the reduced density matrix of a single particle, as well as the exact set of spectra that mixed states can reach (for fermions these are the Pauli principle and Coleman's theorem).

</details>


### [137] [Effective atom-dimer bands of three-body lattice systems at strong coupling: exact critical mass ratios and a statistics-quasimomentum duality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33576)
*Mikhail V. Dolgopolov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes three-particle contact interactions on the square lattice across the full Brillouin zone, deriving exact effective Hamiltonians via Feshbach-Schur reduction, solving the atom-dimer threshold problem via a 4×4 resistance-based Hermitian matrix, and establishing rigorous results on bound-state existence, critical mass ratios, onset laws, dualities, and RG interpretation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend previous work—limited to zero quasimomentum and trimers—to the full Brillouin zone and general statistics (bosonic, fermionic, mixed), resolving the atom-dimer threshold structure and binding physics for infinite-strength contact interactions on the square lattice.

Method: Feshbach-Schur reduction onto the eigenvalue-1 subspace of the pair-contact operator; derivation of explicit effective Hamiltonians with provable first-order error; reduction of threshold condition to a 4×4 Hermitian matrix built from square-lattice two-point resistances; analytical solution via quartic polynomial; verification via lattice quadrature, computer algebra, exact diagonalization, and infinite-lattice solvers.

Result: Exact closed-form atom-dimer zones and critical mass ratio pi/(pi−2) at zero quasimomentum; at most two atom-dimer bound states per (quasimomentum, mass ratio); onset laws featuring essential singularity at zone boundary and linear-over-logarithm behavior in pure p-wave; two exact statistics–quasimomentum dualities; global characterization of second fermionic threshold; RG interpretation identifying inverse lattice Green function as marginal s-wave coupling and zone boundary as marginal (BCS/Kondo) type.

Conclusion: The atom-dimer spectral problem for three hard-core particles on the square lattice is exactly solvable across the entire Brillouin zone; the threshold structure is governed by lattice resistance geometry and admits a marginal RG flow, unifying aspects of BCS, Kondo, and lattice quantum few-body physics.

Abstract: We study three particles on the square lattice with contact interactions of infinite strength, for all total quasimomenta, for three statistics: three identical bosons, two identical fermions plus a third particle, and two identical bosons plus a third particle. Previous work treated only the trimer and zero quasimomentum. Here the atom-dimer sector is analyzed on the whole Brillouin zone. A Feshbach-Schur reduction onto the infinitely degenerate eigenvalue 1 of the pair-contact operator gives explicit effective Hamiltonians with proved first-order error. The threshold problem reduces to a 4x4 Hermitian matrix criterion built from the two-point resistances of the square lattice, and hence to a quartic polynomial. This yields the complete atom-dimer zone and the critical mass ratio on the Brillouin zone, with bound between 1 and pi/(pi-2), and closed forms at all symmetry points. We prove: the zones in closed form; the critical mass ratio with exact value pi/(pi-2) at zero quasimomentum, reproducing a numerical value; at most two atom-dimer states for every quasimomentum and mass ratio; the onset laws, an essential singularity on the zone boundary and a linear-over-logarithm onset in the pure p-wave; two exact dualities showing that a change of statistics is equivalent to a shift of quasimomentum by pi; and the global range of the second fermionic threshold. The onset laws admit a renormalization-group reading: the inverse lattice Green function is a marginal s-wave coupling, binding energies arise by dimensional transmutation with lattice scale 16, and the zone boundary is of marginal (BCS/Kondo) type rather than of Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless type. All constants are verified independently by lattice quadrature, computer algebra, exact diagonalisation, and infinite-lattice solvers.

</details>


### [138] [$p$-adic spectral zeta functions via the inverse Stieltjes transform](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33660)
*Su Hu,Min-Soo Kim*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the $p$-adic spectral zeta function framework from discrete to continuous spectra using the inverse Stieltjes transform, constructs a $p$-adic spectral distribution from the resolvent of a bounded operator, defines bosonic/fermionic $p$-adic zeta functions and functional determinants, establishes their analytic properties and special value formulas, and demonstrates embedding of the discrete case and boson–fermion symmetry; applied to the position operator in $p$-adic quantum mechanics on compact subsets of $\mathbb{C}_p$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the previously introduced $p$-adic spectral zeta function—originally defined for discrete spectra—to continuous spectra, enabling regularization of functional determinants for operators with continuous spectrum in $p$-adic quantum physics.

Method: Uses the inverse Stieltjes transform applied to the resolvent of a bounded operator to construct a generalized $p$-adic spectral distribution; defines bosonic and fermionic $p$-adic zeta functions and functional determinants via this distribution; analyzes analyticity, derives special value formulas and Stirling-type asymptotic expansions; verifies consistency with prior discrete-spectrum framework.

Result: A rigorous extension of $p$-adic spectral zeta theory to continuous spectra; existence and analytic properties (e.g., meromorphy, domain of convergence) of bosonic/fermionic $p$-adic zeta functions; explicit special value formulas and Stirling expansions; demonstration of boson–fermion symmetry; embedding of the discrete-spectrum case as a limit; application to the position operator in $p$-adic QM on compact subsets of $\mathbb{C}_p$ yields well-defined $p$-adic spectral distributions and determinants.

Conclusion: The $p$-adic spectral zeta formalism is successfully generalized to continuous spectra via the inverse Stieltjes transform and spectral distributions, unifying discrete and continuous cases and revealing intrinsic boson–fermion symmetry; this provides a foundational tool for determinant regularization in $p$-adic quantum theories involving unbounded or continuous-spectrum operators.

Abstract: Spectral zeta functions provide a standard tool for regularizing determinants of differential operators in quantum physics. In a previous paper (J. Math. Phys. 66: 083505, 2025), we introduced a $p$-adic spectral zeta function for discrete spectra via a locally analytic interpolation function. In this paper we extend the framework to continuous spectra using the inverse Stieltjes transform. Starting from the resolvent of a bounded operator, we construct a generalized distribution, called the $p$-adic spectral distribution, and define bosonic and fermionic zeta functions and functional determinants. We establish their analytic properties, special value formulas, and Stirling expansions, and show that our previous framework is embedded into the present one in the discrete case. In this approach, the bosonic and fermionic cases exhibit an interesting symmetry. As an application, we consider the position operator in $p$-adic quantum mechanics on an arbitrary compact subset of $\mathbb{C}_{p}$.

</details>


### [139] [Homogenized graphs with spectrally embedded defect states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33907)
*Kirill D. Cherednichenko,Stephen P. Shipman*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs bound states in the continuum (BICs) in effective media derived from homogenized high-contrast periodic metric graphs, by coupling two lattice graphs and exploiting spectral overlap between hybrid state subspaces enabled by critical contrast.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize bound states at energies embedded in the continuous spectrum—i.e., bound states in the continuum (BICs)—in a deterministic, analytically tractable homogenized medium, overcoming the usual requirement of symmetry or randomness.

Method: Homogenization of high-contrast periodic metric graphs via Fourier-based operator interpolation; simultaneous embedding and edge-coupling of two identical lattice graphs to generate an effective medium with internal degrees of freedom; spectral decomposition into orthogonal hybrid state spaces; rigorous operator-norm estimates (O(ε)) for reduced resolvents localized on defects.

Result: Existence of robust, ε-scale BICs in the effective medium, arising from spectral band–gap overlap between two orthogonal hybrid subspaces, sustained by critical high contrast preserving microscopic resonances.

Conclusion: High-contrast homogenization of coupled metric graphs provides a systematic, analysis-driven route to designing continuum media supporting bound states in the continuum, with stability guaranteed by O(ε) resolvent estimates.

Abstract: By homogenizing high-contrast periodic metric graphs, we create continuous media that admit bound states produced by local defects at energies embedded in the continuous spectrum. This is achieved by fitting two copies of a lattice graph simultaneously into space and coupling them by connecting edges. The homogenization of an ODE on a graph to a PDE in space is achieved by representing the associated operators in a common functional space through interpolation by Fourier analysis. The two coupled graphs result in an effective medium with internal degrees of freedom that allow a decomposition into two orthogonal spaces of hybrid states. Critical high contrast retains microscopic resonances in the effective medium and allows a spectral band of one hybrid state space to overlap a spectral gap of the other. This serves as the mechanism for creating bound states at frequencies embedded in the continuous spectrum of the effective medium. These bound states in the continuum persist for $\varepsilon$-scale metric graphs due to $O(\varepsilon)$ operator-norm estimates for reduced resolvents on the defect region.

</details>


### [140] [The equivalence of topological indices for $\mathrm{U}(1)\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_2$-symmetric quantum spin chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34119)
*Hal Tasaki*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the equivalence between Ogata's operator-algebraic index and Tasaki's elementary twist index for integer-spin chains with U(1)⋊ℤ₂ symmetry and a symmetric, locally-unique gapped ground state—without assuming translation invariance or MPS structure. It provides a computable formula for the Ogata index via local twist expectations, with an explicit error bound based on spectral gap and charge fluctuations, and applies it to confirm the Ogata index is (−1)^S for the antiferromagnetic Heisenberg chain.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the relationship between two distinct topological indices—Ogata’s general operator-algebraic index (applicable without continuous symmetry) and Tasaki’s twist index (requiring U(1) symmetry)—in their overlapping domain of validity, and to enable practical computation of the former using local observables.

Method: Uses charge-fluctuation estimates to construct half-chain rotations in the strong limit with well-defined phases; leverages an exact group commutator identity to relate the Ogata index to the limit of local twist expectations; derives an explicit error bound depending on the spectral gap and ground-state expectation values.

Result: Equivalence of Ogata and Tasaki indices is rigorously established under U(1)⋊ℤ₂ symmetry and local uniqueness of the gapped ground state; a computable formula for the Ogata index is obtained with quantifiable accuracy; as a corollary, the Ogata index of the antiferromagnetic Heisenberg chain is shown to be (−1)^S under stated gap and uniqueness assumptions.

Conclusion: Ogata’s abstract index admits a physically transparent, numerically accessible interpretation via local twist operators when U(1) symmetry is present, unifying algebraic and geometric viewpoints on SPT classification in quantum spin chains.

Abstract: Symmetry-protected topological (SPT) phases in quantum spin chains are distinguished by topological indices. Ogata's operator-algebraic indices apply to a broad range of symmetry classes without requiring continuous symmetry, whereas the elementary twist index of Tasaki requires $\mathrm{U}(1)$ symmetry. We prove their equivalence in the common setting of integer-spin chains with on-site $\mathrm{U}(1)\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetry and a symmetric locally-unique gapped ground state, without assuming translation invariance or a matrix product representation. A charge-fluctuation estimate yields a strong-limit construction of half-chain rotations with their phases fixed. An exact group commutator identity then identifies the Ogata index with the limit of local twist expectations and gives an explicit error bound. This provides a computable formula when a lower bound on the gap and the relevant ground-state expectations are available. As an application, a previous twist-index calculation determines the Ogata index to be $(-1)^S$ for the antiferromagnetic Heisenberg chain under explicit uniqueness and boundary-field gap assumptions.
  The author formulated the problem and proposed the basic strategy, while ChatGPT made substantial contributions to the development of the proof.

</details>


### [141] [Discontinuity of Continuum Fermion Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34402)
*Oliver Siebert*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that Heisenberg dynamics for interacting continuum fermions in ℝ^d is not pointwise-norm continuous on the gauge-invariant CAR algebra, implying non-invariance of both the full and gauge-invariant CAR algebras for almost all times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the continuity and algebraic invariance properties of Heisenberg dynamics for interacting quantum many-body systems described by continuum fermions, particularly in relation to the CAR algebra structure.

Method: Rigorous operator-algebraic analysis: construction and estimation of commutators, application of automatic-continuity theorems, and spectral assumptions on dispersion and external potentials; considers a broad class of nonconstant pair potentials.

Result: Pointwise-norm discontinuity of the Heisenberg dynamics on the gauge-invariant CAR algebra; consequent failure of algebraic invariance — neither the CAR algebra nor its gauge-invariant subalgebra is preserved under the dynamics for almost all times.

Conclusion: The standard algebraic framework (CAR algebra) is incompatible with the Heisenberg evolution for generic interacting continuum fermion systems, revealing a fundamental limitation of the algebraic approach in this setting.

Abstract: We study the Heisenberg dynamics of continuum fermions in $\IR^d$ interacting through a pair potential. For a large class of nonconstant pair potentials and under suitable assumptions on the dispersion relation and external potential, we prove that the dynamics fails to be pointwise-norm continuous even on the gauge-invariant CAR algebra. An automatic-continuity argument then shows that neither the CAR algebra nor its gauge-invariant subalgebra is invariant under the dynamics, with non-invariance occurring for almost all times.

</details>


### [142] [Classical BV Pushforward](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34533)
*Xin Geng,Andrey Losev,Vyacheslav Lysov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper defines the classical BV pushforward and proves it preserves solutions of the classical master equation (CME) and BV canonical equivalence under isolation conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formalize and analyze the classical BV pushforward as a tool for reducing field-theoretic data while preserving key algebraic structures—specifically, solutions of the classical master equation and their BV equivalence classes.

Method: Rigorous definition of the classical BV pushforward on graded manifolds; proof of its compatibility with the classical master equation via homological perturbation and local analysis near isolated critical points; verification of preservation of BV canonical equivalence using canonical transformations.

Result: Two main theorems: (1) BV pushforward sends CME solutions on the total space to CME solutions on the base space; (2) under isolation of critical points, it preserves BV canonical equivalence. Examples and applications are provided to contextualize the construction.

Conclusion: The classical BV pushforward is a well-defined, structure-preserving operation in classical BV formalism, enabling consistent dimensional reduction in gauge theories and paving the way for quantization-compatible constructions.

Abstract: We define the classical BV pushforward and establish its two main properties. First, we prove that it maps solutions of the classical master equation (CME) on the total space to solutions of the CME on the smaller space. Second, we prove that, for isolated critical points, it maps BV canonically equivalent solutions to BV canonically equivalent solutions. We illustrate the construction with examples and discuss its applications.

</details>


### [143] [Scattering Measures and Information Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34721)
*Tatsuro Oda*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an information-geometric framework for scattering experiments using parameterized finite measures, decomposes Fisher information into mass and shape components, models measurement processes as information-contracting kernels, and unifies classical scattering and quantum measurements with incomplete detection under Fisher geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified, information-theoretic foundation for scattering experiments—bridging classical scattering theory, optimal experimental design, and quantum measurement with incomplete detection—by leveraging the geometry of finite measures and Fisher information.

Method: Formulates scattering experiments as parameterized finite measures on outcome spaces; derives the associated Fisher metric; decomposes the measure into total mass and normalized shape to separate Fisher information contributions; models measurement processes via probability/sub-probability kernels; analyzes information contraction properties; links to optimal experimental design and quantum measurement theory.

Result: A geometric decomposition of Fisher information into scattering strength (mass) and outcome redistribution (shape); proof that measurement kernels contract Fisher information; a unified framework embedding classical scattering, optimal design, and quantum measurements with partial detection within finite-measure Fisher geometry.

Conclusion: Scattering experiments admit a natural finite-measure formulation whose Fisher geometry provides a rigorous, general language for quantifying information content, experimental sensitivity, and measurement limitations—extending seamlessly from classical to quantum regimes with incomplete detection.

Abstract: We formulate scattering experiments in terms of parameterized finite measures on outcome spaces and develop the associated information geometry defined by the Fisher metric. The decomposition of a scattering measure into total mass and normalized shape separates Fisher information into contributions from changes in overall scattering strength and redistribution among outcomes. Measurement processes are represented by probability or sub-probability kernels acting on the scattering measure. Such kernels contract Fisher information, placing a broad class of experimental operations within a common information-theoretic framework. This formulation connects scattering experiments to the theory of optimal experimental design through the Fisher geometry of scattering measures. The same finite-measure structure arises naturally in quantum measurements with incomplete detection, clarifying its place in the hierarchy from quantum measurement to classical outcome statistics.

</details>


### [144] [Notes on Magnetostatic Interactions in $\mathbb R^3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34766)
*Vladimir Gol'dshtein,Wolfgang H. Müller,Reuven Segev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives a compact magnetostatic force functional from a potential energy formulation based on magnetic dipoles represented by a current density potential, yielding Lorentz, Kelvin, asymmetric active stress (with mechanical couple density), and pressure terms—without invoking constitutive or aether relations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate magnetostatics in a compact, fundamental way without relying on constitutive relations (including aether relations), by directly linking magnetic dipole distributions—parameterized via a current density potential—to vector potential fields and virtual work principles.

Method: Derives potential energy of a magnetic dipole distribution (specified by a current density potential field) interacting with an external vector potential; applies the principle of virtual work—equating virtual mechanical work to minus the time derivative of potential energy during virtual motion—to obtain a mechanical force functional.

Result: A generalized force functional comprising: (i) standard Lorentz and Kelvin force densities; (ii) an asymmetric active stress field, whose skew-symmetric part yields the mechanical couple density; and (iii) a pressure term.

Conclusion: Magnetostatic forces and couples can be fully characterized through a variational framework rooted in dipole–vector potential interaction and virtual work, eliminating dependence on constitutive assumptions and revealing intrinsic asymmetric stress and pressure contributions.

Abstract: A compact formulation of magnetostatics is presented. Without using constitutive relations, including the aether relations, an expression is proposed for the potential energy of a region carrying a distribution of magnetic dipoles under a vector potential field, where the dipole distribution is specified by a current density potential field. Assuming that during a virtual motion of the dipole distribution the virtual work expended by the field is equal to minus the time derivative of the potential energy, an expression for the mechanical force functional is derived. In addition to the Lorentz and Kelvin force densities, the force functional contains an asymmetric active stress field, the skew-symmetric part of which is the mechanical couple density, and a pressure term.

</details>


### [145] [A renormalized Lee Model: resolvent and spectral analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34980)
*Claudio Cacciapuoti,Federica Muscolino,Diego Noja,Andrea Posilicano*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs and analyzes a renormalized 3D massive Lee model using an intrinsic operator-theoretic framework, establishes sector-wise spectral properties—including HVZ-type essential spectrum inclusions and sub-threshold point spectrum characterization via a Birman–Schwinger operator—and proves conditions for bound state existence and absence in multi-excitation sectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously define and analyze the spectral structure of the three-dimensional massive Lee model after renormalization, addressing longstanding challenges in operator-theoretic quantum field models—particularly domain specification, resolvent representation, and sector-wise spectral decomposition under excitation number conservation.

Method: Intrinsic renormalization via abstract operator construction with explicit domain and action; Kreın-like resolvent formula; sector-wise spectral analysis exploiting total excitation number conservation; operator-pencil (Weyl-function) formalism; HVZ-type theorem derivation; Birman–Schwinger principle for sub-threshold eigenvalues; variational and perturbative arguments for bound state existence/absence.

Result: Explicit renormalized Hamiltonian with well-defined domain and resolvent; sector-wise HVZ formula proved fully in sectors 0 and 1, partially in higher sectors; essential spectrum components generated by lower sectors identified; sub-threshold point spectrum characterized by bounded self-adjoint nonnegative Birman–Schwinger operator in all higher sectors; absence of sub-threshold spectrum established unconditionally when renormalized gap ≥ boson mass, and under weak-coupling condition otherwise; sufficient condition derived for simple bound state in two-excitation sector.

Conclusion: The renormalized 3D massive Lee model is rigorously formulated as a self-adjoint operator, and its spectral structure—including essential spectrum, sub-threshold eigenvalues, and bound states—is systematically characterized sector-wise, confirming and extending HVZ-type behavior while providing sharp coupling- and mass-dependent criteria for spectral phenomena.

Abstract: We construct and analyze a renormalized three-dimensional massive Lee model; its renormalized Hamiltonian is defined intrinsically through an abstract construction which explicitly provides the operator domain, action and a Kre\uın-like resolvent formula. Conservation of the total excitation number reduces the spectral analysis of the full Hamiltonian to a sector-wise study of the spectral profile of an explicit operator pencil, playing the role of an operator-valued Weyl function. We construct essential spectrum components in a sector generated by lower excitation sectors and obtain the inclusion in the expected sector-wise HVZ type formula; the full formula is proved in the first two sectors and, under explicit conditions, in every sector. Below the free or one-excitation threshold, the point spectrum in every higher sector is described by a bounded self-adjoint nonnegative Birman--Schwinger operator. In every sector with at least two excitations we prove absence of sub-threshold spectrum for arbitrary coupling when the renormalized gap is not smaller than the boson mass, and under an explicit weak-coupling condition when it is smaller. In the latter case we also derive a sufficient condition for the existence of a simple bound state in the two-excitation sector.

</details>


### [146] [GR-FM: Geometrically Regularized Flow Matching for SDF-Based Medical Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35006)
*Yuxin Ai,Zhichang Guo,Fanghui Song,Dazhi Zhang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: GR-FM is a geometrically regularized flow matching framework for medical image segmentation that uses signed distance fields (SDFs) and incorporates biharmonic regularization and Eikonal constraints to improve boundary accuracy, structural consistency, and spatial smoothness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medical image segmentation suffers from weak boundaries, fine-grained/irregular structures, and limited high-quality annotations; existing flow matching methods lack explicit geometric constraints on spatial regularity and distance-field fidelity, causing oscillations, boundary displacement, and discontinuities.

Method: GR-FM formulates segmentation as continuous probability transport from an initial distribution to a target SDF-based implicit representation using image-conditioned flow matching and ODE-based deterministic transport; it introduces biharmonic regularization and Eikonal constraint into the training objective and analyzes zero-level-set evolution under geometric constraints.

Result: GR-FM achieves competitive region overlap (e.g., Dice) and boundary accuracy (e.g., HD95, ASSD) on MoNuSeg, GlaS, and DRIVE; reduces performance variance across runs and maintains stability with few integration steps.

Conclusion: Explicit geometric regularization—via SDF representation, biharmonic smoothing, and Eikonal enforcement—in flow matching significantly improves boundary localization and structural preservation in medical image segmentation, offering a principled, stable, and efficient alternative to binary-mask-based generative approaches.

Abstract: Medical image segmentation remains challenging in terms of accurate boundary localization and complex-structure preservation, as target regions often exhibit weak boundaries, fine-grained structures, and irregular shapes, while high-quality images and precise annotations are usually limited. Existing generative segmentation approaches based on Flow Matching mainly learn velocity fields in the state space but lack explicit constraints on the spatial regularity and distance-field properties of the recovered representation. This limitation may lead to spatial oscillations, boundary displacement, and discontinuities in fine structures. To address these issues, we propose an image-conditioned Geometrically Regularized Flow Matching framework, termed GR-FM, which formulates segmentation as a continuous probability transport process from an initial distribution to a target implicit representation distribution. Instead of directly modeling binary masks, GR-FM adopts the SDF to describe the target structure, where each pixel is represented by its signed distance to the object boundary, resulting in a continuous geometric field. Flow Matching and ordinary differential equations are then employed to achieve deterministic and efficient distribution transport. The training objective further incorporates a biharmonic regularization term and an Eikonal constraint to enhance the spatial smoothness, structural consistency, and distance-field characteristics of the recovered representation. Moreover, we analyze the continuous transport process under geometric constraints and investigate the evolution of the zero level set. Experiments conducted on the MoNuSeg, GlaS, and DRIVE datasets demonstrate that GR-FM achieves competitive performance in both region overlap and boundary accuracy, reduces performance variation, and maintains stable segmentation results with only a small number of integration steps.

</details>
